Workflow doc 09 · deep dive

The Fleet Script Block

A line-by-line map of allotment_v2.html 13762–36196 — 22,435 lines, 420 top-level functions, and the 5,423-line flLoad() that opens it. This is Fleet Management almost in its entirety.

Range 13762–36196 Lines 22,435 Symbols 531 Commit 094dde1 Branch refactor/booking-v2-migration
Line numbers drift Every :12345 reference means “roughly here” — grep the symbol name instead. The function index at the bottom is the fastest way to find anything.

§ TL;DR

Lines 13762–36196 are Fleet Management, not a mixed bag. This is the single largest <script> in allotment_v2.html — 22,435 lines, about 27% of the whole application — and 290 of the app's 321 fl* functions live inside it, alongside the memo/MO subsystem, cost analytics, the consumable-requisition module (§82) and the document-number duplicate audit. There is no Booking v2, no Agents and no Rate Types code anywhere in it.

The block opens with the fleet store initialiser and closes by calling it. Almost everything between those two points is UI: list pages, detail pages, modals, dashboards and the maintenance-job lifecycle.

A–F, in source order

If you read nothing else, read these six

  1. flLoad() is one 5,423-line function :13815–:19237. All boot-time fleet data handling is inside it: version migration, per-store loads, the auto-snapshot, the defensive field-level merge, and roughly 85 one-shot _app_hooks data patches — at least 9 of which are chained on a prior hook's flag, so hook order is load-bearing.
  2. Hoisting is load-bearing. flLoad reads FL_DEFAULT_* seed arrays that are const-declared at :19911–:20391 — after its own body. It works only because a function declaration hoists and the call sits at :36192, after the whole block has evaluated. Converting flLoad to a const arrow, or moving the call earlier, breaks boot with a TDZ error.
  3. Adding a row to a FL_DEFAULT_* seed usually does nothing. The idempotent back-fill at :18531 covers inventory only. Engines, gearboxes, propellers, maintenance, incidents and memos have no back-fill — appending to those defaults is a silent no-op on any install that has already seeded.
  4. Several write paths bypass flSave(). flProjCleanupV2/V3/V4, flProjMigrate, and flSaveAssignment / flCancelAssignment / flAutoUpdateAssignments :35420–:35530 write the state blob straight to localStorage and hand-mirror only a few named keys. A new sibling key is dropped on those paths — contrary to the read-modify-write rule in CLAUDE.md.
  5. One test prevents money double-counting. The whole §costV2 / §memoToBoat contract :29287–:29302 turns on !item.invId: a memo line carrying an invId is stock, deliberately excluded so it can be counted once later as job parts cost. A memo can legitimately appear split between a boat total and the central bucket — which is why the central table prints a partial amount with a "full memo ฿X" sub-line. Change that test and boat costs double.
  6. Two functions mutate data on render, with no user action. flProjResyncMJLinks and flProjAutoCreateForScheduledMJs run on every Projects render and can create projects or rewrite parentProjectId — including on a plain tab click.
The doc-08 anatomy row, corrected Doc 08 calls this range “Agents, Rate Types, most of Booking v2 (bkV2*), the start of Fleet (fl*), ending in flLoad()”. Only the last clause survives contact with the code: flLoad() genuinely is the block's last meaningful statement (the call, at :36192 — the definition opens the block). But there are zero agent, rt* or bkV2* declarations in range, and “the start of Fleet” describes a block that contains 90% of Fleet. Full verdict table in §02.
Bugs found while mapping — flagged, not fixed
  • Boat-status sync v3 :18442 runs unflagged on every load and force-reopens then deletes boat-log rows — real data loss for manual boat-log edits, and not among CLAUDE.md's list of deliberate self-heals.
  • flSaveApprove writes m.approveNote; the renderer reads m.approvedNote — approval notes never display.
  • flRetireBoat :32716 writes status 'retired', outside the documented available|fixing|unavailable enum, with no confirm() and no cleanup of engines, jobs or assignments.
  • flPropStValues.indexOf() returns -1 for the 'damaged' and 'limited' statuses other code paths write — silently wiping status on the next propeller save.
  • The central-cost card ignores the time filter: _cenLeft is built before the inScope check :29390, so it is all-time while every neighbouring card respects the period.
  • Two conflicting engine-service formulas inside flRenderInsights — the card uses flEngServiceState(e).left (correct, can go negative), the insight uses raw flEngHours % interval and can never report overdue. A third, hard-coded 500h modulo lives in the dashboard.
  • Memo status enum mismatch: insights check 'pending_approval', the cost model and projects use 'pending' — one of the two matches nothing.
  • flSaveCreateJob's split path never writes boatStatus/ boatStatusReason (only the legacy setFixing), while the single path writes all three.

01 What this block is

allotment_v2.html lines 13762–36196 — 22,435 lines, the largest single <script> block in the file and roughly 27% of the whole application. It holds 420 top-level function declarations.

Despite what the file-anatomy table in doc 08 says, this block is not a mixture of modules. It is, almost entirely, Fleet Management — 290 of the app's 321 fl* functions live here — together with the memo/MO subsystem, cost analytics, the consumable-requisition module, and the document-numbering duplicate audit.

Fleet 290 fns

Boats, engines, gearboxes, propellers, maintenance jobs, incidents, projects, daily log, dashboard.

Memo / MO 37 fns

Purchase memos, live totals, inventory linkage via memo.maintId.

Cost v2 21 fns

Cost categories derived from the assets a job touches; the §memoToBoat anti-double-count contract.

Dup audit + §82 rest

Document-number collision repair, and the consumable requisition module.

The one thing to know flLoad() spans :13815–:19237 — a single 5,423-line function, 24% of this block on its own. It is the fleet store initialiser: version migration, per-store loading, an auto-snapshot, a defensive field-level merge, and roughly 85 one-shot _app_hooks data patches. Nothing else in the file comes close to this size.

02 Anatomy & a correction to doc 08

The file-anatomy table in doc 08 describes this range as “the largest single business-logic block: Agents, Rate Types, most of Booking v2 (bkV2*), the start of Fleet (fl*), ending in flLoad() (fleet store init)”. Checked against the code, three of the four claims are wrong.

ClaimVerdictWhat the code shows
“Agents, Rate Types”wrong Zero agent or rt* functions in range. SB_AGENTS is at :39569, SB_RATE_TYPES at :39959, the rt* functions at :60954–:63067 — all outside.
“most of Booking v2 (bkV2*)”wrong Zero of the 492 bkV2* declarations are in range. Booking v2 runs :41531–:78065.
“the start of Fleet”understated Not the start — 290 of 321 fl* functions are here. This block is Fleet.
“ending in flLoad()”correct True of the call, not the definition. flLoad() is defined at :13815 and invoked at :36192, four lines before </script>.

The block's last four lines :36191

// ── init Fleet ──
flLoad();                                                                   // :36192
// Layer 4: ตรวจ data integrity หลัง load · แสดง warning ใน console ถ้ามี
if(typeof flValidateDataIntegrity === 'function') flValidateDataIntegrity(); // :36194
flDrWeekStart=addDays(TODAY,-TODAY.getDay()+1);                             // :36195
</script>                                                                   // :36196

The last function definition in the block is flSavePropeller at :36149; the last statement is the flDrWeekStart assignment.

Hoisting is load-bearing flLoad() is defined at :13815 but the FL_DEFAULT_* seed arrays it reads are const declarations at :19911–:20391, after its body. This only works because the function declaration hoists and the call at :36192 happens after the whole block has evaluated. Moving the call earlier — or converting flLoad to a const arrow — breaks boot with a TDZ error.

A Lines 13762–19361 · Fleet store bootstrap — FLEET_VERSION, snapshot helpers, and the 5,400-line flLoad()

DOC CORRECTIONThe existing description of this block — "the largest single business-logic block: Agents, Rate Types, most of Booking v2 (bkV2*), the start of Fleet (fl*), ending in flLoad()" — does not match these lines. There is no Agent code, no Rate-Type code and no bkV2* code anywhere in 13762–19361. The slice opens the <script> at :13762 with a base64 logo constant, declares FLEET_VERSION, defines two console-only snapshot helpers, and then flLoad() begins at :13815 and does not close until :19237 — 5,423 lines, ~97% of the slice. flLoad is at the start, not the end. The final 124 lines are the §dupAudit duplicate-detection config tables. Agents / Rate Types / Booking v2 live far later in the file (SB_AGENTS :39569, SB_RATE_TYPES :39959, SB_BOOKINGS :41531).

What this region is · reading order :13762

One continuous fleet-data bootstrap. Everything here runs when flLoad() is called at boot; nothing here renders UI.

READ ORDER TRAPflLoad() reads FL_DEFAULT_ENGINES, FL_DEFAULT_GEARBOXES, FL_DEFAULT_PROPELLERS, FL_DEFAULT_MAINTENANCE, FL_DEFAULT_INCIDENTS, FL_DEFAULT_INVENTORY, FL_DEFAULT_MEMOS — but none of those constants are declared in this slice. They are const declarations at :19911–:20391, i.e. after flLoad's body. This works only because flLoad is a hoisted function declaration invoked at runtime, long after the script has finished evaluating. Never call flLoad() from top-level code placed before :20391 — the const temporal dead zone will throw ReferenceError.

Version constants · FLEET_VERSION / DATA_VERSION :13768

Only one version constant lives in this slice.

ConstantLineValueWhat bumping it does
FLEET_VERSION:13768'fleet_v34'Compared against d.fleet_version in the store. A value not in the whitelist array at :13845 takes the first-time/seed path; fleet_v2…fleet_v7 take one migration branch; fleet_v8…fleet_v33 take another. Bumping to fleet_v35 means you must also add 'fleet_v34' to the whitelist and add a v34→v35 branch, or every existing install falls through to the first-time path.
DATA_VERSION:5782 (outside slice)'2026o'Not referenced anywhere in this slice — it gates the non-fleet blob, not the fleet stores.
LS_KEY:5781 (outside slice)'loveandaman_v2'Every read/write in flLoad goes through localStorage.getItem/setItem(LS_KEY) — which is the in-RAM shim, not real disk storage (see CLAUDE.md §2).
LOVE_LOGO_B64:13764~62 KB base64 PNGCompany logo data URI on one physical line. Skip past it; do not print it in a terminal.
WHITELIST DRIFTThe version whitelist at :13845 and the v8–v33 branch list at :13882 are two separate hand-maintained string arrays containing the same 26–33 literals. Adding a version to one and not the other silently changes which branch an install takes. Note that 'fleet_v34' (the current FLEET_VERSION) is in the first whitelist but not in the v8–v33 branch — that is intentional: a v34 install runs no versioned migration at all, only the post-load hooks.

Console-only snapshot helpers :13773

window.flListSnapshots() :13773

Lists every localStorage key starting loveandaman_v2_snap_, sorted oldest→newest, printing index, timestamp and a count summary {MJ, MO, INC, size}. Returns a count string. Read-only.

window.flRestoreSnapshot(idx) :13797

Overwrites LS_KEY with snapshot idx after a confirm(). Requires a manual page refresh to take effect. Destructive if confirmed.

The header comment documents them as console commands (:13769): "flListSnapshots() — ดู snapshot ทั้งหมด / flRestoreSnapshot(N) — restore snapshot ที่ N" (view all snapshots / restore snapshot N).

NOTEThese two are the runtime safety system CLAUDE.md §4 says to preserve. They are not wired to any UI — they exist only for a developer in the browser console.

flLoad · step 1: parse + auto-snapshot :13815

function flLoad(){
  const d=JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(LS_KEY)||'{}');

Everything downstream mutates this one local object d and re-serialises it. The auto-snapshot block :13817–:13844 runs before any migration, and only when d.fleet_maintenance, d.fleet_memos or d.fleet_incidents is non-empty.

  1. Prune first — trims existing snapshots down to 3 before writing a new one, so the write has room.
  2. Size gate — if JSON.stringify(d).length > 1.5e6 (1.5 MB) it deletes all snapshots and writes none.
  3. Quota catch — a QuotaExceededError on setItem drops every snapshot key and logs [Auto-snapshot] quota hit.
  4. The whole block is wrapped in try/catch; failure only warns.
HISTORY MARKERThe inline comment at :13828 records the bug: "A full-state snapshot (~6 MB) can never fit Safari's localStorage quota (~5 MB) — it threw QuotaExceededError on EVERY fleet load there. SQL is the store now; only snapshot small states." The header comment still says "Keeps last 10 snapshots" (:13820) but the code keeps 3 — a stale comment.

flLoad · step 2: first-time / unknown-version path :13845

Taken when !d.fleet_version or the version is outside the fleet_v2…fleet_v34 whitelist. Logs a [flLoad] First-time path · existing data: stats line, then fills only empty arrays from the seeds.

Store keyLoad conditionSeed source (declared outside slice)
fleet_engines!d.x || d.x.length === 0FL_DEFAULT_ENGINES :19911
fleet_gearboxes!d.x || d.x.length === 0FL_DEFAULT_GEARBOXES :19981
fleet_propellers!d.x || d.x.length === 0FL_DEFAULT_PROPELLERS :20063
fleet_daily!d.x → {}(object, no seed)
fleet_maintenance!d.x || d.x.length === 0FL_DEFAULT_MAINTENANCE :20126
fleet_incidents!d.x || d.x.length === 0FL_DEFAULT_INCIDENTS :20200
fleet_inventory!d.x || d.x.length === 0FL_DEFAULT_INVENTORY :20272
fleet_memos!d.x || d.x.length === 0FL_DEFAULT_MEMOS :20391

The Thai comment at :13846 states the intent: "DEFENSIVE: ถ้า user มี data อยู่แล้ว … → preserve · เฉพาะ field ที่ 'ว่างเปล่า' ถึงจะใช้ default seed" — if the user already has data (from export, manual edit, etc.), preserve it; only genuinely empty fields get the default seed. It then stamps d.fleet_version = FLEET_VERSION and persists.

THE IDEMPOTENT-MERGE TRAPBecause every seed is assigned by reference only when the array is empty (d.fleet_engines = FL_DEFAULT_ENGINES), items you append to any FL_DEFAULT_* list never reach an already-seeded install. The codebase has one and only one fix for this, and it covers inventory alone: the back-fill loop at :18531–:18542. Engines, gearboxes, propellers, maintenance, incidents and memos have no back-fill — appending a row to those defaults is a silent no-op for existing users. If you add such a row you must write your own idempotent push-missing-ids merge next to the inventory one.
ALIASINGThe first-time assignment shares the seed array object with the global constant (no deep clone). Later hooks then push into d.fleet_gearboxes / d.fleet_inventory, mutating FL_DEFAULT_* itself for the lifetime of the page. Compare the inventory back-fill at :18539, which correctly does JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(seed)).

flLoad · step 3a: fleet_v2–fleet_v7 migration :13875

Per-version cascade of small data repairs, then an unconditional d.fleet_gearboxes = FL_DEFAULT_GEARBOXES ("refresh with new model names + suffix fields") and a version stamp.

  • fleet_v2 / fleet_v3: hard-replace inventory, memos, maintenance and incidents with the defaults (destructive).
  • fleet_v4 / fleet_v5: rename inventory part 90752-ZY3-003 from the typo สั้มล็อค to ลิ่มล็อค (locking wedge); seed memos if empty.
  • fleet_v6: seed memos if empty.

flLoad · step 3b: fleet_v8–fleet_v33 → v34 migration :13882

The largest versioned branch (~550 lines). It rewrites gearbox identity, then seeds a batch of historical documents.

Gearbox shaft/suffix normalisation :13883–:13936

Four lookup maps decide a gearbox's shaftLength and modelSuffix from brand|position:

MapApplies toNote
suffixMapHonda / Suzuki, monohullPort→X(25"), C.Port/C.Std/Center→XX(30")
catamaranMapBoats in catamaranBoats = {'b1','b10'}All positions 30" — Aluminous1 / Aluminous2
df325MapSuzuki DF325A enginesNo Z/T rotation marker; suffix is just X / XX
df325CatamaranMapDF325A on a catamaranEverything XX (30")

DF325A detection is fuzzy — either the linked engine is brand==='Suzuki' && model==='DF325A', or the gearbox's own model string merely contains 'DF325A'. It also renames DF250 →DF250W on both gearboxes and engines.

Gearbox serial re-encoding :13968–:13999

Every gearbox serial is overwritten, not patched:

installed: <BOAT_ABBREV>-<BASE>-<SUFFIX>-<POSCODE>   e.g. VER-250D-XXD-C
spare:     SPARE-<BASE>-<SUFFIX>-<NN>                  e.g. SPARE-250D-XXD-01

boatAbbrev hardcodes all 15 boat ids (b1:'AL1' … b15:'ROL'); posCode maps Port→P, C.Port→CP, C.Std→CS, Std→S, Center→C. Spare numbering uses a local counter per SPARE-base-suffix key.

DESTRUCTIVEThis loop assigns g.serial unconditionally for every gearbox in the store, including ones a user typed by hand. Any gearbox whose boatId is not in boatAbbrev falls back to boatId.toUpperCase(), and an unknown note position yields -X. Adding a 16th boat without extending boatAbbrev silently produces serials like B16-250D-XXD-X.

ASSET-REF INCONSISTENCYSeeded incident.damagedAssets[] entries are inconsistent: some use the v31 field names ({type:'gearbox', gbId:'g21'} at :14313) and some still use the legacy generic id ({type:'gearbox', id:'g2'} at :14545, {type:'engine', id:'e26'} at :17172). The v31 patch at :14347 normalises fleet_maintenance[].assets only — never fleet_incidents[].damagedAssets. Code that walks damaged assets must read both shapes.

flLoad · step 4: the post-load hook engine :14436

From here to :18523 the function is a flat sequence of ~85 "hooks". The header comment states the contract:

// ── Post-load hooks: idempotent updates that run every load regardless of version ──
// (Use d._app_hooks flags to ensure each hook runs only once)
if(!d._app_hooks) d._app_hooks = {};

Every hook follows the same shape and each one writes the whole blob back:

Guard
if(!d._app_hooks.<flag>){ … } — later hooks also use the defensive form if(d._app_hooks && !d._app_hooks.<flag>)
Optional dependency
Some hooks additionally require a prior flag: && d._app_hooks.mo019_user_flow_approve_order_receive. This creates an ordering chain — reorder the hooks and the chained ones stop firing.
Mutate
Push/patch records in d.fleet_* / d.boats. Inner idempotency is usually a second Set of existing ids.
Flag + persist
d._app_hooks.<flag> = true; localStorage.setItem(LS_KEY, JSON.stringify(d));
PERFORMANCE / WRITE AMPLIFICATIONOn a fresh store this single function performs ~85 full JSON.stringify + setItem round-trips of the entire multi-megabyte state object. On an already-flagged store all guards are false and only the two unflagged blocks (:16461 spareLocation scan, :18442 boat-status sync v3) plus the self-heals run.
THESE ARE SIMULATED USER ACTIONS, NOT MIGRATIONSThe majority of hooks carry comments like "จำลอง user กด …" / "Simulate user clicking Start button" / "mirror flSaveReceive". They hand-replicate UI functions (flMaintStart, flMaintClose, flMaintAddPart, flSaveApprove, flSaveReceive, flAdvanceMemo, flConfirmSwap) against raw JSON. If you change any of those real functions, the mirrored copies here drift and will produce records the UI can no longer produce. Several hooks exist purely to repair a previous hook's mistake (see the *_cleanup_*, *_revert_* and *_fix_* flags).

Boat status sync v3 — the unflagged every-load reconciler :18442

For each inprogress MJ with setFixing !== false:

Collect
All boat-log rows where s==='fixing' and note contains the MJ number.
None found → create
Push {id:'sl_sync_'+mj.id, s:'fixing', from:startDate, to:null}.
Found → force reopen
Keep allFixingForMj[0]; if to !== null set it back to null (including a same-day to===from window); pull from back to startDate if later.
Dedupe
Every other fixing row for that MJ is removed by id.
Fix overlaps
available rows starting before the fixing window get to = dayBefore(startDate); available rows entirely inside the window are marked _remove and filtered out.
Persist + sync RAM
Writes the blob and copies each boat's log onto the live BOATS array — "CRITICAL: also update in-memory BOATS so UI sees the fix".
DATA-LOSS RISKThis block is unflagged and force-reopens and deletes boat-log rows on every single load. A user who manually closes a fixing window through the UI while the MJ is still inprogress will see that edit reverted on the next reload, and any second fixing row they added for the same MJ is silently deleted. It also removes any available row that starts inside a fixing window. If you touch boat-status editing, check this block first.

ASSIGNMENT-BY-REFERENCEFL_* point at the same array objects as d.fleet_* (no clone). That is why later code can mutate FL_INVENTORY and then simply write d.fleet_inventory = FL_INVENTORY. But FL_MAINT is the exception — flDedupeMaint() may return a new filtered array, so FL_MAINT and d.fleet_maintenance can diverge from that line onward. Several deferred blocks below therefore mutate FL_MAINT and call flSave() rather than setItem.

Inventory seed back-fill (the one idempotent merge) :18531

FL_DEFAULT_INVENTORY.forEach(function(seed){
  if(seed && seed.id && !_invHave[seed.id]){
    FL_INVENTORY.push(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(seed))); _invAdded++;
  }
});

The comment states the rule verbatim: "FL_DEFAULT_INVENTORY only seeds when the array is empty, so items appended to the default list later (e.g. the HO-IV26004083 batch) never reach users whose localStorage was seeded earlier. Idempotent: adds only ids that are absent, never touches existing rows." Wrapped in try/catch; logs [flLoad] back-filled N new seed inventory item(s).

PATTERN TO COPYThis is the template CLAUDE.md refers to. It deep-clones, checks by id only, and never mutates an existing row. If you need the same behaviour for engines/gearboxes/propellers/memos, replicate exactly this shape here rather than adding a mutating hook.

The two self-heals :18543 / :18566

Boat stuck-fixing heal :18543

Scans BOATS for the latest open (to == null) status row. If it is fixing/unavailable, its note matches /MJ-\d+/, that MJ is now done, and no other inprogress MJ on that boat still forces fixing → close the row at mj.endDate and push an available row noted Auto-restore · MJ-nnn done. Root cause noted inline: "flMaintClose's boat-restore was skipped on a job". Idempotent.

Engine status heal :18566

An engine that is an asset of any inprogress MJ must read fixing regardless of the job's boatStatus/setFixing — the boat may stay Available on a replacement engine, but the pulled engine is still under repair. Conversely fixing with no active job returns to ready. Skips broken and spare.

Then :18585: FL_INVENTORY.forEach(invSyncLegacy) (defined :20718) forces the legacy qty/location fields to match stocks[] on every item.

BAR TO MATCHThese, plus the charter-boat mirror and van-group heals elsewhere, are the deliberate self-heals CLAUDE.md §4 says to preserve. Both are narrowly targeted (single field, explicit precondition, no cascade) and both are safe to re-run. Do not add an auto-mutation here that cannot meet that bar.

flLoad · step 6: deferred UI-flow hooks :18589

These run after the FL_* globals exist, because they call the real UI functions. Each is guarded by a typeof fn === 'function' check, wrapped in try/catch, ends with delete d._app_hooks._<flag> + flSave(), and logs errors rather than throwing.

Deferred helper functions (declared inside flLoad)

_deferredAddLog(jobNo, date, text, by) :18758

Adds a maintenance progress log by going through the real UI function: it injects a hidden <div> containing #maint-log-text-{id}, #maint-log-by-{id} and #maint-log-date-{id}, calls flMaintAddLog(mj.id), then removes the node in a finally. Dedupes on text+by+date.

Trap: the injected HTML is built with a template literal and no escaping — a text/by containing a quote would break the markup. Also, if flMaintAddLog's input ids ever change, this silently does nothing.

_deferredCreateLaborMemo(jobNo, opts) :18804

Builds a labour memo mirroring flSaveMemo: one item, qty 1, unit 'งาน', no VAT, no discount, status:'pending_approval', currentStep:1. Id is LA_UID('m') + '_' + jobNo. Pushes the memo into FL_MEMOS and a 📋 สร้าง Memo line into mj.progressLog. Dedupes on maintId + title.

§dupNo · §noFree :18828The memo-number generator here carries two history markers. "§dupNo · เดิมใช้ FL_MEMOS.length + 1 · พอลิสต์มีรู เลขใหม่จะไปชนใบที่มีอยู่แล้ว" — the old code numbered from list length, so once the list had gaps the new number collided with an existing memo. "§noFree · ยืนยันว่าเลขที่จะออกยังว่างจริง ไม่ใช่แค่มากกว่าเลขสูงสุด" — confirm the number is genuinely free, not merely greater than the maximum. The fix is a do…while that increments past max(no) until no memo holds that number:
let _moMax = FL_MEMOS.reduce(function(mx,x){
  var v = parseInt(String(x.no||'').replace(/\D/g,''),10); return (v>mx)?v:mx; }, 0);
let no;
do { _moMax++; no = 'MO-' + String(_moMax).padStart(3,'0'); }
while(FL_MEMOS.some(function(x){ return x.no===no; }));

Use this pattern for any new document number. The Krisada hook at :16672 still uses the weaker max+1 without the free-check.

§mjDedupe — one-shot duplicate maintenance-job cleanup :19190

Guarded by mj_dedupe_v1. The comment explains the root cause: "hook รุ่นเก่าตั้ง id ด้วย Date.now() ทุกครั้งที่ seed ใหม่ จึงเกิด record ที่ id ไม่ซ้ำ แต่เนื้อหาเหมือนกันทุกช่อง · โผล่ซ้ำในทุกหน้าที่นับเงิน" — old hooks used Date.now() ids, so every re-seed produced records with unique ids but identical content, double-counting money on every page. Ids are static now; this cleans up the residue.

Group key (deliberately strict)
[no, boatId, title.trim(), type, startDate] joined with ␟. Comment: "เข้มไว้ก่อน · ต้องเหมือนกันครบทุกช่องถึงจะถือว่าเป็นใบเดียวกัน" — be strict; every field must match.
Score the survivor
parts.length*10 + progressLog.length*5 + linkedMemoCount*20 + (endDate?3:0) + (status!=='pending'?2:0) + (closeNote?1:0) — the record with the most substance wins.
Re-point every reference
memo.maintId, inventory[].history[].jobId and incident.maintId are all remapped from the dropped id to the kept id — "เงินต้องไม่หายไปกับใบที่ลบ" (money must not disappear with the deleted job).
Filter + report
Removes the losers, console.warns a report, and stores it on d._app_hooks.mj_dedupe_v1_report.
REFERENCE COMPLETENESSThe remap covers memos, inventory history and incidents. It does not remap boat.repairHistory[].jobNo (which keys on no, not id, so it happens to survive) nor any other collection that might hold an MJ id. If you add a new store that references maintId/jobId, add it to this remap.

§dupAudit — duplicate-detection configuration :19238

flLoad closes at :19237. The rest of the slice is declarative config for the system-wide duplicate audit. The governing principle is stated up front: "คีย์ที่ใช้ตัดสินว่า 'เป็นรายการเดียวกัน' ตั้งเข้มไว้ทุกก้อน · ต้องเหมือนกันครบทุกช่องที่ระบุ ถึงจะถือว่าซ้ำ · ยอมพลาดดีกว่าลบของจริง" — keys are strict everywhere; better to miss a duplicate than delete real data.

LA_DUP_SETS — top-level record duplicates :19241

11 entries. Each has {k, label, money, clean, sure, get(), key(x), name(x), cols(x)}. clean:true means the UI offers a delete button; sure:true means a match is definitely a bug.

kSourceDuplicate keyclean / sure
maintFL_MAINTno, boatId, title, type, startDate, endDate (null if no no)clean sure
memoFL_MEMOSno, maintId, boatId, title, round(amount), createdDateclean sure
incidentFL_INCIDENTSno, boatId, title, dateclean sure
consumFL_CONSUMABLE_LOGSdate, boatId, itemId, qty, amount, note, byview only
invFL_INVENTORYname, partNo, location, sorted stocks[].location — "ของชิ้นเดียวกันคนละคลัง = คนละ record โดยตั้งใจ" (same part in different warehouses is intentionally a separate record)view only
engineFL_ENGINESserial, boatId, possure

Every get() is written as typeof X !== 'undefined' ? X : [], so the audit works even if a store has not been initialised.

LA_DUP_NESTED — line-item duplicates :19308

4 entries, all needId:true, all keyed only on the row's own id: invHist (inventory receive/withdraw history), mjParts (parts on a maintenance job), moItems (memo lines), pierPay (booking.pierPayments).

§dupNoId :19312Repeated four times, once per set. The reasoning: "เบิก → ยกเลิก → เบิกใหม่ ในวันเดียวกัน ได้บรรทัดที่เหมือนกันทุกฟิลด์โดยธรรมชาติ · เป็นคนละเหตุการณ์จริง ไม่ใช่การเขียนซ้ำ · เทียบด้วยเนื้อหาไม่ได้ จึงเทียบด้วย id เท่านั้น · บรรทัดเก่าที่ยังไม่มี id = ข้าม" — withdraw → cancel → withdraw again on the same day naturally yields byte-identical rows that are genuinely separate events, so content comparison is impossible; compare by id only, and skip legacy rows that have no id rather than judging on the user's behalf. Same for memo lines (ordering the same part on two lines is normal) and pier cash receipts (two equal cash takings really happen).
CONSEQUENCEBecause the key returns null for id-less rows, the nested audit is blind to every pre-id record. Any new writer of inventory.history, mj.parts, memo.items or booking.pierPayments must stamp a unique id or its rows will never be auditable.

LA_DUP_NUMS — colliding document numbers :19339

3 entries: noMemo (FL_MEMOS), noMaint (FL_MAINT), noInc (FL_INCIDENTS).

§dupNo :19336"เลขที่เอกสารซ้ำ แต่เนื้อหาคนละใบ · ไม่ใช่ข้อมูลซ้ำ · เป็นการออกเลขชนกัน ต้องออกเลขใหม่ ไม่ใช่ลบทิ้ง" — a repeated document number on genuinely different documents is a numbering collision, not duplicate data: renumber, never delete. "ต้นเหตุที่พบ: ตัวสร้าง memo บางตัวออกเลขจากจำนวนใบ (length+1) แทนเลขสูงสุด" identifies the culprit as memo generators that numbered from list length.
§noOne :19344Each entry's no(x) returns null for a cancelled (and, for memos, rejected) record — "ใบที่ยกเลิกแล้วไม่ได้แย่งเลขกับใคร", a cancelled document is not competing for its number. Note this cancellation list is separate from the booking-aggregate exclusion list ['cancelled','cancelled_weather','rejected'] used elsewhere.

The cols() renderers surface x.prevNo as (เดิม …), i.e. a renumbered document keeps a breadcrumb to its old number.

§renumber · laRenumberSet(k) :19361

Begins on the last line of this slice and continues past it. Header: "§renumber · ออกเลขใหม่ให้เอกสารที่เลขชนกัน · ไม่ลบอะไรทั้งสิ้น · คืนค่า {n, changed:[{id,from,to,title}]}" — reissue numbers for colliding documents, delete nothing, and return a change report for display. Only the first three lines (config lookup and var arr=U.get()||[];) fall inside 13762–19361; the body belongs to the next fragment.

Cross-cutting traps for anyone editing this region

  • flLoad is 5,423 lines of one function. Do not read it whole; grep -n "_app_hooks\." to find the hook you want and read a 40-line window.
  • Hook order is load-bearing. At least 9 hooks are guarded on a prior flag (mj006_gb_swap_g2_g57, mj006_prop_cascade_…, mj006_close_success_v1, mj006_close_boat_log_cleanup_v1, mo019_user_flow_…, mj006_withdraw_i60_v1, memo_krisada_4073_create_v1, memo_krisada_user_flow_v1, mj021_mj027_via_ui_v2). Never reorder or splice hooks out of the middle.
  • Never use Date.now() for a seeded record id. Three separate comments in this slice record the same incident ("static — Date.now() id made every re-seed a duplicate"), and §mjDedupe exists solely to clean up the damage.
  • Never number a document from list.length + 1. Use the §dupNo/§noFree loop at :18828.
  • Adding a version bump means editing two string arrays (:13845 and :13882) plus writing a branch, per CLAUDE.md §2.
  • Adding a row to any FL_DEFAULT_* other than inventory is a no-op for existing users — copy the back-fill at :18531.
  • The two unflagged blocks run every single load (:16461 spareLocation scan and :18442 boat-status sync v3). Anything you add without a flag joins that set.
  • Every hook writes the full blob. A new hook in the middle of the chain costs another full serialise on a fresh store.
  • FL_MAINT may not be the same array as d.fleet_maintenance after :18527. Mutating one after that line does not necessarily update the other — :19052 hand-copies 7 fields for exactly this reason.
  • Boat status is written from five places in this function alone (sync_boat_fixing_v1, tadeo_drydock_unavailable_v1, the per-close hooks, boat-status-sync v3, and the stuck-fixing self-heal). Trace all five before changing boat-log semantics.
  • Thai comments are the design record here. They document intent that the English code does not (e.g. why gearbox spares detach, why a drydock must not mark engines fixing, why a voucher may span bookings). Do not delete them when refactoring.

The duplicate-audit model — what collides and why :19238

The three descriptor tables that drive everything in the first region are declared immediately before this slice (:19240–:19361); the functions that consume them start at :19362. You cannot understand the functions without the tables, so they are summarised here.

The system distinguishes three different failure modes that all look like "duplicates" to a user but need three different fixes:

1 · Real duplicate records (LA_DUP_SETS :19241)

The same document written twice. Fix = delete the shadow copy, after re-pointing every reference at the survivor.

2 · Duplicate nested rows (LA_DUP_NESTED :19307)

Line items inside one document written twice (inventory history, MJ parts, memo items, pier payments). Fix = drop the extra line.

3 · Colliding document numbers (LA_DUP_NUMS :19338)

Two genuinely different documents that were issued the same no. Nothing may be deleted. Fix = re-issue one of the numbers.

Stated root cause :19340

The comment names it: some memo generators issued numbers from length+1 instead of max+1, so deleting/cancelling a memo made the counter hand out a number that already existed.

Each LA_DUP_SETS entry is {k, label, money, clean, sure, get(), key(x), name(x), cols(x)}. The flags matter:

FlagMeaningConsequence in the UI
surea match here is certainly a bugdrawer titled "ซ้ำแน่นอน" (definitely duplicated) vs "น่าสงสัย" (suspicious)
cleansafe to auto-deleteonly clean:true sets get a Clean button; laDupClean returns 0 otherwise :19553
moneyduplicates here distort financialscounted separately into out.nMoney

Stores scanned, and the identity key each uses:

kStoreKey ("same record" test)clean
maintFL_MAINTno + boatId + title + type + startDate + endDateyes
memoFL_MEMOSno + maintId + boatId + title + amount + createdDateyes
incidentFL_INCIDENTSno + boatId + title + dateyes
consumFL_CONSUMABLE_LOGSdate+boat+item+qty+amount+note+by (no doc number exists)no
invFL_INVENTORYname+partNo+location+sorted stock locationsno
engine/gearbox/propFL_ENGINES / FL_GEARBOXES / FL_PROPELLERSserial (+boatId, +pos for engines); no serial → skippedno
boatBOATSname + regno
agentSB_AGENTSname + codeno
bookingSB_BOOKINGSid onlyno
TRAP The inv key deliberately includes the warehouse. The comment says it outright: "ของชิ้นเดียวกันคนละคลัง = คนละ record โดยตั้งใจ" — the same part in two warehouses is two records by design, so warehouse must be part of the key or the audit would propose merging real stock.
WHY ID-ONLY booking compares by id alone (:19298) because one voucher legitimately maps to many bookings (b2c_<order>_<line>). Only an id collision is a real problem. Every LA_DUP_NESTED entry is also id-only — see below.

§dupNoId — why nested lists compare by id and nothing else :19309

All four nested lists (invHist, mjParts, moItems, pierPay) set needId:true and use key(h) = h.id ? 'id␟'+h.id : null. The reasoning, repeated per entry in Thai comments:

  • Inventory history :19312 — "withdraw → cancel → withdraw again on the same day" produces two rows identical in every field. They are two real events, not a double write.
  • MJ parts / memo items / pier payments — ordering the same part on two lines, or taking two identical cash payments, is normal business.
  • A null key means skip, not "duplicate". Legacy rows written before ids existed are never judged: "บรรทัดเก่าที่ยังไม่มี id = ข้าม ไม่ตัดสินแทนผู้ใช้" — old id-less rows are skipped rather than decided on the user's behalf.
DO NOT "Improve" these keys to compare content. Doing so would let laDupClean silently delete genuine second withdrawals, second cash payments, and second order lines. The id-only comparison is the safety property, not an oversight.

§noOne — cancelled documents do not compete for a number :19344

Each LA_DUP_NUMS entry defines no(x) which returns null for cancelled/rejected documents:

no:function(x){ return (x.status==='cancelled'||x.status==='rejected')?null:(x.no||''); }

A null is dropped from the grouping map in every consumer (laRenumberSet, laDupScan, costDupInspect). So a cancelled MO-014 and a live MO-014 are not a collision — the cancelled one is not using the number any more. This is why the collision count can drop after a cancel with no renumbering at all, and it is why costNoCancelOne is offered as an alternative fix to renumbering.

§renumber · re-issuing colliding document numbers :19362

laRenumberSet(k) :19362 — bulk fix for one LA_DUP_NUMS bucket (noMemo / noMaint / noInc). Prefix is chosen by key: MO- / MJ- / INC-, zero-padded to 3 digits.

1 · Seed the counter
maxN = highest integer found in any x.no after stripping non-digits (replace(/\D/g,'')), across the whole array — cancelled ones included, so a cancelled high number is never reused.
2 · Group by number
Using U.no(x), so cancelled docs are excluded. Groups of size 1 are skipped.
3 · §keep — the elder keeps the number
:19375 Sort by createdDate || startDate || date, tie-broken by id. g[0] keeps its no; everyone after is renumbered. Deterministic — re-running produces the same winner.
4 · §noFree — find a genuinely free number
:19380 do { maxN++; newNo=pre+pad(maxN); } while(arr.some(x=>x!==m && x.no===newNo)). The comment explains why max+1 is not enough: number formats in the data are inconsistent, so the digit-extraction max can under-count. The loop guarantees the emitted number is unused.
5 · §trace — keep the old number findable
m.prevNo = m.prevNo || oldNo then m.no = newNo. prevNo is written once and never overwritten, so the original number survives repeated renumbers. cols() renders it as "(เดิม MO-014)".
6 · §refs — rewrite text that quoted the number (memos only)
:19389 For k==='noMemo': every FL_INVENTORY[].history[] row whose h.memoId===m.id has the old string swapped for the new one in its note. Linked by id, so this is always safe.
7 · progressLog — only when unambiguous
:19395 The maintenance job's progressLog[].text is rewritten only if no other member of the collision group shares the same maintId. If two same-numbered memos hang off one job, you cannot tell which log line belongs to which — so the code leaves the text alone rather than corrupting it.
8 · Persist
If anything changed: flSave() then save('config') in a try/catch. Returns {n, changed:[{id,from,to,title}]} for the confirmation dialog.
INVARIANT Renumbering never deletes anything and never touches: maintId/incidentId/memoId foreign keys (they are ids, not numbers), amounts, statuses, or any list membership. It rewrites exactly three things: no, prevNo, and free-text strings that happened to quote the old number.

laRenumberOne(k, id) :19414 — same algorithm for one chosen document. Difference: it does not apply §keep. The user picks the row, so whichever row is clicked is the one that moves, even if it is the elder. Its ambiguity test differs too — it re-derives the group by scanning arr for x.no===oldNo instead of using a pre-built group.

costNoRenumberOne(k,id) :19446 and costNoCancelOne(k,id) :19457 are the UI wrappers on each drawer row. The confirm text spells out the guarantees: other same-numbered documents are untouched, the old number is kept in prevNo, nothing is deleted. costNoCancelOne refuses anything but noMemo and delegates to the app's existing flCancelMemo (which enforces "must give a reason" and "a paid memo cannot be cancelled") rather than re-implementing cancel rules.

costDupRenumber(k) :19467 — the bulk button. Lists up to 10 colliding numbers in the confirm, calls laRenumberSet, then reports up to 12 from → to lines.

§keep scoring — which record survives a clean :19487

laDupScore(x) ranks "how much content does this record carry":

SignalWeight
parts[], items[]×10 each
progressLog[], history[]×5 each
attachments[] / assets[]×3 / ×2 each
endDate present+3
approvedBy/confirmedBy/closeNote+2
status beyond pending/draft+2
inbound references (memo.maintId, maint.incidentId, inventory history jobId/memoId)×20 each
WHY ×20 The comment: "ใบที่มีคนชี้อยู่ ห้ามลบทิ้งก่อนใบที่ไม่มีใครชี้" — a referenced document must never lose to an unreferenced one. The 20× weight is what makes reference count dominate content count. If you add a new signal, keep it well below 20 or you will break that ordering.

§scan · laDupScan() — read-only survey :19509

Returns {sets, nested, nums, nDup, nMoney, nNo}. Explicitly mutates nothing. Three passes:

  • sets — group by S.key, sort each group by laDupScore desc, report {name, keepId, drop, ids} (the ids that would be removed). Capped at 40 groups for display; the extra count is not capped.
  • nums — counted into out.nNo, deliberately not into nDup: "ไม่นับรวมกับ ข้อมูลซ้ำ เพราะแก้คนละวิธี" — number collisions are a different problem with a different fix, so mixing the counters would mislead. clean:false is hard-coded for every nums row.
  • nested — per owner document; only lists with ≥2 entries are examined; id-less rows contribute nothing.

extra throughout means "records beyond the first in each group", i.e. how many could be removed, not how many groups exist.

§clean · laDupClean(key) — the only deleting path :19550

Guard
if(!S.clean) return 0 — boats, engines, gearboxes, propellers, agents, bookings and inventory can never be auto-cleaned here.
Build the survivor map
Highest laDupScore wins per group; map[loserId] = keeperId.
Re-point references FIRST
:19565 "ย้าย reference ทุกเส้นก่อน · เงินและประวัติจะได้ไม่ขาดตอน" — move every link before deleting so money and history stay connected. Three link types are rewritten: FL_MEMOS[].maintId, FL_MAINT[].incidentId, and FL_INVENTORY[].history[].jobId / .memoId.
Delete in place
Filters, then arr.length=0 + re-push. The array object identity is preserved on purpose — FL_MAINT etc. are referenced by other closures, so reassigning would orphan them.
Persist
Only when removed>0: flSave() + save('bookings'). Note the different save() topic vs the renumber path's 'config'.
REFERENCE COVERAGE The re-pointing list is hard-coded to exactly three link shapes. If you add a new field anywhere that stores a maintenance/incident/memo id, you must add it here too, or laDupClean will leave a dangling id pointing at a deleted record.

laDupCleanAll() :19593 loops both LA_DUP_SETS and LA_DUP_NESTED, skipping clean:false.

§see / §owner · costDupInspect(key) — the evidence drawer :19599

Renders whichever of the three tables owns key into the shared cost drawer (costDwOpen then direct injection into #cost-dw-tbl). The tab bar of the drawer is hidden (#cost-dw .cdt → display:none).

  • Rows are grouped visually by a gold border-top:2px solid #E0B24B on the first row of each group; only the first row shows the group label.
  • §owner :19613 — for nested lists a second grey line under the group label prints the owner id. Reason given: "เลขที่ใบไม่ใช่ตัวระบุที่เชื่อถือได้ (มีเคสเลขชนกัน)" — the document number is not a reliable identifier precisely because numbers collide, so the id must be shown alongside it.
  • For LA_DUP_NUMS only, a "จัดการ" (manage) column is appended with an ออกเลขใหม่ (renumber) button per row, plus a ยกเลิกใบนี้ (cancel) button when key==='noMemo' && status ∉ {paid, cancelled}.
  • Header/count wording switches: number collisions read "N เลข · N ใบ ... ห้ามลบ ต้องออกเลขใหม่" (must renumber, never delete); duplicates read "N กลุ่ม · N รายการ · ส่วนเกิน".
esc IS LOCAL :19603 costDupInspect declares its own const esc = (typeof escapeHTML==='function') ? escapeHTML : String. Per the project rule, esc is not global — any new renderer here must declare its own or it throws silently on click.
NOTE Row ids are interpolated into inline onclick strings with only String(x.id).replace(/'/g,'') as sanitisation. Fine for generated ids; would break for ids containing quotes or backslashes.

costDupClean(key) :19666 and costDupCleanAll() :19674 are the confirm-then-clean wrappers. The "clean all" confirm names what is deliberately excluded: boats / engines / gearboxes / propellers / agents / bookings — "เพราะถูกอ้าง id กระจายทั้งระบบ" (their ids are referenced all over the system, so they must be handled one at a time).

§mjDedupe · flDedupeMaint(list) :19683

A defensive filter meant to be called wherever FL_MAINT is assigned, guarding against an old hook or an import re-inserting identical jobs. Key = no + boatId + title + type + startDate. Jobs with no no are passed through untouched — an unnumbered draft must never be matched against anything.

§flBoatPersist · flSave() :19696

The Fleet module's write path. Reads the RAM blob, overwrites the fleet keys, writes back:

d.fleet_engines · fleet_gearboxes · fleet_propellers · fleet_daily
fleet_fuelprice · fleet_drlock · fleet_maintenance · fleet_incidents
fleet_inventory · fleet_memos · fleet_safety · fleet_projects
fleet_version · fleet_consumable_logs
d.boats = BOATS        // §flBoatPersist

The §flBoatPersist comment records the bug this line fixes: the Fleet side mutates b.log (boat status) constantly, but never called save() — save() is the only other writer of d.boats, and Fleet does not call it. Result: every boat-status change made from a Fleet screen vanished on refresh. Adding d.boats=BOATS to flSave() is what makes Fleet-side status edits durable.

TRAP Because flSave() now writes the whole BOATS array, any Fleet code path that mutates a boat and then calls flSave() publishes all in-memory boat state. Conversely, first line: if(laCanEditArea && !laCanEditArea('fleet')) return; — a view-only fleet user's flSave() is a silent no-op, so a caller that assumes "flSave then re-render shows my change persisted" is wrong for those users.

Consumable requisition · §82 :19709

Withdrawing a consumable (engine oil, filters…) from central inventory to top up a boat. The header comment states the design boundary: this is not a repair job. It deducts stock and books the cost in its own bucket. The page's headline metric is Upkeep รวม = ค่าซ่อม(MJ) + ของใช้ — repairs plus consumables.

Stores touched

Write: FL_CONSUMABLE_LOGS (own store, persisted as fleet_consumable_logs) and FL_INVENTORY[].stocks[] + .history[]. Read: BOATS, FL_ENGINES, FL_MAINT.

Numbering

None. Records get LA_UID('cons_') ids only — no no field, which is exactly why the consum dup-audit bucket is sure:false, clean:false.

Statuses

None either. A consumable log has no lifecycle: it exists or it is deleted (which restores stock). There is no draft/approved/paid chain like a memo.

Record shape :19821

{id, date, itemId, itemName, unit, qty, unitCost, cost, location, boatId, engineId, engineLabel, by, note}

Module state and formatters :19712–:19724:

  • _flConsumeMonth — YYYY-MM filter, built from local getFullYear/getMonth. The inline comment flags the reason: toISOString shifts −7h in Thailand and lands you in the previous month.
  • _flConsumeBoat — declared 'all' but not referenced anywhere else in this slice (inferred: dead or reserved for a future filter).
  • _flcEsc, _flcB (฿ formatter), _flBoatNm — module-local helpers.
  • flConsumeCostOf(rec) = round(qty × unitCost) — uses the snapshotted unitCost on the log, not the item's current cost, so history does not re-price itself.
  • flBoatRepairCostMonth(boatId, ym) :19719 — MJ cost for a boat in a month, bucketed by closedDate || startDate || date || createdDate (first non-empty wins), valued via flMaintCalcCost(m.id).

Withdraw modal flConsumeOpen() :19726 — a full-screen overlay #fl-consume-modal, built as an HTML string. Notable behaviours:

  • The item list is sorted oil-first everywhere (/oil|น้ำมัน/i test as the primary sort key) — in the modal seed, in the typeahead, and in the "oil withdrawn" KPI.
  • Typeahead: flConsumeItemFilter :19760 filters name+partNo+category, caps at 40, and each option is picked via onmousedown + event.preventDefault() so the input's onblur-hide (180 ms timeout) does not eat the click.
  • flConsumeItemChanged :19777 repopulates the warehouse dropdown from it.stocks[], falling back to a synthetic single-stock entry for legacy items with only location/qty.
  • flConsumeBoatChanged :19782 narrows the engine dropdown to that boat's engines; engine is optional ("ทั้งลำ / ไม่ระบุ").
  • flConsumePreview :19788 shows qty × cost and a red warning when qty exceeds invQtyAt(item, loc).

flConsumeSubmit() :19798 — the commit:

Validate
item required, qty ≥ 1, boat required (English alert() for the first two, Thai for the boat).
Over-withdraw = soft confirm
If qty > have, confirm "ยืนยันเบิกทำให้ติดลบ?" — negative stock is allowed on confirmation, not blocked.
Deduct
invRemoveAt; if it returns false (insufficient) the code manually drives the stock line negative and calls invSyncLegacy, or falls back to it.qty -= qty for legacy items.
Double-write
Pushes a type:'withdraw' row onto it.history carrying consumeId, AND pushes the log onto FL_CONSUMABLE_LOGS. consumeId is the only link between the two.
Save + re-render
flSave() → close → renderConsumables().
TRAP flConsumeDelete(id) :19826 restores stock via invAddAt and removes the history row by h.consumeId!==id. If a future code path writes a consumable history row without consumeId, delete will restore the stock but leave the history line behind — a phantom withdrawal.
DATE The modal's date field defaults with toISOString().slice(0,10) :19746 and flConsumeSubmit falls back to the same — contradicting the local-date rule that _flConsumeMonth itself follows. Late-evening Thai withdrawals can therefore default to yesterday and fall out of the current month filter.

renderConsumables() :19834 paints #fl-consume-wrap: month stepper (flConsumeMonthShift), KPI strip (consumable spend, oil units withdrawn, MJ repair cost, combined Upkeep), a per-boat table of repair vs consumable vs upkeep (rows with zero on both sides are dropped, sorted by upkeep desc), and the month's withdrawal history with a delete button per row. Accent colour #0F6E56.

Fleet seed tables · FL_DEFAULT_* :19911

~690 lines of literal seed data. Per the project rule these fill empty first-run arrays only; appending an item here never reaches an already-seeded install unless flLoad() gets an idempotent merge.

ConstantLineContents / notes
FL_DEFAULT_ENGINES:19911~57 outboards, grouped by boat with a // ── BoatName (bN) ── banner each. Honda BF250D/BF250A and Suzuki DF325A/DF250W. DRIFT The FL_FUEL_PRICE comment says the second key is a pier, but the Daily Log actually reads and writes FL_FUEL_PRICE[ds][b.id] — a boat id (:22112, and the row comment "PER BOAT · no pier-level price box"). The comment is stale. FL_DR_LOCK's pier key is genuine.

FL_DAILY is the Daily-Log store, shaped FL_DAILY[date][boatId] = {fuel, paxActual, trips:{tripKey:{engines:{engineId:meterReading}}}}. The Daily Log UI only ever writes the 'normal' trip key (:22140), though flEngHours reads all of them.

Safety equipment: categories + one-shot seed :20615

FL_SAFETY_CATEGORIES — 10 categories keyed to Thai Marine Department (กรมเจ้าท่า) requirements: bilge_pump, life_jacket, fire_ext, epirb, flare, vhf, first_aid, anchor, nav_light, each {label, th, color, regulatory, pmMonths, pmYears}.

KEY DISTINCTION The header comment is explicit: inspection cadence ≠ physical expiry. A flare is inspected monthly (pmMonths:1) but physically expires after 3 years. pmYears is retained only for backward-compatible display.

_generateSafetySeed() :20628 — no-ops if FL_SAFETY.length > 0, so it runs once. Skips retired and charter boats. Seeds 6 items per boat (bilge pump, life jackets, 2 fire extinguishers, EPIRB, VHF, first aid); flare, anchor and nav_light are defined as categories but never seeded. Life-jacket qty tracks b.cap || b.licensePax || 30. Ids are sf0001… from a local counter, and nextPM is computed from a fixed inspectBase='2025-12-01' plus the category cadence.

TRAP Ids are assigned sequentially at seed time from sid=1. Re-running after a partial wipe (some rows deleted but FL_SAFETY non-empty) does nothing; re-running against an emptied array would re-mint sf0001 and collide with any surviving references elsewhere.

Multi-warehouse inventory helpers :20715

WAREHOUSE_LOCATIONS = ['คลัง Tub Lamu','คลัง Visit Panwa','คลัง Ranong'] — Thai display strings, not the pier enum. Do not conflate these with pier:'tublamu'|'panwa'|'ranong'; the two vocabularies are separate and are matched by exact string equality inside stocks[].

FnLineBehaviour
invSyncLegacy(item):20718Migration + recompute. Creates stocks[] from legacy location/qty if missing; recomputes totalQty, mirrors it into qty, and sets primaryLocation/location to the warehouse holding the most.
invQtyAt(item, loc):20733Exact-string lookup in stocks[]; 0 if the item has no stocks at all.
invAddAt(item, loc, qty):20740Creates the stock line if absent, then re-syncs.
invRemoveAt(item, loc, qty):20753Returns false and changes nothing when the line is missing or short. Callers must handle false (see flConsumeSubmit).
invTransfer(item, from, to, qty, date, note):20763Remove-then-add, writing two history rows (transfer-out + transfer-in). Aborts before any mutation if the source is short.
TRAP invSyncLegacy overwrites item.qty with the computed total. Any legacy code that still writes item.qty directly (there is such a path in flConsumeSubmit's fallback branch) will have its value discarded the next time any inv* helper runs on that item.
NOTE invQtyAt returns 0 for a legacy item that has never been through invSyncLegacy — flConsumePreview and flConsumeSubmit guard this by falling back to it.qty when the helper is undefined, but not when it is defined and returns 0.

Engine hours & the engine service cycle :20789

flGetEng / flGetGb :20789 are trivial finders. The substance is flEngHours(id) :20791:

hours = baseHours + (latest Daily-Log meter − first Daily-Log meter)
// readings with v == null or +v <= 0 are skipped
  • It walks all of FL_DAILY (every date × every boat × every trip) looking for trip.engines[id]. The comment explains why it is not scoped to the boat the engine currently sits on: the hour meter lives on the engine and follows it across boat swaps, so scoping by boat would lose history.
  • Skipping <= 0 readings is the documented fix for a 0 placeholder entry otherwise inflating the total enormously.
  • With no usable readings it returns baseHours.

flEngServiceState(e) :20810 — countdown relative to the last recorded service, not a modulo of total hours: base = e.lastServiceHours ?? e.baseHours ?? 0, next = base + serviceInterval, left = next − curH, overdue = left < 0. With interval === 0 it returns a neutral state rather than dividing by zero.

flEngMarkService(engId) :20823 — prompt() for the hour reading (defaulted to current), sets lastServiceHours/lastServiceDate, appends a type:'service' log entry, flSave(), then re-renders whichever engine views exist.

TWO INTERVALS COEXIST Per-engine serviceInterval is seeded at 100 h and drives flEngServiceState. But the dashboard's "Service due" card at :21646 hard-codes its own SERVICE_INTERVAL=500 and uses a pure modulo of total hours, ignoring lastServiceHours entirely. The two will disagree. Changing one does not change the other.

Gearbox service cycle :20840

Mirrors the engine cycle but counts hours from the attached engine, because a gearbox has no meter of its own.

lifetime = Σ(log[type==='remove'].usedHours)          // prior engines
         + max(0, flEngHours(g.engineId) − g.baseHours) // current engine
  • GB_DEF_SVC_INTERVAL = 200 :20840 — default gear-oil interval when a gearbox has no serviceInterval; flGbServiceState returns defaulted:true so the UI can say so.
  • Never-serviced handling :20853: base = g.lastServiceHours ?? life — i.e. assume "serviced now" rather than "overdue since forever". The comment: don't spam overdue until a real service is logged.
  • attached: !!g.engineId — a detached (spare) gearbox accrues nothing.
  • flGbMarkService :20861 and flGbSetInterval :20876 mirror the engine equivalents; flGbSetInterval rejects non-positive input and re-renders to restore the displayed value.

Maintenance cost share & service-baseline reset :20888

flMaintCostShare(m, kind) :20888 — flMaintCalcCost(m.id) ÷ (number of assets of the SAME kind on the job), keyed engId/gbId/propId. So a 4-engine oil change shows ~¼ of the cost on each engine detail page.

SCOPE This division is for asset detail pages only. Job / boat / project / dashboard totals must keep counting each job once — using flMaintCostShare in an aggregate would under-count. The denominator floors at 1 (|| 1).

flMaintServiceReset(m, outcome) :20894 — offered when a maintenance job closes.

Outcome gate
Only 'success' or 'limited'. A cancelled or failed job never resets hours.
Looks-like-a-service gate
m.type==='scheduled' OR the title+detail matches /น้ำมัน|ถ่าย|เซอร์วิส|service|\boil\b|svc|เกียร์|gear/i. Free-text driven — a service job titled without any of these words silently will not offer the reset.
Collect assets
De-duped engIds and gbIds from m.assets[]. Bails if both are empty.
One confirm for all
Lists every engine/gearbox with its current hours; OK resets all, Cancel resets none. There is no per-asset choice despite what the comment above the function suggests.
Write baselines + logs
Sets lastServiceHours/lastServiceDate=TODAY_STR and appends a log line naming the job (m.no || m.id). Returns n>0. Does NOT call flSave() — the caller must save.

flMaintServiceResetManual(jobId) :20925 is the button version: forces outcome='success', and does call flSave() plus re-renders. Its failure alert() lumps three different causes into one message ("not a service job, no engines, or cancelled").

flStatusPill(s) :20931 maps status→pill-green|amber|red|gray + English label (a status not in the map falls through as raw text in a grey pill). flFmtShort(s) :20936 renders "D ม.ค." style short Thai dates.

The n8n-style canvas: wires, world space, pan/zoom :20941

flDashDate :20941 / flDashShift(n) :20942 — date state for the dashboard. Note flDashShift reads and writes #fl-dash-date, so flDashDate itself is effectively unused after init (inferred).

§draw · flDrawPierWires() :20949

Draws every parent→child connector as one SVG path string injected into #fl-pier-wires.

  • Generic, data-driven: it does not know about piers or boats. It indexes every #fl-canvas-world .fl-node[data-nodeid], then for every node carrying data-parent it draws a wire from the parent's right edge to the child's left edge, coloured by the child's data-wcolor. Adding a new node tier requires only those three attributes.
  • World-space maths :20950 — the critical detail. Bounding rects are screen-space, so every coordinate is taken relative to the SVG's own rect and then divided by the current scale: (r.right - ref.left) / sc. The SVG lives inside the transformed world, so the wires must be authored in untransformed world units or they drift as you zoom.
  • Bezier shape: M x1 y1 C (x1+dx) y1, (x2−dx) y2, x2 y2 where dx = max(18, (x2−x1)*0.5) — a horizontal-tangent cubic, i.e. the classic node-editor S-curve. The max(18,…) floor keeps the curve readable when a child has been dragged to the left of its parent.
  • Stroke is fixed at width 1.9, opacity 0.62. Because the whole world is CSS-scaled, wire width scales with zoom.
  • Redraw is required after any node move, any re-render, and window resize (a one-shot resize listener is installed at :21781).
TRAP sc is read from world.dataset.sc. If you ever transform the world without writing dataset.sc, every wire silently lands in the wrong place. apply() in flCanvasInit is the only writer.

§full-n8n · flCanvasInit() :20969

Three-part state: the view (pan/zoom) and the node positions persist by different mechanisms, and both survive re-render.

View state — window._flCanvasView

:20973 {tx,ty,sc} mirrored onto world.dataset and into a window global on every apply(). On the next flCanvasInit the global wins over the dataset. Why: a background sync from another user re-renders the dashboard; without this the view would snap back to 1× mid-inspection.

Node positions — localStorage

Key fl_n8n_pos_<user>, taken from world.dataset.lsk (built at :21403 from laSbUser() / ME.username / 'anon'). Value: {nodeId: [x,y]}, ints. Written on drag end only.

ALLOWED localStorage This is a tiny per-user UI preference, not business data — it is within the project's "tiny UI/cache keys only" rule. Do not extend it to carry anything else.

Pointer handling :20987 onward, all on the viewport #fl-canvas-vp with pointer capture:

  • vp.dataset.bound guards against double-binding across re-renders — but note the apply(); redraw(); above the guard runs every time, which is what restores the saved view.
  • pointerdown picks the mode: on a .fl-node → drag node (raises zIndex=20); on empty space → pan. Zoom buttons are excluded by a closest() test.
  • 4 px drag threshold — below it nothing moves, so clicking a job card still navigates. Above it, vp._swallowClick=1 is set and a capture-phase click listener eats the click that follows the drag. Without this, every node drag would also open that job.
  • Node movement divides by scale (dx/sc) so a dragged node tracks the cursor exactly at any zoom.
  • Wheel zoom :21012 — factor 1.12 / 0.89, clamped to [0.35, 2.2], anchored at the cursor: tx = mx − (mx−tx)*(ns/sc). {passive:false} + preventDefault so the page does not scroll. Button zoom (#fl-zin/#fl-zout) uses the same maths anchored at the viewport centre; #fl-zfit resets to 0,0,1 (it is a reset, not a fit-to-content).
  • The dotted-grid background is kept in register with the world by setting backgroundPosition = tx,ty and backgroundSize = 19*sc.

flRenderDashboard() — the Fleet dashboard :21024

~760 lines: compute a large metric block, then build one giant HTML string into #fl-dash-body, then requestAnimationFrame(flCanvasInit). Palette constants SVG_PINK={accent:'#E03B7E',soft:'#FCE5EC',text:'#9F1B4F'} and a dim greyscale set are redeclared locally (and again inside flRenderDR).

Metrics block :21028

  • companyBoats = BOATS minus ownership==='charter' and retired. Status via getCurStatus(b, ds).s; pier via getBoatCurrentPier(b) falling back to b.pier — the "where is it really" vs "home pier" distinction.
  • Spare definition :21042: x.spareLocation || x.status==='spare'. Sub-buckets read the spareLocation prefix: boat: → carried on board, shop: → in repair.
  • §active incident :21083 — status==='open' || status==='inprogress'. The tag records the bug: counting only 'open' made incidents that were actively being repaired disappear from the count.
  • Low stock uses the legacy i.qty <= i.minQty (item-level, not per-warehouse) and sorts by qty/minQty — which is NaN/Infinity when minQty is 0, as it is for every seeded item.
  • High-alert boat :21093 — score = 3 per major open incident, 1 per minor, 2 per open maintenance job; highest wins.

Header bar with per-pier chips (TL/VP/RN, RN only when non-empty), a 6-column KPI strip (Fleet · Fuel · High alert · Maintenance · Projects · Incidents), a brand pill row (Honda/Suzuki share, spares on board), a Boat-Status card grouped by current pier with a proportional 3-colour status bar, an engine-model bar chart, a pink spare-inventory tile, and a low-stock + memo-chips tile. NOTE Pier metadata is declared twice with different shapes and slightly different labels: PIER_META at :21227 (includes a synthetic shop pier) and _pierMeta at :21393 (adds other). Editing one does not affect the other.

Pending-work model :21395

  • Per boat: open jobs + open incidents, total cost via flMaintCalcCost, and oldest = greatest age in days of any job's startDate. Sorted by load, then age, then cost.
  • §n8n canvas incident filter :21390 — _incRepairsAllDone(i) hides an incident from the canvas when every linked job (maintId + relatedMaintIds[]) is done. The comment is careful to note that KPI and incident-list counts are unchanged — only the canvas hides them. So the canvas can legitimately show fewer incidents than the Incidents KPI tile.

§3-tier canvas layout engine :21400

Deterministic absolute layout, computed fresh on every render, then overridden by saved drag positions.

ConstantValueRole
PIERX / BOATX / INCX / MJX14 / 286 / 560 / 850Column x for each tier
PIERH / BOATH / JOBH48 / 62 / 54Node heights
JOBGAP / BOATGAP / PIERGAP12 / 26 / 42Vertical gaps
MEMOH / MEMOGAP / JOB_TO_MEMO42 / 10 / 2924th tier: memos to the right of their job

Tiers: pier → boat → [incident | standalone job] → [incident-linked job] → memo. Routine (non-incident) jobs hang directly off the boat and are placed in the INCX column; incident-caused jobs sit one column further right at MJX.

  • _placeJob(m, jx) :21423 reserves a vertical slot max(JOBH, memoBlockHeight) so a job with many memos cannot overlap the next job, centres the job card in that slot, stacks its memos beside it, and returns the job's centre-Y.
  • Parents are centred on their children: an incident is centred between its first and last linked job; a boat between its first and last column-3 entry; a pier between its first and last boat.
  • §dedupe memo :21416 — the data contains several memo records sharing one no and maintId, differing only by status (e.g. an approved and a paid copy). The canvas keeps one card per no, picking the highest _memoRank. This is display-only de-duplication; the underlying records are untouched (that is laDupClean's job).
  • Saved positions win last :21444: Object.keys(_savedPos) overwrites _pos[id] — but only for ids the computed layout also produced, so stale entries for deleted jobs are harmless.
  • World size: _worldH = max(320, _cy+10), _worldW = max(MJX+282, MJX+JOB_TO_MEMO+264). Viewport height is clamped to [360, 720].
TRAP A dragged node keeps its saved position forever, even after the auto-layout would place it somewhere sensible. There is no "reset layout" control — #fl-zfit only resets pan/zoom. Clearing localStorage['fl_n8n_pos_<user>'] is the only way back.

The effective-PAX model :21804

One computation feeds both KPIs and rows — this is called out in the comment as the single source.

booked
flBoatBookingsFor(b.id, ds).pax — from sales.
actual
FL_DAILY[ds][boatId].paxActual if entered, else null.
ranDespite — "ออกแล้วเสียเย็น"
:21815 The boat is marked fixing/unavailable but actually ran that day. Evidence accepted: real Daily-Log data already saved (fuel>0, paxActual!=null, or any engine meter in trips) OR bookings exist AND cs.from === ds (today is the first day of the downtime → it broke after the morning trip).
eff
operated = available || ranDespite; eff = !operated ? 0 : (actual ?? booked). Everything downstream — totals, top boat, anomalies, whether the row is editable — reads eff.
TRAP The cs.from === ds half of the ranDespite test only fires on the first day of a downtime window. A boat that ran on day 2 of a "fixing" window with no Daily-Log data yet will be scored 0 PAX and shown as non-operating.

Day metrics :21823

  • Totals sum eff; totalTrips += max(1, bp.trips) so an operating boat always counts ≥1 trip.
  • Anomaly = a boat whose fuel/eff > 20 L/PAX. Drives a red row background and the Anomalies KPI.
  • §boatSplit :21845 — charter counting uses bkBoatIdsOn(bk, ds) to catch every boat a split charter booking uses, falling back to t.charterBoatId || bk.ops.boatId. Cancelled statuses (cancelled|rejected|cancelled_weather) are excluded, as everywhere.
  • plannedTrips = companyBoats.length — a baseline of 1 trip per boat, so "% executed" is a rough figure by construction.

Week strip & the §ป้ายเดือน month card :21960

Monday-first 7-day strip showing the week containing the selected day; the ‹ › buttons move one day (not one week), so stepping back from Monday selects Sunday and the strip slides to the previous week.

§ป้ายเดือน :21983 A long Thai comment explains the month card: the strip showed only day numbers, so after arrowing back several weeks "6" was ambiguous between July and June — the only month indicator was the tiny date input in the corner that nobody looks at. The card shows the month (both months, e.g. "มิ.ย. – ก.ค.", when the week straddles two) and the Buddhist year. Its width is hard-coded to 208px to match the table's BOAT column exactly (32px avatar + 14 gap + 162px name) so it aligns with the header below. If you change the row grid, change this number too.

Row builder buildBoatRow(b, maxEng, locked) :21886

Grid: 32px | 162px | 232px | 1fr = avatar · boat · routes/trips · entry cards.

  • Route source order :21925: operations TRIPS[ds][boatId].trips[] → single route/program → derived from the boat's actual bookings (bp.routes) → a single 'Normal' fallback when pax>0.
  • Orphan warning :21913 — a booking still points at this boat but the boat was pulled from Boat Operation for that date (removed, chartered, or re-routed). Detected with the shared bkV2BoatPulled(bk, ds); renders "⚠ เรือถูกถอดจาก Boat Op · จัดใหม่".
  • Engine columns are ordered by flPosRank (Port → C.Port → Center → C.Std → Std), not by array order.
  • Entry cards, all fixed-height 50px so they line up across rows: PAX (read-only, pulled from bookings), Fuel L (amber, shows live L/PAX, red when flagged as an anomaly), ฿/L per boat (pulses when fuel is entered but no price is), then a divider and one engine-hour input per engine showing the previous meter as placeholder and +Δh once filled.
  • noEntry — no inputs at all when the boat is unavailable/fixing and did not run, or when pax is 0.
  • _DIS = locked ? 'disabled' : '' — flDRLocked(ds, pierKey) makes the whole pier read-only once saved.
TRAP Engine meters are read and written at dayDR.trips.normal.engines[engId] — the trip key 'normal' is hard-coded in both the read :22140 and the flSaveMeter call. flEngHours iterates all trip keys, so any other trip key written elsewhere would count toward hours but never be visible or editable here.

Pier sections :22181

  • _drPierClosed(pierKey) :22173 — if every route at that pier reports getDayStatus(rt, ds).type === 'closed', the whole section collapses to a "ปิด · Program" banner and no log is recorded (off-season).
  • Section header aggregates PAX/trips/L and fuel cost. Cost is Σ(boat litres × that boat's ฿/L); if any boat has litres but no price, a "บางลำไม่มีราคา" warning appears rather than a wrong total.
  • Save/Edit: flSaveDayLog(this, pierKey, ds) locks the pier; flDREdit(ds, pierKey) unlocks it. Lock state lives in FL_DR_LOCK[ds][pierKey].
  • Section header PAX/trips here use TRIPS[ds][b.id].booked — not the boatPax effective model used by the rows and the KPI strip, so the section subtotal can disagree with the rows above it (inferred inconsistency).
  • Three sections are emitted: Tub Lamu, Visit Panwa, Ranong. Sections with no boats render nothing.
INLINE STYLE BLOCK :22237 flRenderDR injects a <style> element inside its own innerHTML, defining .fldr-cell:focus-within rings (driven by per-card --ring/--rb CSS vars) and the flfpPulse animation for a missing fuel price. It is re-injected on every render.

The slice ends mid-declaration at function flPrevMeter(engId,dateStr){ :22244 — the previous-meter lookup used by the engine-hour placeholders; its body continues in the next region.

C Lines 22245–24002 · Fleet: Daily-Log meters & fuel pricing, Company Asset overview, Boat & Engine registries

This slice is one contiguous run of Fleet Management code. It opens with the tail of the Daily Fleet Log write-helpers (engine meter readings, fuel litres, diesel price, day-lock), then switches at :22366 into the Company Asset module — the tabbed registry of boats / engines / gearboxes / propellers / docs / safety — and covers its Overview dashboard, the Boat list + boat detail panel, and the Engine list. Every renderer here is a full innerHTML replace of a mount div; there is no virtual DOM and no template engine — just tagged template strings with inline styles.

SHAPE Three global stores drive most of this slice, all declared far earlier at :20605–:20607 and loaded from SQL at :18526: FL_DAILY[YYYY-MM-DD][boatId] = {fuel, paxActual, trips:{tripType:{engines:{engineId: meterValue}}}}, FL_FUEL_PRICE[YYYY-MM-DD][pierKey|boatId] = ฿/L, and FL_DR_LOCK[YYYY-MM-DD][pierKey] = true. Everything in this slice read-modify-writes those objects in place and then calls flSave().

flPrevMeter + the engine-hours model :22245

flPrevMeter(engId, dateStr) scans every day in FL_DAILY strictly before dateStr, walks every boat and every trip of that day, and returns the meter reading for engId from the latest such day. It is used by the Daily-Log form to show the Δ (hours run) next to a freshly typed meter value.

Two rules are baked into the loop and both matter:

  • Readings <= 0 are skipped — if(v!=null && +v>0 && ds>maxDate). The Thai comment says it outright: "ข้ามค่า 0/ติดลบ (placeholder)". A 0 saved as a placeholder is not a real meter reading.
  • Returning null is deliberate — there is no fallback to baseHours. The comment: null = ยังไม่มีค่าก่อนหน้า → ไม่โชว์ Δ … baseHours ที่เป็น "ชั่วโมงสะสม" คนละหน่วยกับมิเตอร์ — baseHours is cumulative lifetime hours, a different unit from the physical meter face. Mixing them produces nonsense deltas.

The companion accumulator flEngHours(id) lives just outside this slice at :20791, but the whole slice depends on it (engine cards, KPI totals, service countdowns). Its formula:

hours = baseHours + (latest Daily-Log meter − first Daily-Log meter)
        // all readings with value == null or value <= 0 are skipped
        // no log at all  →  hours = baseHours
THE 0-PLACEHOLDER TRAP The meter travels with the engine, not the boat, so readings are never scoped by which hull the engine was on. That means first is the earliest reading ever logged for that engine. If a single 0 placeholder were allowed through, first would become 0 and the delta would become the entire meter face — e.g. a 4,200 h engine would suddenly report baseHours + 4200. Both flPrevMeter and flEngHours guard with +v>0; any new code that reads trip.engines[id] must repeat that guard.
COST flPrevMeter is O(days × boats × trips) and is called per engine input field while rendering a Daily-Log day. It does not short-circuit once it passes dateStr — the if(ds>=dateStr)return; is a per-entry skip inside a full Object.entries walk, not an early exit.

Daily-Log fuel litres & diesel price lookup :22258

FnLineWhat it does
flSaveFuel(ds,bid,val):22258Writes litres bunkered onto FL_DAILY[ds][bid].fuel. parseFloat(val)||null — so a typed 0 also becomes null.
flFuelPrice(ds,pierKey):22264Strict per-pier ฿/L for a day, else null.
flFuelPriceForBoat(ds,boat):22266Per-boat override first (m[boat.id]), then the pier default (m[boat.pier]), else null. Some boats bunker at a different ฿/L.
flSaveFuelPrice(ds,pierKey,val):22321Writes/deletes a price. Empty or NaN deletes the key rather than storing 0 — important, because 0 and "unset" mean different things downstream. Re-renders the Daily Report to refresh cost totals.
KEY SPACE FL_FUEL_PRICE[ds] is a mixed map: its keys are either pier enums (tublamu, panwa) or boat ids. Anything iterating it must disambiguate by looking the key up as a boat (that is exactly what _flPierSibPrice does).

§fuelEff — the fuel price you can actually bill :22267

A banner comment block marks this region §fuelEff · ราคาน้ำมันที่เอาไปคิดเงินได้จริง ("the fuel price you can actually charge"). Its reasoning, translated:

  • flFuelPriceForBoat above is deliberately strict — no price for that boat that day ⇒ null. That is correct for the data-entry screen: a blank cell must stay blank so staff can see it has not been filled in.
  • But P&L cannot use it directly — "no price" would silently mean "fuel cost = ฿0", which is never true.
  • The recorded evidence: of 175 real trips, 49 (28%) had no price, so 6,803 litres were being costed at ฿0.

So flFuelPriceEff(ds, boat) :22289 walks a fallback ladder and — crucially — reports where the number came from, returning {price, src, from}.

1 · src:'boat'
FL_FUEL_PRICE[ds][boat.id] — this boat's own price today.
2 · src:'pier'
FL_FUEL_PRICE[ds][pier] — the pier default today. pier = boat.pier || boat.homePier.
3 · src:'sib'
_flPierSibPrice :22278 — any other boat at the same pier that day. Justified by the comment "น้ำมันท่าเดียวกันวันเดียวกันราคาเดียวกันเสมอ" (same pier, same day ⇒ same price, always).
4 · src:'back'
Walk earlier dates newest-first, retrying boat → pier → sibling at each. Capped by FL_FUEL_BACK_DAYS = 30 :22288 via _flDayGap :22274 — beyond a month the price is no longer credible, so the loop breaks.
5 · fall through
Returns the initial {price:0, src:'', from:''}. Only now is fuel genuinely costed at ฿0, and the UI says so.

flFuelSrcTxt(FP) :22313 turns that src into a Thai provenance sentence shown next to the number (e.g. "ยังไม่ได้ลงราคาวันนี้ · ใช้ราคาล่าสุด <date>").

GOTCHA · the 5th src flFuelSrcTxt handles src === 'plan' ("ไม่มีราคาจริงเลย · ใช้ราคาที่ตั้งไว้ในแผนต้นทุนของเส้นทางนี้") but flFuelPriceEff never returns it. The 'plan' source is injected by the caller — the trip P&L at :56491 does if(!FP.price && plan.planFuel>0) FP={price:plan.planFuel, src:'plan'}. So the ladder is really 6 rungs long and rung 5 lives in another file region. Do not "clean up" the unreachable branch in flFuelSrcTxt.
CONTRACT Never swap flFuelPriceForBoat ↔ flFuelPriceEff at a call site without thinking about which side of the line you are on: entry forms use the strict one (blank must show blank), money screens use the effective one (plus its provenance text, which is displayed whenever src is not 'boat'/'pier').

Daily-report lock, save, PAX override, meter write :22329

The Daily Fleet Log is edited per (day × pier). Locking is how a day gets "signed off".

state
unlocked
fields editable; FL_DR_LOCK[ds][pier] absent
→
flSaveDayLog
blur → flSave → lock
