→
state
locked (read-only)
flDRLocked(ds,pier) === true
→
flDREdit
unlock
:22336 — deletes the key
  • flDRLocked(ds,pierKey) :22329 — pure read; the renderer uses it to decide whether inputs render disabled.
  • flDRSetLock(ds,pierKey,val) :22330 — sets true or deletes the key (never stores false), then flSave() then flRenderDR().
  • flDREdit :22336 — one-liner unlock.
  • flSaveDayLog(btn,pierKey,ds) :22338 — the Save button. Three-part behaviour worth reading closely:
    1. Blurs document.activeElement first. The inputs commit on onchange/onblur; without the blur, the value still being typed would be lost on the re-render.
    2. flSave() with a save() fallback in a nested try/catch.
    3. If pierKey && ds it locks and returns immediately — the re-render inside flDRSetLock replaces the button, so the "✓ Saved" flash is skipped. Only the button-only path (no pier/day) does the 1.5 s green-flash-then-restore.
  • flSavePaxActual(ds,bid,val) :22349 — actual PAX override. Empty/NaN ⇒ null, which means "revert to the booked-from-sales count"; otherwise Math.max(0,v). Re-renders because KPI totals (PAX / trips / avg / anomalies) depend on it.
  • flSaveMeter(ds,bid,type,engId,val) :22357 — writes one engine meter reading for one trip type.
FIXED BUG, KEEP THE GUARD flSaveMeter carries an inline note: a boat entry created fuel-first has {fuel:…} but no trips key, which used to throw a TypeError so engine hours could not save. The three defensive lines (if(!…trips), if(!…trips[type]), if(!…trips[type].engines)) are load-bearing. Note the asymmetry: flSaveFuel seeds {trips:{}} while flSaveMeter seeds {fuel:null,trips:{}}.
NOTE Locking gates the UI only. flSaveMeter, flSaveFuel, flSavePaxActual and flSaveFuelPrice never consult flDRLocked — they will happily write to a locked day if called programmatically. The lock is enforced by the renderer, not by the store.

COMPANY ASSET — tab state & spare-location helpers :22366

The // ── COMPANY ASSET ── banner at :22366 starts the asset registry. Module state: flAssetTab='overview' :22367 and flTreeCol={} :22368.

SPARE_LOC_LABELS :22371 is a small lookup keyed by a namespaced location string. This is a real enum — do not invent new keys ad hoc:

KeytypeLabel
pier:tublamupierคลัง Tub Lamu
pier:panwapierคลัง Visit Panwa
pier:centralpierคลังกลาง (central store)
shop:honda-phuketshopอู่ Honda Phuket

  • flParseSpareLoc(loc) :22379 — returns {icon,label,short,type,color,key,boatId?}. Four branches: falsy ⇒ type:'unknown'; boat: prefix ⇒ look up BOATS; a known label ⇒ spread the table row; anything else falls through to type:'other' with the raw string as its label (a legacy-string escape hatch). Callers that switch on type and only handle pier/boat/shop will silently drop 'other' rows — the Overview spare panel guards against this by also collecting unknowns.
  • flGetBoatSpares(bid) :22392 — gearboxes and propellers whose spareLocation === 'boat:'+bid, i.e. spares carried aboard. Returns {gearboxes,propellers,total}. Note it queries only gearboxes and propellers; engines are not included.

Equipment bay — one-piece swap / remove :22398

A modal (fl-modal-equip, body mount #fl-equip-modal-body) that lets an operator move a single engine, gearbox or propeller. Two module-level variables hold the interaction: _flEquipBoat (selected boat) :22399 and _flSwap = {type:'eng'|'gb'|'prop', id} while a swap is armed :22400.

flEquipSetBoat / StartSwap / CancelSwap :22401

Trivial state setters, each re-renders. Selecting a different boat cancels an armed swap.

_flEquipObj(type,id) :22404

Type dispatch: flGetEng / flGetGb / linear find over FL_PROPELLERS. Propellers have no getter helper.

_flEquipLog(obj,kind,desc) :22405

Appends {date:TODAY_STR,type,desc,by:'Equip'} to the asset's own log[], creating it if missing. by:'Equip' is how you find these entries later.

flEquipOpen(boatId) :22427

Sets the boat, clears the swap, renders, then openModal('fl-modal-equip') — guarded with typeof … === 'function'.

flEquipSwapDo(targetId) :22406 exchanges the two pieces' attachment fields, per type:

eng : swap (boatId, pos) between a and b
gb  : swap engineId; then status auto-flip:
      engineId set   && status==='spare' → 'ready'
      engineId null                        → 'spare'
prop: swap gearboxId

Both sides get a _flEquipLog(…,'swap',…) entry naming the counterpart serial, then flSave() + re-render.

TRAPS IN THE SWAP
  • No confirm dialog. flEquipRemove asks confirm('ถอด … เก็บเป็น Spare?'); flEquipSwapDo commits on the first click of "ใส่ที่นี่".
  • The gearbox status flip is destructive. Detaching a gearbox forces status='spare' unconditionally — a gearbox that was 'fixing' or 'broken' loses that state. The reverse flip is conditional (only if status==='spare'), so the two directions are not symmetric.
  • Engine removal does not clear pos. flEquipRemove('eng',…) nulls boatId but leaves a stale drive position on the now-spare engine (cosmetically hidden because list rows show 'SPARE' when !boatId).
  • Coupling model. Here a gearbox is bound to an engine (gb.engineId) and a propeller to a gearbox (prop.gearboxId), so an engine swap drags its gearbox and propeller along with it. That is the opposite of the "gearbox + propeller stay at the boat's drive position (onBoatId/onBoatPos)" model described for the maintenance-side engine swap — flEquipSwapDo never touches onBoatId/onBoatPos or spareLocation. (inferred: these are two different swap paths with different semantics; check which one a bug report is about before changing either.)

flRenderEquip() :22428 builds the modal body: a boat <select>, one card per engine position (sorted by flPosRank) each stacking EN / GB / PR rows via pieceRow, plus a Spare pool strip of chips. When a swap is armed, same-type rows become "ใส่ที่นี่" buttons and an amber hint bar with a cancel button appears; other types are inert. spE/spG/spP define spare-ness structurally (!boatId / !engineId / !gearboxId), not by status==='spare' — a different definition than the Overview panel uses (see below).

Asset tab router — flRenderAsset / flGoAssetTab / flGoToAsset :22466

  • flRenderAsset() :22466 — an if/else chain over flAssetTab dispatching to flRenderOverview · flRenderBoatList · flRenderEngList · flRenderGbList · flRenderPropList · flRenderDocsList · flRenderSafetyList. Seven tabs: overview, boats, engines, gearboxes, propellers, docs, safety. Adding a tab means editing this chain plus #fl-ap-<id> markup.
  • flGoAssetTab(id, el) :22475 — manual tab machinery: clears .active off every .fl-tab-btn and .fl-tab-panel inside #view-fl-asset, adds it to the clicked button and to #fl-ap-<id>, then re-renders. It dereferences assetView without a null check — calling it while that view is absent throws.
  • flGoToAsset(tab, id) :22485 — "open this asset from anywhere". It finds the tab button by a substring match on the inline onclick attribute: querySelector('#view-fl-asset .fl-tab-btn[onclick*="\'engines\'"]'), then sets whichever of flSelBoatId/flSelEngId/flSelGbId/flSelPropId matches, then renders again (so flRenderAsset runs twice per call).
FRAGILE SELECTOR flGoToAsset depends on the tab buttons keeping literal single-quoted onclick attributes. Reformatting that markup to double quotes, or moving to addEventListener, silently breaks every cross-view jump into the asset registry with no error — tabBtn just comes back null and the branch is skipped.

Cross-view navigation helpers :22496

Four helpers used by the Smart Recommendations cards (their call sites are at :31381–:31401). All follow the same idiom: find the sidebar element by [data-view="…"] and hand it to the global nav().

flGoToMaint(maintId) :22496 is the interesting one — it navigates and picks the right archive sub-tab so the job is actually visible when you land:

nav to fl-maintenance
nav(document.querySelector('[data-view="fl-maintenance"]')), guarded by if(navEl).
set _selMaintId
Selects the job.
decide _maintArchiveTab
Looks up the job in FL_MAINT, then collects its memos from FL_MEMOS (memo.maintId === m.id) and computes _invSettled = memos.length > 0 && every(status==='paid').
branch
done && awaitingInvoice && !_invSettled → 'awaiting' · else done → 'archive' · else → 'active'.

HOISTING flGoToMaint assigns _selMaintId and _maintArchiveTab, which are declared with let later in the same script at :28965. This works only because the function runs after script evaluation (from a click). Calling any of these during initial script eval would throw a TDZ ReferenceError.
MAGIC 100 ms The scroll uses a fixed setTimeout(…,100) to wait for flRenderMaint's DOM. On a slow render the element is not there yet and the jump silently does nothing. Same pattern recurs at :23697 for the incident jump (flSelIncident) inside an inline onclick.

flRenderOverview — the Company Asset dashboard :22543

A ~730-line single function that builds the entire Overview tab into #fl-asset-tree in one innerHTML assignment at :23263. Final page order: heroBar + heroStrip + pillRowHtml + statsHtml + spareHtml + listHtml + retiredHtml.

THE FLEET FILTER, REPEATED 5× "Company boats" means b.ownership!=='charter' && !b.retired. That predicate is written out independently at least five times in this one function — _companyBoats :22553, inside boatMatrixCard :22616, companyBoats :22911, the listHtml map :23110, and again in flRenderBoatList :23283. There is no shared helper. Change the definition of "company boat" and you must find every copy.

Aggregation pass :22544

Builds four count maps — statBoats (keyed "<type> <n>EN · <material>"), statEngines / statGearboxes (keyed brand+' '+model), statProps (keyed size). matShort(m) :22546 normalises hull material by substring sniffing in Thai and English (อลูมิ|alumin → Aluminum, ไฟเบอร์|fiber|frp|grp → Fiberglass, stainless → Stainless) and otherwise truncates to 8 characters — so an unrecognised material becomes a truncated raw string and gets its own matrix row.

Card builders :22589–:22908

BuilderLineShape
statCard:22589Generic title/total/rows card. Defined but not used in the final statsHtml — the four matrix/grouped cards replaced it. (inferred: leftover from the pre-redesign layout.)
boatMatrixCard:22608Cross-tab type × engineCount × material. Types sorted Speedboat-first; EN ascending; materials ordered Aluminum → Fiberglass → Stainless → other (matOrder fallback 9).

The gearbox suffix ordering encodes physical drive position and is brand-specific:

Honda  suffixes: XCD  XXCD  XXD  XD
Suzuki suffixes: ZX   ZXX   TXX  TX
posLbl = {XCD:'Po', XXCD:'CP', XXD:'CS', XD:'St',
          ZX:'Po',  ZXX:'CP',  TXX:'CS', TX:'St'}
// Po=Port · CP=C.Port · CS=C.Std/Center · St=Std

getBase(g) :22732 strips a trailing " Gearbox" and a trailing suffix token to recover e.g. "BF250D". Anything whose brand is neither Honda nor Suzuki is silently dropped from the matrix (if(brand!=='Honda'&&brand!=='Suzuki')return;) while still counting toward the headline FL_GEARBOXES.length — the columns will not add up to the total.

Hero header, pier pills & status pills :22910

Computes the counts shown at the top. Spare Inventory panel :22980

Groups spare gearboxes and propellers by spareLocation into a three-column layout: คลังท่าเรือ (pier) · Boat · อื่นๆ (shops + unknown, merged at :23076).

Spare-ness here uses a third, looser definition than the two above:

spareGbs   = FL_GEARBOXES.filter(g => g.status==='spare' || (!g.boatId && !g.engineId))
spareProps = FL_PROPELLERS.filter(p => p.status==='spare' || !p.gearboxId)
  • Items with no spareLocation land under the sentinel UNKNOWN_LOC = '__unknown__' and render with a ⚠ ตั้งค่าคลัง ("set a store location") chip :23066.

Per-boat drivetrain list :23108

One card per company boat, containing a 3-row aligned CSS grid — Engine / Gearbox / Propeller — where every column is one drive position. Grid template: 80px repeat(nEngines, minmax(0,1fr)), so the rows stay vertically aligned across the three asset types.

  • Engines are sorted by an inline literal order map {Port:1,'C.Port':2,Center:3,'C.Std':4,Std:5} at :23111 — a duplicate of FL_POS_RANK (:23793) that does not handle the Suzuki Starboard/Stbd aliases. A Suzuki engine recorded as Starboard sorts to the end (||9) instead of into the Std slot.
  • Gearbox cell = FL_GEARBOXES.find(g => g.engineId === eng.id); propeller cells = FL_PROPELLERS.filter(p => p.gearboxId === gb.id), sorted Front → Rear via propPos. Dual-prop cells make each line individually clickable with event.stopPropagation(); single-prop cells are clickable as a whole.
  • Each cell navigates via flGoToAsset('engines'|'gearboxes'|'propellers', id).
  • A "📦 พกสแปร์" (carrying spares) chip and a bottom Spare strip come from flGetBoatSpares(b.id).
PIER LABEL BUG The boat sub-line at :23190 renders b.pier==='tublamu'?'Tub Lamu':'Visit Panwa' — a two-way ternary. Any other pier value (ranong, or a future enum) is mislabelled as "Visit Panwa". The Boat-list renderer below uses a proper PIER_LBL map instead.

flRenderBoatList — Boats tab, and §In Shop :23279

Renders into #fl-boats-pink-wrap: a header bar of pier pills + + Add Boat, a 5-cell KPI strip, then a two-column body — a scrollable 380 px boat list on the left and a detail mount (#fl-boat-detail-mount) on the right, filled by flRenderBoatDetailPink().

KPIs :23283–:23305: company boat count, total & average cap, Speedboat / Catamaran split (matched by exact type or lowercase substring), engine count and Honda/Suzuki split, average and oldest age from b.year vs TODAY_STR's year, and docAlerts = documents across all boats with d.exp <= today+90d.

§In Shop — the bug this section exists to prevent Grouping at :23410–:23417 uses _pierOf(b) = getBoatCurrentPier(b) ?? b.pier and builds five lists. The comment on the shop line is the history marker: 
const shopList = allCompany.filter(b => _pierOf(b)==='shop');
  /* §In Shop เคยตกหล่น — ไม่มี section นี้ เรือเลยหายจากลิสต์ */
i.e. "In Shop used to be missed — with no such section, those boats vanished from the list." Because getBoatCurrentPier can return the pseudo-pier 'shop', a boat at a workshop matches none of the three real piers. Immediately after, a catch-all closes the hole for good: 
const _shownIds = new Set([...tlList,...vpList,...rnList,...shopList].map(b=>b.id));
const otherList = allCompany.filter(b => !_shownIds.has(b.id));
  /* catch-all กันเรือ pier แปลกๆ หาย */
Any new pier bucket must be added to _shownIds too, or its boats will appear twice (once in the new section, once under "Other").

Section order and colours :23419–:23423. Sections with zero boats are skipped entirely:

SectionPredicateColour
Tub Lamu_pierOf(b)==='tublamu'#0F6E56

DIFFERENT FROM BOAT STATUS This is not the 3-tab Boat Status grouping. The Boat Status page groups by (pier + status) — Tub Lamu = pier==='tublamu' && last.s!=='fixing', Visit Panwa = pier==='panwa' && last.s!=='fixing', In Shop = last.s==='fixing' at any pier. Here in the asset registry the grouping is by getBoatCurrentPier alone, where "shop" is derived from an active in-progress maintenance job with a location that leaves the boat non-available (see §boatPierDate at :9926). The two pages can therefore legitimately disagree about which bucket a boat is in.

Row rendering: boatAvatarColor :23438 prefers getBoatColor(b.id).text (rejecting the neutral #666) and otherwise indexes a 10-colour palette by the boat's position in allCompany — so avatar colours shift when a boat is added or retired. boatInitials handles multi-word names, digit-bearing names, and a 2-char fallback. STATUS_STYLE maps available/fixing/unavailable to badge colours, defaulting to available for an unknown status.

flSelBoat(id) :23441 sets the id, re-renders the whole tab, then in a setTimeout(…,0) finds the row by [onclick*="flSelBoat('id')"] and scrolls it into view with block:'nearest'. Another onclick-substring selector — same fragility as flGoToAsset.

flRenderBoatDetailPink — boat detail panel :23450

Fills #fl-boat-detail-mount. With no selection it shows an empty state; with an unknown id it clears the mount. Every external helper is called through a typeof … === 'function' guard (getBoatColor, getEngPositions, flEngHours, getActiveAssignment, getPlannedAssignments, getPastAssignments).

Sections in render order (final assembly at :23771):

  1. Header strip :23517 — gradient (grey if retired), avatar, boat colour picker (<input type="color"> → flSetBoatColor), status/RETIRED badge, and three buttons: ↩ Restore (retired only), ↔ ถอด/สลับ → flEquipOpen, Edit → flOpenEditBoatModal.
  2. 4 stat cards :23535 — engines, capacity, build year + age, documents (+ expiring count).
  3. General info :23563 — brand/model, reg, callsign, imo, use, material, year, homeportCity labelled "Registered at", and engine layout from getEngPositions(b.engineCount).
  4. Both blocks render only if at least one field is present.
  5. Engines :23620 — per-engine card with position badge (red for Suzuki), serial, current hours via flEngHours, and ⇄ Swap (flOpenAssignEngModal) / ↓ Unassign (flUnassignEng) buttons.
  6. Documents :23645 — expired (exp < today) red ✕, expiring within 90 days amber ⚠, else green.
  7. Recent repairs :23670 — FL_MAINT where boatId matches and status==='done', newest first, capped at 4.
  8. Incidents history :23687 — FL_INCIDENTS for this boat, newest first, capped at 5. Status is derived: an explicit inc.status==='resolved' wins, otherwise it is inherited from the linked maintenance job (pending/inprogress/done→resolved), otherwise open. Shows → <MJ no> or a ⚡ Quick Fix chip when inc.quickFix and there is no MJ.
  9. Owner / Homeport :23736 — owner, homeport, ownerAddr; whole block skipped if all empty.
  10. Pier Assignments :23745 — active / planned / past (past capped at 3) cards showing fromPier → toPier via the external PIER_LABELS map, dates, type (temporary/permanent), reason, cost. + New → flOpenNewAssignment, Cancel → flCancelAssignment. The count badge excludes status==='cancelled'.
TIMEZONE VIOLATION Both 90-day windows are built with ninetyDays.toISOString().slice(0,10) — :23303 in flRenderBoatList and :23503 here. The project rule is to build YYYY-MM-DD with bkV2LocalYMD(dt); toISOString converts to UTC and at +07:00 shifts the boundary back a day, so a document expiring exactly on the 90-day edge can flip in and out of the alert. Both call sites should be migrated together so the KPI count and the per-boat badges stay consistent.
ESCAPING Nothing in this slice escapes interpolated data. Boat names, serials, notes, retiredReason, incident titles and document names go straight into template strings, several of them into title="…" and into inline onclick="…('${id}')". A quote or angle bracket in any of those fields breaks the markup or the handler. This matches the codebase-wide fact that esc/escapeHTML is declared locally per function and is simply absent here.

flRenderBoatDetail — legacy shim :23787

function flRenderBoatDetail(id){ if(typeof flRenderBoatDetailPink==='function') flRenderBoatDetailPink(); } — the comment says it is "kept for backward compatibility, not rendered in pink view". It ignores its id argument and there are no remaining call sites in the file.

FL_POS_CANON / FL_POS_RANK — canonical drive positions :23792

The single source of truth for engine-position naming. Suzuki writes "Starboard" where Honda writes "Std" — same physical side.

FL_POS_CANON = {Port,'C.Port',Center,'C.Std',Std,
                Starboard:'Std', Stbd:'Std', Starb:'Std'}
FL_POS_RANK  = {Port:0,'C.Port':1,Center:2,'C.Std':3,Std:4,
                Starboard:4, Stbd:4, Starb:4}
flPosLabel(p) → canonical label, unknown passes through unchanged  :23794
flPosRank(p)  → rank, unknown → 98 (sorts last)                    :23795
USE THESE, NOT A LITERAL Two places in this slice bypass them with inline literal maps that lack the Suzuki aliases: the drivetrain list at :23111 ({Port:1,…}||9) and the unused POS_ORDER array at :23935. New sorting code should call flPosRank.

flRenderEngList — Engines tab :23796

Renders into #fl-engs-pink-wrap: header bar (Honda/Suzuki pills, + Add Engine), 5-cell KPI strip, filter bar, then list panel (#fl-eng-listpanel) + detail mount (#fl-eng-detail-mount), finishing with flRenderEngDetailPink().

The "retired engine" predicate :23811

const _isRet = (e) => e.retired === true || e.status === 'broken';
// ปลดระวาง (decommissioned) · counted separately, excluded from the fleet
totalEng = all.length − retired
STATUS 'broken' ≡ RETIRED An engine whose status is 'broken' is treated as decommissioned, not as a broken-but-owned unit. It is excluded from totalEng, from the Honda/Suzuki split, from ready/fixing/spare counts, and is pushed into a separate purple "ปลดระวาง · Retired" group at the bottom of the list. The legacy variable broken :23817 is re-aliased to retired with the comment "(legacy var)". Meanwhile the status filter chip still offers "Broken" :23904, which filters on e.status==='broken' — the same engines the KPIs call retired. Do not "fix" one without the other.

KPI strip :23833

  • Total hours — all.reduce(… flEngHours(e.id) …) with e.baseHours||0 as fallback. Note it sums over all engines including retired, while the headline count excludes them — so avgHours divides by all.length, not by totalEng. (inferred: inconsistent, probably unintentional.)
  • Service due :23824 — dueEngs = engines with a serviceInterval where (curH % serviceInterval) / serviceInterval >= 0.8. This is a pure modulo against total hours; it is not the lastServiceHours-based countdown that flEngServiceState (:20808) uses. The two can disagree for an engine whose last service was not on an exact interval boundary.
  • Models — distinct e.model count plus the top 3 by frequency.
  • Issues — fixing count on a dark card.

Grouping & rows :23934

Engines are partitioned into three buckets: byBoat[boatId] (sorted by flPosRank), spareList (no boat), and retiredList (_isRet). Boat groups are sorted by boat name and each gets a header <div id="fl-eng-grp-<boatId>"> — that id is the scroll anchor. Then "Spare · ไม่อยู่เรือ" and "ปลดระวาง · Retired" groups follow, each sorted by serial.

buildEngRow(e) :23913 shows position badge (or SPARE), serial, status badge, location text (boat.name · pos or spareLocation||'Workshop'), current hours, and a service countdown svcLbl — next = ceil(curH/interval)*interval, left = next − curH, coloured red under 30 h, amber under 100 h, and rendered as over Nh when negative. Row background encodes status (fixing amber-ish, broken red-ish, off-boat cream).

Engine filter setters & the jump-to-boat navigator :23989

flSetEngBrand / flSetEngStatus / flSetEngLocation :23989–:23991 are one-liners: assign, re-render.

flSetEngBoat(v) :23992 is deliberately different — the comment on the state line spells it out: "remembered (for the dropdown's selected value across re-renders) · does NOT filter". Instead of filtering it:

  1. Stores v so the <select> keeps its selection across re-renders.
  2. Returns early on 'all'.
  3. Scrolls #fl-eng-listpanel to #fl-eng-grp-<boatId>, computing the offset from getBoundingClientRect() deltas minus the sticky header height (read from panel.firstElementChild.offsetHeight, falling back to 36) minus 6 px.
  4. Flashes the group header pink (#FCE5EC) for 700 ms so the landing point is obvious.
SCROLL DISCIPLINE This region shows the codebase's two approved answers to the "re-render scrolls the page to top" problem. flSetEngBoat scrolls the inner panel rather than the document. And flSelEng (which begins at :24003, immediately past this slice) opens by capturing lp.scrollTop and window.scrollY and calling document.activeElement.blur() before flRenderEngList(), with the explicit comment "was: scrollIntoView, which made the page jump". Any new full-list re-render here must do the same capture/blur/restore dance.
SLICE BOUNDARY The slice ends at the closing brace of flSetEngBoat. flSelEng(id) starts at :24003 and belongs to the next fragment.

Engine selection & detail page — flSelEng, flRenderEngDetailPink :24003

flSelEng(id) :24003 sets the module-level flSelEngId and re-renders the whole engine list. It does not use scrollIntoView — the comment says that made the page jump. Instead it snapshots #fl-eng-listpanel.scrollTop + window.scrollY, blurs the active element, re-renders, then restores twice (synchronously and again inside requestAnimationFrame).

SCROLL-JUMP PATTERN This blur-snapshot-restore-restore idiom is repeated verbatim in flSelGb :24453 and flSelProp :24924. It is the local workaround for the codebase-wide "replacing a mount's innerHTML while focus lives inside it scrolls to top" trap. If you add a fourth asset type, copy the idiom — do not just call the list renderer.

flRenderEngDetailPink() :24016 paints #fl-eng-detail-mount. It is a single ~230-line template-literal renderer with an empty state, then these sections in order:

SectionData readNotes
Header stripe.serial, e.brand/model/hp, e.pos, getBoat(e.boatId)Accent colour is brand-driven: Suzuki #A32D2D, else #185FA5; grey #999 when unattached. Edit button → flOpenEditEngModal.
Hours cardflEngServiceState(e) → {curH,next,left,pct}; FL_DAILY meter scanProgress bar colours at 60% / 80%. บันทึกเซอร์วิส button → flEngMarkService(e.id) resets the service baseline.

The engine-hours meter scan :24051 is the subtle part. It walks FL_DAILY[date][boatDay].trips[].engines[engId] to find the first and latest meter reading, and:

if(_inst && ds < _inst) return;                 // ignore readings before installDate
const _v = trip.engines?.[flSelEngId];
if(_v == null || +_v <= 0) return;              // skip 0 / non-positive placeholders
TRAP Both guards are load-bearing. Dropping the installDate guard makes an engine inherit the previous boat's meter history after a swap; dropping the <= 0 guard lets a single 0 placeholder Daily-Log entry become "first meter reading" and blow the running-hours total up. The displayed formula is สะสมเดิม baseHours + วิ่ง (latest − first) = curH.

flRenderEngDetail(id) :24250 is a legacy shim: it ignores id entirely and just calls the Pink renderer, which reads the module global flSelEngId. Same shape for flRenderGbDetail :24717 and flRenderPropDetail :25152. Calling flRenderEngDetail('someOtherId') silently renders the currently-selected engine, not the one you asked for.

flChangeEngStatus(id, newSt) :24254 flips e.status, appends a {type:'status'} log entry with the current hours, saves and re-renders the list. Note the detail page deliberately does not expose status buttons — the comment at :24145 reads "Status section removed — status changes via Edit button to prevent accidental clicks".

The drivetrain link model — 1 engine = 1 gearbox = 1 propeller :24016

Nothing in this slice defines the invariants in one place; they are enforced implicitly by how every renderer traverses the links. Read this before touching any of the three pages.

boat
boats[]
drive position lives here
→
engine
FL_ENGINES
boatId + pos
→
gearbox
FL_GEARBOXES
engineId (+ onBoatId/onBoatPos)
→
propeller
FL_PROPELLERS
gearboxId
  • The chain is one-directional and singular. A gearbox points at its engine (g.engineId); a propeller points at its gearbox (p.gearboxId). The engine page finds its gearbox with .find :24065; the propeller page walks p.gearboxId → gb.engineId → eng.boatId :24951. Two gearboxes claiming the same engineId means one is invisible forever — nothing in this slice detects that.
  • A spare part must be detached. "Spare" is not a status flag, it is the absence of a link: flRenderGbList classifies spares as !g.engineId && !g.onBoatId :24284, and the propeller list as !p.gearboxId :24729. A part with status:'spare' that still carries an engineId/gearboxId will be counted as installed, grouped under a boat, and accrue hours from an engine it is not on.
  • Hours are always derived from the engine. Gearbox current hours = flEngHours(eng.id) − g.baseHours, clamped at 0 :24289. Propeller current hours = flEngHours(eng.id) − p.installHours :24875. If the link is wrong, the hours are wrong — there is no independent meter for gearboxes or propellers.
  • Lifetime = previous + current. Both gearbox :24288 and propeller :24862 sum log[] entries of type:'remove' (each carrying usedHours) and add the current cycle. Removing an asset without writing a remove log entry with usedHours loses that service life permanently.

Drive positions · Port · C.Port · Center · C.Std · Std. The canonical maps live just before this slice — FL_POS_CANON / FL_POS_RANK :23794 — and are consumed here through flPosLabel(p) (label) and flPosRank(p) (sort order, unknown → 98).

// Suzuki "Starboard" / "Stbd" / "Starb"  ===  Honda "Std"  (same physical side)
FL_POS_RANK = {Port:0, 'C.Port':1, Center:2, 'C.Std':3, Std:4, Starboard:4, Stbd:4, Starb:4}
TRAP Both the gearbox list :24409 and the propeller list :24884 declare a local const POS_ORDER=['Port','C.Port','Center','C.Std','Std'] and then never use it — sorting actually goes through posIdx = p => flPosRank(p). Editing POS_ORDER changes nothing; edit FL_POS_RANK. Never compare raw position strings ('Starboard' !== 'Std') — always normalise through flPosLabel.

Engine swap: the engine moves, the gearbox and propeller stay on the boat. The model is visible in the gearbox row/detail code. A gearbox carries two independent location fields:

FieldMeaningRendered as
g.engineIdWhich engine it is bolted tobadge = g.shaftLength or GB, row shows engine serial · boat name
g.onBoatId + g.onBoatPosWhich boat + drive position it physically sits at, with no engineamber WAIT badge, text คาเรือ · รอเครื่อง ("still on the boat, waiting for an engine")
g.spareLocationOff-boat shelf locationgrey SPARE badge
1 · Engine comes off
The engine is the swappable unit. Its boatId/pos clear (or move to the new boat); spareLocation becomes the repair/store location.
2 · Gearbox + propeller stay put
The gearbox keeps onBoatId/onBoatPos and nulls engineId → renders คาเรือ · รอเครื่อง :24608. The propeller stays linked to that gearbox, so it follows the gearbox, not the engine.
3 · Incoming engine adopts them
Setting the new engine's boatId/pos and pointing the gearbox's engineId at it re-forms the chain; hours restart from g.baseHours / p.installHours against the new engine's meter.
DO NOT Null a gearbox's onBoatId when only the engine leaves. That turns a "waiting for engine" gearbox into a shelf spare, drops it out of its boat's group in the list, and loses the drive position it must be re-mated at. Conversely, a truly shelved gearbox must have both engineId and onBoatId nulled or it will keep appearing under a boat header.

Per-asset maintenance cost share — flMaintCostShare usage :24176

The function itself is defined outside this slice at :20888, but this slice is where every one of its call sites lives:

function flMaintCostShare(m, kind){
  const full = flMaintCalcCost(m.id);                  // whole job cost
  const key  = kind==='gb' ? 'gbId' : kind==='prop' ? 'propId' : 'engId';
  const n    = (m.assets||[]).filter(a => a[key]).length || 1;
  return full / n;                                     // job cost / same-type assets on the job
}
Call sitekindUsed for
Engine detail :24176 :24178 :24186'eng'Lifetime cost · YTD · Avg/job · per-row job cost
Gearbox detail :24672–:24682'gb'same three tiles + rows
Propeller detail :25108–:25118'prop'same three tiles + rows
RULE The division applies only on these three asset detail pages. A maintenance job that services 4 engines shows ฿X/4 on each engine page, but the job itself, the boat page, the project rollup (flProjCalcCost :27080) and the fleet dashboard all still count the full ฿X exactly once. Note the asymmetry: a job carrying both engId and gbId assets appears at its own full share on an engine page and on a gearbox page, so summing across kinds over-counts. That is by design.

Only m.status==='done' jobs feed the cost tiles; YTD filters on m.endDate.startsWith(currentYear).

Gearbox list — flRenderGbList and filters :24263

Renders into #fl-gbs-pink-wrap: header bar (Honda/Suzuki count pills + flOpenAddGearboxModal), a 5-column KPI strip, a filter bar, then a 380px list panel + detail mount grid. Ends by calling flRenderGbDetailPink().

  • Filter state is three module globals — flGbBrand, flGbStatus, flGbLocation — set by the one-liners flSetGbBrand/Status/Location :24450–:24452. The 'installed' filter is g.engineId || g.onBoatId — a "waiting for engine" gearbox counts as installed :24272.
  • KPI "Service due" = gearboxes with an engine whose flGbServiceState(g).pct >= 80 :24298. Detached gearboxes are excluded outright.
  • Spare split TL/VP is a substring test on spareLocation — .includes('tub') / .includes('panwa') :24306. Free-text location, not the pier enum; a location typed as "Tub Lamu Yard" counts, "TL" does not.
  • Grouping :24409: boat comes from engine.boatId, falling back to g.onBoatId; position from engine.pos falling back to g.onBoatPos. Boats sorted by name; the Spare · ไม่อยู่เรือ ("not on a boat") group is always last.

Gearbox detail — flRenderGbDetailPink :24462

Two hours cards, four spec cards, linked-engine block, propellers block, then history + maintenance side by side.

  • รอบถ่ายน้ำมันเกียร์ (gear-oil cycle) card reads flGbServiceState(g) → {since, interval, next, pct, defaulted, attached}. The interval is editable inline (flGbSetInterval(g.id, value)) and defaults to GB_DEF_SVC_INTERVAL = 200 h (defined :20840); when defaulted the UI appends (ค่าตั้งต้น). flGbMarkService(g.id) resets the cycle.
  • If !_gss.attached the card adds the note "เกียร์ยังไม่ติดเครื่อง · ชั่วโมงนับจากเครื่องที่ติดอยู่" — hours only accrue while bolted to an engine.
  • Linked engine :24591 is a 3-way branch: engine present → clickable card; else g.onBoatId → the amber คาเรือ · รอเครื่อง panel; else a plain "Storage" panel showing spareLocation.
  • Propellers :24626 uses FL_PROPELLERS.filter(p => p.gearboxId === flSelGbId) — plural filter, and the grid switches to 2 columns at 2+ props, so the data model tolerates more than one propeller per gearbox even though the operational invariant is one.
  • Maintenance match :24670 is looser than the engine page: a.gbId === id || (a.type==='gearbox' && a.label.includes(g.serial)). The label-substring branch can false-positive when one serial is a prefix of another.

Propeller list — flRenderPropList :24722

Same architecture as the gearbox list, into #fl-props-pink-wrap, with three differences worth knowing:

  • Brands are discovered from data, not hardcoded: brandCounts is built by scanning FL_PROPELLERS, the top two get header pills, and the filter bar renders one button per discovered brand :24730.
  • Default status is 'active' (not 'ready' as on engines/gearboxes) — the propeller STATUS_STYLE map keys are active|fixing|spare|broken :24842. Mixing the two vocabularies is the easiest bug here.
  • Boat grouping is a 3-hop walk prop → gearbox → engine → boat, with the same gb.onBoatId fallback :24885, so a propeller on a "waiting for engine" gearbox still sorts under the right boat and position.
  • matShort / matLong normalise free-text material: regex /stainless|ss/i → SS/Stainless, /alu/i → Alu/Aluminium, else raw.

Propeller detail — flRenderPropDetailPink :24933

Current cycle (flEngHours(eng) − p.installHours, "Counted from last installation"), lifetime hours, four spec cards (brand / size / blades / material), linked gearbox card, history and maintenance.

NOTE This renderer looks the propeller up with a raw FL_PROPELLERS.find(x => x.id === flSelPropId) :24950 instead of a flGetProp accessor (engines and gearboxes use flGetEng/flGetGb). If a propeller accessor with normalisation is ever added, this call site will be missed.

"Last install date" comes from the newest log[] entry with type:'install' :24866 — the installHours field and that log entry must be written together or the header date and the hour count disagree.

Memo modal — state, type/scope selection, item table :25159

Module state for the create/edit memo form:

GlobalLineMeaning
_memoType:25159'parts' | 'labor' | 'mixed'
_memoScope:25160'vessel' | 'general' — general = not tied to a boat
_memoEditId:25161set by flEditMemo; makes flSaveMemo update in place instead of creating

memoSelectScope(s) :25162 toggles the scope pills, shows/hides the general-category select, and — when scope is general with no MJ context — replaces the ref box with "🏢 Memo ทั่วไป (ไม่ผูกกับเรือ/เครื่อง)".

memoSelectType(t) :25179 has a side effect that surprises people: switching away from mixed rewrites every existing item's category to the new type. Switching parts → labor → parts silently reclassifies all lines.

memoRenderItems() :25198 rebuilds #memo-items-tbody from _memoItems and then calls memoCalcTotal(). Per row it shows:

  • A category select (mixed mode) or a fixed Parts/Labor pill.
  • 🔗 Inv when it.invId is set, or 🆕 Auto when it.autoRegister is set.
  • Price is locked when the item is linked to inventory — the input becomes a read-only div with a padlock. Comment: locking "forces wrong-part selection to surface as price mismatch".
  • price mismatch when |it.price − inv.cost| > 0.5 :25214 — i.e. a legacy line whose price drifted from the inventory cost.
  • Per-line discountPct input.
TRAP memoRenderItems resets _memoSelectedInv = null on every call :25201. Any code path that selects an inventory item and then re-renders the table before pushing the line loses the link — the item falls through to name matching in memoAddItem.

Two-level discount — memoCalcTotal :25241.

lineGross = qty * price
lineAfter = lineGross * (1 - item.discountPct/100)
subAfterLineDisc = sum(lineGross) - sum(lineGross - lineAfter)
memoDiscount = round(subAfterLineDisc * memoDiscPct/100) + memoDiscAmt
afterDisc = max(0, sub - (lineDiscSum + memoDiscount))
vat = round(afterDisc * vatRate/100 * 100)/100   // vatRate 0 when VAT unchecked
total = afterDisc + vat

Returns {sub, discTotal, afterDisc, vat, vatRate, vatEnabled, total} and paints #memo-sub/#memo-disc/#memo-vat/#memo-total. The memo-level discount is deliberately a second-level discount applied after per-item discounts.

memoAddItem() :25280 is the three-branch link resolver:

Picked from dropdown
_memoSelectedInv matches the typed name → push with invId, fromInventory:true, partNo, unit, and an inventorySnapshot{cost, primaryLocation, qtyAtSelection}.
Exact name already in inventory
Auto-link to the existing record (autoRegister:false). No snapshot is written in this branch.
Brand new name
autoRegister = !(_memoType==='labor') — labour lines never enter stock; parts/mixed lines are registered into FL_INVENTORY at save time.

Inventory autocomplete (comment: "Phase 1+2") :25340

memoSearchInv(query) :25342 fuzzy-ranks FL_INVENTORY into #memo-inv-dropdown, max 8 rows:

MatchScore
empty query (show recent)1
name.startsWith(q)100
name.includes(q)60
partNo.includes(q)40
supplier.includes(q)20

Ties break on stock quantity. Each row shows a stock badge (qty or หมด = out of stock), a location label mapped from pier:tublamu|panwa|ranong|central, supplier, part number and cost. A "เพิ่มใหม่" (add new) row is appended whenever the query has no exact name match.

QUOTE / ESCAPING HAZARD The create-new row interpolates the query into an inline handler with only q.replace(/'/g,"\\'") :25369, and the visible label uses the raw ${query} with no HTML escaping. A part name containing < or a double quote breaks the markup. The same unescaped pattern applies to inv.name, inv.supplier and inv.partNo in the matches list.

Supporting one-liners: memoSelectInv :25417 (fills name + cost, auto-fills an empty supplier field, focuses and selects the qty input), memoCreateNewFromQuery :25435, memoUnlinkInv :25444, memoUpdateInvPill :25449, memoCloseDropdown :25455.

Paste import — memoParseImport :25459

Splits #memo-paste-input by newline, each line by tab or comma, strips a leading "1. " numbering, and pushes {name, qty, price}. Price falls back to the second field if there is no third. Imported lines never get invId or autoRegister :25469 — they will not link to or create inventory records, unlike lines added through memoAddItem.

Copy-to-accounting dialog — moCopyText / moCopyPaint :25476

State: _moCopyId, _moCopyFmt ('tsv'|'name'|'txt'), _moCopyHead :25476. Entry point flCopyMemoItems(moId) :25498 creates a #mo-copy-host div on demand and paints into it; moCopyClose empties it.

FormatOutputIntended target
tsv "ตาราง"Tab-separated รายการ · รายละเอียด(blank) · จำนวน · หน่วย · ราคา/หน่วย; optional header rowPaste the whole block into Excel or a table-accepting form

moCopyName(it) :25478 is the accounting-form naming convention: "<partNo> <name>" when a part number exists, else just the name. The "รายละเอียด" column is intentionally emitted blank for staff to fill in.

moCopyDo() :25548 tries navigator.clipboard.writeText and falls back to document.execCommand('copy'), then to a "select it yourself" toast.

Printable memo — flPrintMemo :25556

Builds a complete standalone A4 HTML document as a string, wraps it in a Blob, and opens it via an object URL in a new tab (revoked after 5 s). Not a modal — a separate document.

  • Totals are read from the stored memo (mo.subtotal/discount/vat/amount) with recomputation only as a fallback :25562. vatEnabled defaults to true, vatRate defaults to 7.
  • Per-row VAT is computed independently per line (round(rowSub * vatRate/100)) so the column may not sum exactly to the document VAT.
  • Company block is hardcoded: บริษัท เลิฟ ไอแลนด์ จำกัด, TAX 0825554000447. Logo comes from the global LOVE_LOGO_B64.
  • Signature grid is 4 boxes: approval (from mo.to as the approver role, default กรรมการผู้จัดการ) · ผู้เสนอ · ผจก.บัญชี · ผอ.การเงิน. When mo.approvedBy is set the first box turns into a green "อนุมัติแล้ว" stamp with name, note and date.
ESCAPING Only mo.note is HTML-escaped in this document :25774. Item names, title, to/cc/from, boat name and approvedBy are interpolated raw.

Memo statuses — _memoStatusMeta :25803

statusThai labelChipEditable?
pending_approvalรออนุมัติamberyes (initial state on create)

Unknown statuses fall back to a grey chip with the raw key. The lock rule canMod = status!=='paid' && status!=='cancelled' is re-derived independently in flViewMemo :25911, moLiveStart :25817, moLiveSave :25883, flEditMemo :26014 and flCancelMemo :26044 — five copies, so adding a new terminal status means editing all five.

COST VISIBILITY Cost rollups only count memos in paid | received | approved (see flProjMJCostBreakdown :27090) — a pending_approval or ordered memo is invisible to project cost.

Live line-item editing — moLiveStart and the moLive* family :25815

This is an in-place editor grafted onto the read-only memo view: instead of opening the big memo form, it swaps the item table for inputs. State is two globals declared at :25814: _moLiveId (which memo is in live mode, null = none) and _moLiveItems (a shallow-cloned working copy).

moLiveStart(moId) :25815
Guards: memo must exist; status not paid/cancelled; window.laCanEditArea('fleet') must pass ("สิทธิ์ดูอย่างเดียว" = view-only). Then clones items with Object.assign({}, it) and re-renders via flViewMemo.
moLiveSet / moLiveDel / moLiveAdd :25824–:25830
Numeric fields are clamped at 0. moLiveAdd repeats memoAddItem's exact-name inventory link (but never autoRegister). Edits and deletes trigger a full flViewMemo repaint; moLiveSet only repaints totals.
moLiveTotals :25853
Same formula as memoCalcTotal but reads discount/VAT from the memo record, not the form DOM — the header, discount and VAT are deliberately untouched by live editing ("แก้เฉพาะรายการ · หัวเอกสาร ส่วนลด และ VAT ไม่ถูกแตะ").
moLiveSave :25881
Rejects an empty list, writes items/subtotal/discount/afterDiscount/vat/amount, pushes an {type:'edit'} history row describing the count and amount delta, mirrors to the MJ log via flPushLog(mo.maintId, …), flSave(), then re-renders MJ detail + memo list + the memo view.
LEGACY DISCOUNT TRAP — moLiveLegacyDisc :25847 Old memos stored only a lump discount amount with no discountPct/discountAmt. Recomputing naively would make that discount vanish silently. The helper reverse-engineers it: if both percentage fields are zero, it subtracts the per-line discount from the stored discount and carries the remainder forward as a fixed amount. Any new totals path over historic memos must call this or it will quietly wipe old discounts.

Memo view / edit / cancel — flViewMemo :25905

Renders into #memoview-body / #memoview-foot and opens modal fl-modal-memoview. It declares its own local const esc=… at the top :25906 — the codebase-wide rule that esc is not global. Two rendering modes selected by live = (_moLiveId === mo.id): read-only rows, or input rows plus a "add" row where Enter submits.

  • Header shows mo.no, title and the status chip; a 2-column meta grid (เรือ / วันที่ / เรียน / จาก / ผู้เสนอ / Supplier).
  • Cancelled memos get a red reason banner with cancelReason, cancelledBy, cancelledDate.
  • History renders mo.history[] reversed with icons for create / edit / cancel.
  • Footer (non-live): แก้ไข (flEditMemo) · ยกเลิก (flCancelMemo) — both only when canMod — plus คัดลอกรายการ, Print, ปิด.
  • Inventory-linked rows keep the price lock in live mode too.

flCloseMemoModal() :26005 clears _memoEditId and resets the modal title / save-button label back to "สร้าง Memo". Any other path that closes the form must do the same or the next "create" silently overwrites the last-edited memo.

flEditMemo(moId) :26011 exits live mode, refuses paid, then repopulates the whole big form from the record (including _memoMaintId / _memoProjectId context), clones mo.items into _memoItems, calls the three selectors + memoRenderItems, and relabels the modal to "แก้ไข Memo" / "บันทึกการแก้ไข".

flCancelMemo(moId) :26042 requires a non-empty prompt() reason, sets status cancelled + cancelReason/cancelledBy/cancelledDate, appends history, mirrors to the MJ log and re-renders MJ detail and/or the Projects page.

memoPopulateSupplierList() :26063 builds the #memo-supplier-suggestions datalist from the union of memo suppliers and inventory suppliers. memoAutofillSupplierFromItem :26076 only fills when the field is empty — never overrides typed input.

Data-integrity helpers ("Layer 2" / "Layer 4") :26086

flAssertUniqueNo(arr, no, type) :26086 — Layer 2. Before pushing a numbered record it looks for an existing .no; on collision it logs, alert()s and throws, deliberately aborting the save rather than silently overwriting.

flValidateDataIntegrity() :26098 — Layer 4, a post-load console audit over FL_MAINT (MJ-), FL_MEMOS (MO-) and FL_INCIDENTS (INC-). It warns on duplicate no, duplicate id, and numbers not matching the expected prefix. Gaps in the sequence are treated as normal (deleted records) and only logged at info level.

Creating / updating a memo — flSaveMemo :26139

Validate
Subject and date required (alert() + return).
Edit branch (_memoEditId)
Mutates the existing record in place, deliberately preserving id, no, status, approval fields and history. Builds a human diff (เรื่อง / ยอด / รายการ / supplier / หมายเหตุ), appends an edit history row, mirrors to the MJ log, resets the modal labels, and re-opens flViewMemo. A stale _memoEditId falls through to create.
Numbering
MO- + zero-padded max(existing numbers) + 1 — explicitly not length+1, to survive deletions and gaps :26191.
Build record
Includes maintId (the MJ link), projectId (project-level memo, no MJ in between), scope, generalCategory, all discount/VAT fields, status:'pending_approval', currentStep:1, and a create history entry. boatId is null for general scope, otherwise inherited from the MJ or the project.
Phase 3 · auto-register inventory
For every item with autoRegister && !invId: re-check for a same-name inventory record created meanwhile (link it), otherwise push a new FL_INVENTORY row with id: 'i_auto_'+Date.now()+…, qty:0, empty stocks[], createdFrom: memo.no, and a register history entry. Then flips autoRegister off.
Commit
flAssertUniqueNo → push → flPushLog on the MJ + a progressLog entry → project log if project-scoped → reset _memoProjectId → flSave() → close modal → re-render MJ detail / Projects / Inventory → toast.
TRAP The auto-registered inventory record has qty:0 and no stock rows — real stock only appears at Receive. Its cost is snapshotted from the memo line price, which then becomes the locked price on every future memo line linked to it.
SIDE EFFECT On create (but not on edit) the function may switch the Inventory page to the Memorandum tab via invSwitchTab('memo', …) and always calls flRenderInventory().

Project migrations, cleanups and resync :26371

Six functions, orchestrated by flProjMigrate(force) :26554, which flRenderProjects calls on every visit.

FunctionLineRunsWhat it does
flProjCleanupV2:26371one-shot, flag _fl_proj_cleanup_v2Unlinks MJs with parentProjectId whose type !== 'scheduled' — v1 mis-linked them. Logs on both sides.
flProjCleanupV3:26522one-shot, _fl_proj_cleanup_v3_openClears the old default "+30 days" planTo from auto-generated projects → Open. Skips anything a user edited (log text contains "Project edited" / starts with "Edit") and anything whose first log by is not migration/auto-create/resync. Only triggers on an exactly 30-day span.
flProjCleanupV4:26505one-shot, _fl_proj_cleanup_v4_baselineBackfills originalPlanTo (the schedule baseline) from the current planTo.
flProjResyncMJLinks:26471every visit, idempotentLinks unlinked scheduled MJs to an existing non-completed project on the same boat with overlapping dates.
flProjAutoCreateForScheduledMJs:26400every visitFor still-unlinked scheduled MJs, creates a fresh project from the MJ (or links to an overlapping one). Status maps done→completed, inprogress→inprogress, else planned.
flProjMigrate:26554one-shot core, flag _fl_proj_migrated_v1; force=true re-runsScans every boat's log[] for reason==='dry_dock' or s==='unavailable' lasting 14+ days, creates drydock/overhaul projects, tags the boat log entry with entry.projectId so it is never re-migrated, then links overlapping scheduled MJs.
PERSISTENCE TRAP flProjCleanupV2/V3/V4 and flProjMigrate all do JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(LS_KEY)), mutate, then localStorage.setItem(LS_KEY, JSON.stringify(d)) directly :26394 :26517 :26548 :26643 — bypassing flSave(). Per the project's storage rules the blob is a RAM shim, so this works today, but it also means these functions overwrite the whole blob from their own snapshot: anything changed between the read and the write in the same tick is lost. They also mirror d.boats, d.fleet_projects, d.fleet_maintenance back by hand, so a new sibling key added to the projects data will be dropped unless it is added here too.
AUTO-MUTATION flProjResyncMJLinks and flProjAutoCreateForScheduledMJs run on every Projects page render and can create records and rewrite parentProjectId without user action. They are guarded (scheduled type only, skip already-linked, skip completed projects) but this is the loosest auto-heal in the fleet module — do not widen the criteria casually.

flProjGuessEnd(startStr) :26651 now simply return null — "Default = Open (no fixed end)". This is the counterpart to Cleanup v3; the old behaviour was start + 30 days.

Phases, health score and rule-based insight :26657

PROJ_PHASES :26657 — per-type phase ladders:

typephases
drydockPlanning → Lift-out → Hull/Paint → Mechanical → Sea Trial → Handover
overhaulPlanning → Disassembly → Install → Bench Test → Sea Trial → Handover
refitPlanning → Strip-out → Rebuild → Finishing → Handover
scheduledPlanning → Service → Test → Handover
otherPlanning → Execute → Verify → Handover

flProjPhasesFor :26664 falls back to other. flProjCurrentPhaseIdx :26665: completed → last phase, cancelled → -1, explicit p.phase → its index, else inprogress ? 1 : 0. flProjSetPhase :26676 logs the transition and re-renders.

flProjHealth(p) :26690 returns {key,label,color,bg,score,budgetPct,schedOver,openRatio}. Scoring starts at 100 and subtracts:

budgetPct > 100                 -> -min(40, (budgetPct-100) * 1.5)
budgetPct 80..100               -> -5
days past planTo (schedOver)    -> -min(35, schedOver * 2)
timePct>80 and >half MJs open   -> -15
status === 'on_hold'            -> -10
buckets: <40 CRITICAL · <70 AT RISK · else ON TRACK

Completed → fixed 100 / COMPLETED, cancelled → 0 / CANCELLED. Open-ended projects (no planTo) get no schedule penalty at all — a project can sit open forever and stay "ON TRACK".

flProjInsight(p) :26964 — explicitly "Rule-based AI Insight (no real AI, just smart rules)". Returns {lines, recs} covering over-budget, >85% budget used, missing end date, past end date, behind schedule (time elapsed vs MJs done), on-hold, and fewer than half of documents verified. Falls back to "Project running smoothly".

Year-over-year analysis :26727

flProjYoYStats(yearTY) :26727 buckets projects into a year by actualTo || planFrom || createdAt and computes, for TY and LY: count, completed, cancelled, totalSpend, avgCost, avgDuration (days, completed only), onTimePct, avgSlip, per-type counts and a 12-element monthly spend array.

BASELINE RULE On-time and slip are measured against originalPlanTo || planTo :26753 — the immutable baseline captured at creation (backfilled by Cleanup v4), not the current, editable planTo. Pushing a deadline out therefore does not improve the on-time metric. Negative slip = finished early.

flProjRenderSlipTile :26786 is a dedicated KPI tile because slip has inverted polarity (lower is better): green when negative, red when positive, and the delta reads "N d better" / "N d worse".

flProjOpenYoy :26814 / flProjYoySetYear :26819 / flProjRenderYoyModal :26824 paint #fl-proj-yoy-body in modal fl-modal-proj-yoy: a 3x2 KPI grid, a hand-built SVG paired-bar monthly-spend chart (720x180, TY blue vs LY grey), and a per-type comparison table. The year dropdown is built from all years present in FL_PROJECTS.

The generic delta(ty, ly, betterUp) helper :26833 takes a betterUp flag so "spend" and "duration" colour-invert correctly; ly===0 renders the label new.

Vendor visits, photos, lightbox :27017

  • flProjAddVendorVisit :27017 — two prompt()s (vendor, role), pushes into p.vendorVisits[] with a generated id and today's date, plus a project log line. flProjDeleteVendorVisit :27034 confirms then filters.
  • flProjAddPhoto :27044 — photos are not a separate array: they are stored in p.docs[] with type:'photo', a phase tag and status:'received'. That matters because flProjInsight's document-verification rule counts p.docs — every added photo lowers the "verified documents" ratio.
  • flProjLightbox(url, caption) :27065 — appends a click-to-dismiss fullscreen overlay to document.body. url and caption are interpolated unescaped.

Project cost rollups :27077

  • flProjChildMJs(projId) :27077 — FL_MAINT.filter(m => m.parentProjectId === projId).
  • flProjCalcCost(projId) :27080 — sum of flMaintCalcCost(m.id) over child MJs. It does not include project-level (overhead) memos, unlike the breakdown below — the two numbers can legitimately differ.
  • flProjMJCostBreakdown(mjId) :27085 — {parts, labour, memos, total}. Parts = direct m.parts[] (qty*cost) plus memo items categorised parts; labour = memo items categorised labor/labour; memos is the legacy/mixed bucket for uncategorised items and for memos with zero items (the whole mo.amount lands there).
  • flProjCostBreakdown(projId) :27108 — sums the child MJ breakdowns and then adds memos linked directly by mo.projectId (project overhead, no MJ in between).
  • flProjGetById :27130, flProjBoatName :27131 (null boat → "General · ไม่เกี่ยวกับเรือ").
TRAP Both breakdown functions only count memos with status paid | received | approved, and they compute from it.qty * (it.price || it.cost) — ignoring per-item and memo-level discounts and VAT. The number therefore does not match mo.amount for any discounted memo. The zero-item shortcut is the only path that uses mo.amount.

Boat avatar helpers :27134

BOAT_AVATAR_PALETTE (10 colours) :27134 + flBoatAvatarColor :27135 — colour is the boat's index in the filtered company fleet (ownership!=='charter' && !retired) modulo 10, deliberately matching Boat Status. Adding, retiring or reordering a boat re-colours every boat after it.

flBoatInitials(name) :27143 — two words → first letters; a name containing a digit → first uppercase letter + last character; else first two characters. flBoatMeta(boat) :27150 builds "Speedboat · 65 PAX · 4 EN", counting engines from FL_ENGINES and falling back to boat.engineCount.

Projects render entry point and List view :27162

flRenderProjects() :27162 is the single entry point: run flProjMigrate(false), find #fl-proj-mount, then branch to flProjRenderDetail(_selProjId) (defined past this slice) or flProjRenderList().

flProjRenderList() :27174 assembles, in order:

  1. Greeting hero — time-of-day greeting, today's date, a "Live Data" pill, and an "AI Project Insight" card. Its Health Score is not flProjHealth: it is (inprogress + completed) / total * 100 :27230 — a completely different, much cruder number sharing the same label. The keyInsight line picks the first true of: 3+ boats off-line · any on-hold · more than 3 planned · "running smoothly".
  2. View-mode switch (List / Timeline / Gantt), a "Re-scan logs" button that calls flProjMigrate(true), and "Quick Create" → flProjOpenNew().
  3. 6 KPI tiles — Total / Scheduled / In Progress / On Hold / Completed / YTD Spend. YTD spend counts only completed projects whose actualFrom||planFrom starts with the current year.
  4. Status tab strip — planned (labelled "Scheduled") · inprogress · on_hold · completed · cancelled, driving _projTab.
  5. Body — Timeline or Gantt when selected; otherwise hub panels + tab strip + the sorted card list (newest planFrom first) via flProjRowCard (defined past this slice, :27897).
TRAP Hub panels render only in list mode :27285. Every one of these buttons calls flRenderProjects(), which re-enters flProjMigrate — so a simple tab click can create projects as a side effect.

Hub dashboard panels — flProjRenderHubPanels :27311

A 4-column grid of cards computed from all projects:

At-Risk Projects

Active projects (planned/inprogress/on_hold) scored with flProjHealth, keeping critical + atrisk, worst score first, max 5. Empty state: "All active projects on track".

Upcoming Deadlines

Active projects with a planTo inside the next 30 days, soonest first, max 5. Urgency colour: 3 days or less red, 7 or less amber. Open-ended projects never appear.

Recent Activity

Flattens every project's log[] into one feed sorted by date string descending, last 8. Log text is interpolated unescaped :27411.

Spend Trend

Hand-built SVG bar chart, last 6 calendar months, only completed projects matched by (actualTo||planTo).startsWith('YYYY-MM'), values via flProjCalcCost.

DATE HANDLING All of this compares YYYY-MM-DD strings with localeCompare/startsWith and derives "today" from new Date(TODAY_STR). Day maths is (a-b)/86400000. Keep to that convention — do not introduce toISOString().slice(0,10) here (UTC shift at +07:00).

Timeline view — flProjRenderTimeline / flProjRenderTimelineCard :27462

flProjRenderTimeline(allProjects) :27462 filters to planned | inprogress | on_hold only — "completed/cancelled go to Gantt history" — sorts by planFrom ascending and maps each to a card.

flProjRenderTimelineCard(p) :27475 is a 140px side panel + body grid:

  • Side panel tone is chosen by status: inprogress orange (label flips to OVERDUE when today > planTo), on_hold amber, planned indigo, else green.
  • Due text: Open when planTo is null, else "N Past" / "Today" / "N Due".
  • Shows done/total child MJs and "Complete by" = p.planTo || 'Open'.
  • Three sub-tabs — GENERAL / MORE INFO / FINANCIALS — persisted per project in _projTimelineCardTabs, dispatching to flProjRenderTimelineGeneral (:27565, past this slice), flProjRenderTimelineInfo (:27637) and flProjRenderTimelineFin (:27689).
  • Action icons: camera / clipboard / arrow → flProjOpen(p.id), pencil → flProjOpenEdit(p.id), plus a vendor-visit count badge.
TRAP Every interactive element inside the card calls event.stopPropagation() before its handler :27502 :27510. The card container is not itself clickable today, but the guards exist because a card-level click handler is expected (inferred) — omit them on a new control and it will fight any future wrapper handler.

E Lines 27565–31464 · Fleet Projects, Maintenance list, Cost Analytics, Fuel Intelligence & Fleet Insights

This slice is four consecutive Fleet-Management pages plus the shared cost model that two of them read from. In reading order: the Projects module (timeline card bodies, Gantt, row card, detail page, plan/docs/memos tabs, lifecycle actions, create/edit modal, MJ↔project linking), the Maintenance job list (flRenderMaint + its selection/filter state), the Cost Analytics page (the §costV2 aggregation model, the §dw detail drawer, and the page renderer), Fuel Intelligence, and Fleet Insights. The slice ends one line before _flMaintTimelineInner.

STYLE Practically every function here returns a template-literal HTML string with fully inline styles — there is no external CSS for these pages except the one <style> block injected inside renderFuelIntel :30157. Colours come from three ad-hoc palette objects that are re-declared in each renderer: PROJ_PALETTE (Projects, defined earlier in the file), dim + SVG_PINK/PK (Maintenance/Cost/Fuel), and FOREST/LIME (Insights). Changing a colour means grepping for the hex, not editing a variable.

esc Per CLAUDE.md, esc/escapeHTML is not global. Most functions in this slice interpolate raw values with no escaping at all (all of the Projects module, all of flRenderMaint, all of flRenderInsights). Only the Cost Analytics block defensively declares const esc=(typeof escapeHTML==='function')?escapeHTML:(x=>String(x||'')) :29438 :29584. A boat name or memo title containing < or a quote will break markup or an inline onclick in the unescaped renderers.
TZ Every date computation in the Projects timeline/Gantt uses date.toISOString().slice(0,10) :27577 :27586 :27591 :27762 — exactly the UTC-shift bug CLAUDE.md warns about (bkV2LocalYMD is the correct helper). At +07:00 a date built from a local new Date(y,m,d) can render as the previous day. The Insights time filter has the same pattern :30219–:30226. Treat any "off by one day" report in these views as this bug.

Projects · Timeline card "GENERAL" body :27565

flProjRenderTimelineGeneral(p, kids) is one of the three tab bodies of the project Timeline card. The card wrapper flProjRenderTimelineCard lives just before this slice and dispatches at :27517–:27519:

if(cardTab==='general')   body = flProjRenderTimelineGeneral(p, kids);
else if(cardTab==='info') body = flProjRenderTimelineInfo(p, boat);
else                      body = flProjRenderTimelineFin(p, kids);

It renders a horizontally scrolling strip of day columns starting at TODAY_STR and running _projTimelineDays days forward (declared at :26305, default 14). Each column lists the child MJs active on that day.

  • Active-on-day test :27576: ms <= ds && me >= ds where ms = m.startDate and me = m.endDate || p.planTo || ds. An MJ with no endDate on a project with no planTo falls back to "today" — so an open-ended MJ appears on every column, not just today. That fallback chain is easy to misread.
  • Overdue count :27584: non-done MJs whose endDate || p.planTo is before today. Rendered as a red "N DUE" pill whose onclick just re-selects the project and re-renders — it does not filter to the overdue jobs.
  • Each MJ chip navigates by setting the global _selMaintId then calling nav(document.querySelector('[data-view=\'fl-maintenance\']')) :27598. Note the escaped single quotes inside the inline attribute — a fragile but repeated idiom throughout this slice.
  • MJ status colour: done→mint, inprogress→amber, everything else→blue :27597.
  • Footer has "+ Add MJ" → flProjCreateMJ(p.id).

Projects · Timeline card "MORE INFO" and "FINANCIALS" bodies :27637

flProjRenderTimelineInfo(p, boat) :27637 — a static facts panel: budget-approved badge (green if p.plannedBudget truthy, amber "No budget set" otherwise), boat name, vendor, current phase from flProjPhasesFor(p) + flProjCurrentPhaseIdx(p) (both defined at :26664), created-by avatar, a hardcoded "Operations" project lead, and up to 4 vendor-visit avatars from p.vendorVisits[].

flProjRenderTimelineFin(p, kids) :27689 — the money panel. Reads flProjCostBreakdown(p.id) (defined :27108) for parts/labour/memos, and filters FL_MEMOS to memos whose maintId matches a child MJ.

TRAP The Paid/Due split here :27692–:27695 counts only memos reachable via a child MJ — it ignores project-level memos (mo.projectId === p.id), which are counted by flProjRenderMemosTab :28177 and by the detail page. So the Timeline-card Financials tab and the detail page's Memos card can legitimately disagree. Also the "Add note" button :27716 has no onclick — it is decoration.

Projects · Gantt view :27735

flProjRenderGantt(allProjects) builds a month-column Gantt grouped by boat. Called from the projects page dispatcher at :27300 when _projViewMode==='gantt'.

  • Window = 1 month back to Math.max(_projTimelineMonths, 6) months forward :27738–:27741. The ± buttons mutate _projTimelineMonths in 3-month steps, but the "3m back" label is hardcoded to 1 month (monthsBack is a const) — only the forward edge actually moves.
  • Only projects with both planFrom and planTo are drawn :27758. A project with an open end date (planTo: null, which flProjSaveModal explicitly allows) silently vanishes from the Gantt. This is the single most common "my project disappeared" report.
  • Rows are keyed by p.boatId; boatId === null (General / ไม่เกี่ยวกับเรือ, "not boat-related") groups into a grey "General" row :27818–:27823.
  • Bar colours by p.status via a local barStyle() :27774 — a second status→colour map that duplicates flProjStatusPillStyle :27888 with different hexes. Adding a status means editing both.
  • Today marker is absolutely positioned with left:calc(200px + (100% - 200px) * ${todayPct}/100) :27851 — the 200px is the frozen boat-name column width, repeated in three places in this function.

Projects · Lifecycle actions and the completion cascade :28621

Six state transitions. All of them push to p.log[] and call flSave(); the three that touch boat status also call the app-level save() because BOATS lives in the main blob, not the fleet blob.

planned
Scheduled
created by flProjSaveModal
→
flProjStart
inprogress
boat → unavailable
→
flProjHold / Resume
on_hold ⇄ inprogress
boat log untouched
→
flProjMarkComplete
completed
cascade-close MJs · boat → available

flProjStart(id) :28621

  • Guard: only from planned. Sets actualFrom if unset.
  • Pushes a boat status-log entry :28632: {id:'sl_'+Date.now(), s:'unavailable', from:TODAY_STR, to:null, loc:p.vendor, note:'Project '+p.no+' · '+p.name, reason: p.type==='drydock'?'dry_dock':'overhaul', projectId:p.id}. The projectId field is the join key the cancel/complete handlers use to find and close this entry again.
DANGER flProjStart does const boat = BOATS.find(b=>b.id===p.boatId) with no null check on p.boatId. For a General (non-vessel) project — where boatId is deliberately null :28866 — boat is undefined, the if(boat) guard skips the log push, and the project's own log still claims "boat status → unavailable" and the toast still says "boat removed from operation". Cosmetic today, but any code that trusts that log line will be wrong.
SEMANTICS The status pushed is 'unavailable', not 'fixing'. Per CLAUDE.md the Boat Status page's "In Shop" tab keys on last.s==='fixing' — so a boat in an active drydock project shows as unavailable at its pier, not in the In Shop group. Do not "fix" this without checking the Boat Status grouping first.

flProjHold :28649 / flProjResume :28664

Hold requires inprogress and a non-empty prompt() reason (null = user cancelled → abort; empty string → alert('Reason required')). Sets holdReason + holdSince. Resume clears both to null but keeps the history in p.log. Neither touches the boat log — a held project still holds the boat as unavailable, which is deliberate.

flProjCancel(id) :28677

Guard
Completed projects cannot be cancelled; already-cancelled is a no-op toast.
Reason prompt
Required, non-empty.
Unlink open MJs?
If there are open child MJs, a confirm(): OK = set m.parentProjectId=null on each and log "Unlinked from cancelled project"; Cancel = keep them linked to a cancelled project.
Commit
status='cancelled', cancelReason, cancelledOn. If it was inprogress or on_hold: close every open boat-log entry with e.projectId===p.id (set to=TODAY_STR, append " · project cancelled" to the note) and push a fresh available entry, then save().
TRAP Choosing "keep linked" leaves open MJs pointing at a cancelled project. Those MJs still contribute to flProjCalcCost and to the project's Financials — a cancelled project can keep accruing spend.

flProjReopen(id) :28727

Cancelled → planned, clears cancelReason/cancelledOn. It does not re-link the MJs that were unlinked at cancel time, and does not restore the boat to unavailable — the user must press Start again.

flProjMarkComplete(id) :28740 — the cascade

This is the destructive one. Confirm text spells out the cascade; then:

openKids.forEach(m => {
  m.status = 'done';
  m.endDate = m.endDate || TODAY_STR;
  m.progressLog.push({date:TODAY_STR,
    text:'✓ Auto-closed by parent project '+p.no+' mark-complete', by:'cascade'});
});
p.status='completed'; p.actualTo = p.actualTo || TODAY_STR;
// close boat-log entries where e.projectId===p.id && !e.to, then push {s:'available'}
flSave(); save();
  • Irreversible from the UI. There is no "reopen completed" path — the button set for completed is Edit only :28017. Editing cannot change status either (flProjSaveModal never writes p.status). Undoing a mis-click requires console surgery.
  • The cascade writes by:'cascade' in the MJ progress log — that string is the forensic marker for auto-closed jobs.
  • Cascade-closing an MJ does not reset any engine service counters, close its memos, or clear awaitingInvoice. A cascade-closed MJ with awaitingInvoice set immediately shows up in the maintenance "Awaiting Invoice" tab :29051 and in the Insights "Awaiting Invoice" recommendation :30870.
  • Same null-boat issue as flProjStart: for a General project the boat block is skipped but the log/toast still claim the boat returned.

Projects · Create MJ from a project :28784

_projCreateForId :28784 + flProjCreateMJ(projId) :28785. Opens the generic create-job modal and then pokes DOM fields directly: forces the type card to scheduled via selectJobType('scheduled', card) (deliberately going through the real handler "so all wiring fires"), then sets #job-boat, #job-start-date, #job-location (= p.vendor), #job-title (= "${p.name} · sub-job"), and finally calls populateJobAssetList() because the asset list depends on the boat.

TRAP This is prefill-by-DOM-id. Renaming any of those five element ids, or changing the job-type card's data-type attribute, silently breaks the prefill with no error. Note also that _projCreateForId is set here but the actual parent-link is applied by the job-commit code elsewhere — this variable is the only channel carrying "which project asked for this MJ".

Projects · New/Edit modal :28810

flProjOpenNew :28810 / flProjOpenEdit :28811 both delegate to flProjOpenModal(editId) :28812, which repopulates the boat <select> (with a sentinel __none__ option = "⚙ General · ไม่เกี่ยวกับเรือ") and fills the form, then openModal('fl-modal-project').

flProjSaveModal() :28862 validates and writes.

Validation
name required · a boat or the General sentinel must be chosen (empty string is rejected) · planFrom required · planTo optional but if present must be ≥ planFrom.
boatId mapping
boatId = (val==='__none__') ? null : val :28866. null is a meaningful value here, not "unset".
Edit branch
Mutates in place. Backfills originalPlanTo for older records only when it is undefined. Logs boat-change and planTo-change.
New branch
id = proj_${yr}_${boatId||'gen'}_${seq}_${rand}, no = PRJ-${seq} where seq = (FL_PROJECTS.length+1) zero-padded. Seeds status:'planned', originalPlanTo = the creation planTo, empty actuals, one log line. Then switches the page to the planned tab.
BASELINE originalPlanTo is the on-time KPI baseline and must never be edited :28885 :28904. The edit modal shows it as a read-only hint (#fl-proj-to-baseline) and the save path only ever writes it when it is undefined. Any new code that touches planTo must leave originalPlanTo alone or the on-time metric becomes self-fulfilling.
TRAP no is derived from FL_PROJECTS.length+1 :28899. Delete a project and the next created one reuses an existing PRJ- number. The random suffix keeps id unique, but no is not guaranteed unique — and no is what the UI shows everywhere.

MJ ↔ Project linking :28924

flMaintLinkProjectPick(mjId) :28924 — a prompt()-driven numbered picker (no modal). Two hard gates:

  • Only type==='scheduled' MJs can be linked :28927; corrective/preventive jobs are "independent". This mirrors flProjCreateMJ forcing the scheduled type.
  • Candidates are filtered to p.boatId===m.boatId && p.status!=='completed' :28931. Consequence: a General project (boatId null) can never be linked from this picker unless the MJ also has a null boatId. Project-level overhead therefore has to arrive as a direct project memo, not via an MJ.

flMaintUnlinkProject(mjId) :28947 — confirm, null m.parentProjectId, write a 🔗 line into both proj.log and m.progressLog, then flRenderMaintDetailPink(). Both functions re-render the MJ detail, not the projects page.

Maintenance · job list page :28965

Module state on one line :28965:

let _selMaintId=null, _maintStFilter='all', _maintArchiveTab='active',

flRenderMaint() :28967 paints #fl-maint-pink-wrap: a header bar of type-count chips, a 5-cell KPI strip, then a 340px job list + a detail mount, and finally calls flRenderMaintDetailPink() for the right pane.

KPI strip maths :28972–:28995

  • YTD spend counts only status==='done' jobs whose startDate year matches :28979, but avgCost divides that YTD figure by cntDone — the all-time done count :28980. The "avg ฿x/job" number is therefore understated whenever there are done jobs from previous years. (Bug, not a rounding artefact.)
  • avgDaysOpen averages today - startDate across inprogress jobs, skipping jobs with no startDate in the numerator but still counting them in the denominator :28992.
  • Costs come from flMaintCalcCost(m.id) (guarded by typeof checks throughout).

The three tabs :29046–:29057

TabPredicate
Activestatus === 'pending' || 'inprogress'
Awaiting Invoicestatus==='done' && m.awaitingInvoice and not yet settled
Archivestatus === 'done' (all of them, including the awaiting ones)
§awaitingInvoice The auto-clear rule :29051–:29055 is precise and commented in Thai (ต้องติ๊ก "รอใบวางบิล" ตอนปิดงานเท่านั้น — "only if the 'awaiting bill' box was ticked at close"): a job leaves this tab only when it has ≥1 linked memo and every one of them is paid. A merely received memo still means we are waiting for the invoice. A job with zero memos stays in the tab forever until someone unticks the flag. The Awaiting tab appears in the tab bar only when non-empty.
  • Filters compose in order: tab → status (_maintStFilter) → type → boat, then sort by startDate desc :29066–:29070. The sub-status filter set changes per tab :29147.
  • Type and boat filter counts are computed from baseJobs = the current tab before type/boat filtering :29157, so the chip counts stay stable while you click.
  • The boat filter chips carry a per-boat cost roll-up (flMaintCalcCost summed) :29171.
  • Pinning: only in the Active tab, pinned jobs are hoisted into a pink block sorted by pinnedAt desc :29222. The 📌 toggle (flMaintTogglePin, defined elsewhere) is only rendered for non-done jobs :29115.
  • The list panel is max-height:calc(100vh - 360px) with its own scroll :29243 — the 360 is hardcoded and will drift if the KPI strip changes height.

flSelMaint and the maintenance-selection state :29266

function flSelMaint(id){ _selMaintId=id; flRenderMaint(); }

That is the whole thing — selecting a job re-renders the entire page, list panel included, not just the detail mount. Consequences a developer must know:

  • Scroll jump. Because flRenderMaint replaces wrap.innerHTML wholesale, clicking a job in a long scrolled list rebuilds the list DOM and the browser loses the scroll position — the exact failure mode CLAUDE.md describes. The correct fix is the surgical-update pattern (re-render only #fl-maint-detail-mount), which is precisely why _flMaintTimelineInner :31465 was factored out for the progress log.
  • _selMaintId is a cross-module handoff variable: the Projects timeline :27598, the project detail MJ list :28157, and costDwGoJob :29507 all set it directly and then nav() to the maintenance view rather than calling flSelMaint. If you add side effects to flSelMaint, those three callers will not get them.

The remaining setters are all "assign then full re-render":
