§costV2 · the cost-category model :29287

Cost Analytics page state starts at :29283: _costTimeFilter ('all' | 'ytd' | 'month' | 'last30') and costSetFilter(v).

The comment at :29287 states the model: หมวดของค่าใช้จ่าย · อิงจากอุปกรณ์ที่ใบงานไปแตะ (assets[].type) — "expense category, derived from the equipment the job touches (assets[].type)". Categories are not a field on the job; they are inferred from what the job's asset list points at.

var COST_CATS = [
  {k:'hull',      t:'เรือ',        c:'#5B7CB3'},
  {k:'engine',    t:'เครื่องยนต์', c:'#E03B7E'},
  {k:'gearbox',   t:'เกียร์',      c:'#BA7517'},
  {k:'propeller', t:'ใบจักร',      c:'#1D9E75'},
  {k:'other',     t:'อื่น ๆ',      c:'#9AA0A6'},
  {k:'memo',      t:'memo ตรง',   c:'#8B5CF6'}   // §memoToBoat
];

costCat(k) :29297 looks a key up and falls back to COST_CATS[4] = other — a positional index, so reordering or inserting into COST_CATS silently changes the fallback category. If you add a category, append it after memo, or fix the fallback to a .find(x=>x.k==='other').

MODEL hull, engine, gearbox, propeller, other are all maintenance-job categories. memo is different in kind: it is the §memoToBoat bucket, money that never passed through a job at all. Mixing them in one array is what lets the six category cards share a renderer, but it also means A.byCat.memo is populated by a completely different code path from the other five.

§memoToBoat · boat money that never touched a job :29294 :29299

A memo (FL_MEMOS entry) can name a boat directly via mo.boatId without being linked to a maintenance job (mo.maintId empty). That money is real spend on that boat and must appear in the boat's total — but naively adding it double-counts, because a memo line that enters inventory will be charged again later when the part is issued to a job.

laMemoDirectShare(mo) :29302 computes what fraction of a memo is safe to attribute directly. The Thai comment :29299–:29301 says it exactly: "the part of the memo that did not go into stock, so it can be counted as a boat expense right away · an item with invId went into the warehouse and will be counted again when it is issued to a job, so it must be excluded · no line items at all (e.g. a pure-labour memo) = the whole memo counts."

function laMemoDirectShare(mo){
  var items=(mo&&mo.items)||[];
  if(!items.length) return 1;                       // no lines → whole memo is direct
  var line=function(i){ var q=(i.qty==null?1:+i.qty)||0,
        p=+((i.price!=null)?i.price:i.cost)||0;
        return (i.amount!=null? (+i.amount||0) : q*p); };
  var sub=0, direct=0;
  items.forEach(function(i){ var v=line(i); sub+=v; if(!i.invId) direct+=v; });
  if(sub<=0) return items.every(function(i){ return !i.invId; }) ? 1 : 0;
  return Math.max(0, Math.min(1, direct/sub));
}
DOUBLE-COUNT The whole anti-double-counting contract lives in the single test !i.invId. An item that has an invId is stock and is deliberately excluded here so it can be counted once, later, as job parts cost. If someone adds a new "goes to warehouse" flag without setting invId, or starts writing invId onto lines that are consumed immediately, every boat total on the Cost page shifts — with no error and no visual signal. The three amount shapes (amount, or qty×price, or qty×cost) are also load-bearing: a new line shape that uses a different field name evaluates to 0 and skews the ratio.
EDGE sub<=0 (all lines zero-valued) returns 1 if no line has an invId, else 0 — an all-or-nothing decision, not a proportion. A ฿0-priced memo with one stock line therefore contributes nothing to the boat.

costAggregate() · the single source of the whole page :29314

The §costV2 comment above it :29312 states the intent: "put every number on the page in one place · every panel reads from this one blob, so there is no way for the top cards and the bottom table to compute differently and disagree." Do not add a panel that computes its own totals — extend A instead.

Scope

  • Counted memo statuses: CNT = ['paid','received','approved'] :29315. Anything else (created / pending_approval / rejected / cancelled) is invisible to this page.
  • §total pool :29322: jobs with status ∈ {done, inprogress, on_hold}. Cancelled jobs are excluded. Note this is a wider pool than the maintenance page's YTD-spend figure, which is done-only — the two pages will not match, by design.
  • Date of a job = m.endDate || m.startDate || '' :29325; the time filter compares that string.

Per-job build :29335

var as   = (m.assets||[]);
var keys = as.length ? as.map(a => costCat(a.type).k) : ['other'];
var share = c / keys.length;              // cost split evenly per touched asset
  • A job with no assets lands entirely in other.
  • A job touching 3 engines splits its cost into 3 equal shares — all into engine.
  • row.parts = Σ(qty×cost) over m.parts minus flMaintPartsCoveredByMemo(m) :29348 — again to avoid counting a part twice when a memo already paid for it. row.memo = Σ amounts of counted memos linked to this job.
§count :29360: "a job touching several assets in the same category must count as 1 job, not N". The money still divides per asset (so totals are unchanged) but the job counter A.byCat[k].n is guarded by a seenCat set. If you refactor this loop, keep money-per-asset and count-per-category separate or the "N jobs" labels inflate.

Unit roll-up :29368

For each asset in the job, uid = a.engId || a.gbId || a.propId. Assets without one of those three ids (i.e. hull work) are skipped, so A.units only ever contains engines, gearboxes and propellers. Labels come from costUnitLabel :29419, which prefers the job's own asset.label and otherwise looks up serial · pos in FL_ENGINES/FL_GEARBOXES/FL_PROPELLERS, falling back to the raw id.

§memoToBoat pass :29377

Filter
Skip memos with a maintId (those were already counted through the job). Skip statuses outside CNT.
Split
share = mo.boatId ? laMemoDirectShare(mo) : 0; amt = round(amtAll*share); rest = amtAll-amt.
Route the remainder
If rest > 0.5 or the memo names no boat, push {mo, amt: boatId?rest:amtAll} onto _cenLeft — the §cen "central / into-stock" bucket.
Attribute the direct part
If the memo names a boat, has amt>0 and is in the time scope, synthesise a row with cats:['memo'], kind:'memo', done = (status==='paid'), and add it to A.rows, A.done/A.proc, A.byCat.memo and the boat totals.
SPLIT MEMO A single memo can appear in two places at once: its non-stock portion inside a boat's total, and its stock portion inside §cen. That is intentional and is why A.central.amtOf[mo.id] exists — the §cen table prints the partial amount with a "เต็มใบ ฿X" (full memo value) sub-line whenever the two differ :29570. If you ever "simplify" this by showing mo.amount in the §cen table, you re-introduce the double count on screen.
SCOPE ASYMMETRY The memo date used for the time filter is mo.createdDate || mo.approvedDate :29383, whereas jobs are filtered by endDate||startDate. Also, _cenLeft is built before the inScope check is applied :29390–:29391 — so the §cen "central cost" card is effectively all-time regardless of the selected period, while everything else respects the filter. Anyone reconciling the two cards will hit this.

Output shape

FieldMeaning
A.done / A.proc / A.totalclosed vs in-progress money; total = done+proc. §cen is not included.
A.nDone / A.nProc / A.nJobsrow counts (memo rows count as rows too)
A.byCat[k]{done, proc, n} per COST_CATS key
A.byType[t]{done, proc, n} for corrective/preventive/scheduled — jobs only, memo rows never land here
A.boats[id] / A.boatList{id,name,done,proc,n,cats{},tot}, sorted desc by tot
A.units / A.unitListper engine/gearbox/propeller, top 10 by cost
A.rows / A.alldrawer rows. They differ: A.all also receives rows built with intoRows=false, and only A.all feeds the 12-month chart :29669.
A.central{n, amt, list, amtOf} — the §cen bucket, excluded from A.total

§dw · the cost detail drawer :29430

_costDw :29431 holds {rows, cat, title, sub, kpis}. costDwOpen(title, sub, kpis, rows) :29432 lazily creates #cost-dw-ov (overlay) and #cost-dw (panel) on document.body if absent, builds a header + a category tab strip containing only the categories actually present in rows, then calls costDwPaint() and adds the on class to both.

  • costDwTab(k, el) :29451 — sets the filter and moves the on class across sibling buttons manually.
  • costDwClose() :29457 — removes the classes only; the nodes stay in the DOM and are reused.
  • costDwPaint() :29461 — filters by cat, sorts by cost desc then date desc, renders a Thai-headed 8-column table (ใบงาน / memo · วันที่ · เรื่อง · หมวด · สถานะ · อะไหล่ · memo · รวม) with a totals <tfoot>. Row click dispatches on r.kind: memo rows → costDwGoMemo(r.no), job rows → costDwGoJob(r.id).
  • Global Escape handler registered at :29577 — a document-level listener added at script-eval time, so it is always live even when the drawer is closed.

Navigation out of the drawer — §memoToBoat again :29498: "a row that came from a memo has no job to open · take the user to the memo page and search by document number".

costDwGoMemo(no) :29499

Close drawer → set the global _memoSearch to the memo number → nav() to fl-inventory → invSwitchTab('memo') → flRenderInventory(). Every step is typeof-guarded, so a rename fails silently rather than throwing.

costDwGoJob(id) :29507

Close drawer → set _selMaintId → nav() to fl-maintenance. Bypasses flSelMaint.

costOpenBoat(id) and the per-boat view :29530

var A=costAggregate(), b=A.boats[id]; if(!b) return;
var rs=A.rows.filter(function(r){ return r.boatId===id; });
costDwOpen(b.name, 'ค่าใช้จ่ายซ่อมบำรุงทั้งหมด · '+b.n+' ใบงาน',
  [['รวมทั้งหมด',f(b.tot)], ['ปิดงานแล้ว',f(b.done)],
   ['กำลังดำเนินการ',f(b.proc),'#A05A1A'],

TRAP b.n is not the number of rows shown. b.n is incremented once per job and once per attributed memo :29375 :29408, but the subtitle labels it "ใบงาน" (work orders). Also, A.total in the "% of fleet" KPI excludes §cen, so the boat percentages add to 100% of attributable spend, not of all money spent. And b.tot does include that boat's direct-memo money, so this figure legitimately exceeds the sum of the boat's maintenance jobs.

costOpenCentral() :29547

Opens the drawer with an empty rows array, then hides the category tab strip (tabs.style.display='none') and replaces #cost-dw-tbl's innerHTML with a bespoke 6-column memo table. The amount column prints A.central.amtOf[mo.id] and, when it differs from mo.amount, a grey "เต็มใบ ฿X" sub-line showing the full memo value — the visual tell for a memo split between a boat and the central bucket.

NOTE Because it mutates the drawer DOM after opening, calling costOpenCentral() and then any other costOpen* works only because costDwOpen rebuilds dw.innerHTML from scratch each time — which also re-creates the tab strip it hid. Don't switch costDwOpen to an incremental update without handling this.

flRenderCostAnalytics() · the page :29579

Paints #fl-cost-wrap. Reads A = costAggregate() exactly once :29591 and derives every panel from it. Layout, in order:

  1. Header + time filter — All time / YTD / Last 30 days / This month :29596.
  2. §total card :29623 — big A.total, a two-segment done/proc bar, and two clickable legend boxes → costOpenStatus. PROC='#E0B24B' :29584 is the single "in progress" colour for the whole page.
  3. §cen card :29644 — "ค่าใช้จ่ายกลาง / ซื้อเข้าคลัง" (central cost / stock purchase), explicitly labelled "ไม่รวมในยอดซ้าย" (not included in the total on the left) and carrying an amber explainer box that restates the §memoToBoat rule for the operator. → costOpenCentral().
  4. §cat cards :29660 — six cards from COST_CATS × A.byCat, each with a done/proc split bar. → costOpenCat(k). The grid is repeat(6, …) hardcoded :29826 — adding a 7th category needs that number changed too.
  5. 12-month bar chart :29666 — buckets A.all by date.slice(0,7). Rows outside the last 12 months are simply dropped, so the chart total ≠ the card total whenever a wide time filter is active.
  6. §boat panel :29742 — top 12 boats, segmented by category, → costOpenBoat.
  7. §unit panel :29770 — top 10 assets, → costOpenUnit.
  8. Type panel :29788 — corrective/preventive/scheduled from A.byType (jobs only).
  9. Outcome panel :29808 — done rows only; re-reads FL_MAINT to get m.outcome (success / limited / rework / decommission / cancelled, defaulting to success). Memo rows have no matching FL_MAINT entry so they fall into the success default — a small but real skew.
  10. §dupAudit bar :29796–:29820 — wrapped in try/catch that swallows everything into dupBar=''. Calls laDupScan() (:19509) and offers costDupInspect(k) / costDupClean(k) / costDupCleanAll() (:19599, :19666, :19674). Findings split into "ซ้ำแน่นอน" (certain) and "น่าสงสัย" (suspicious — the system can't decide, look yourself), plus a "numbers reused across documents" list. The rationale comment is the key one: "duplicate data inflates every figure on this page with no signal at all."
TRAP costDupClean keeps the record with the most content and moves memos / issued parts onto it. It is a destructive data mutation reachable from a dashboard button. Per CLAUDE.md's "keep data fixes user-triggered" rule this is acceptable because a human clicks it — but never wire it into a render path or a self-heal.

Fuel Intelligence · state and budget storage :29867

  • _fuelMonth :29868 — YYYY-MM, seeded from TODAY_STR with a hardcoded '2026-06-15' fallback.
  • _fuelExpand{} :29869 + fuelToggleBoat(bid) :29870 — per-boat route breakdown expand. Note it captures and restores window.scrollY around the full re-render — the manual workaround for the scroll-jump problem, applied here and in fuelWkMetric :29909 but nowhere else in this slice.
  • fuelMonthShift(n) :29871 — month stepper built with new Date(y, m-1+n, 1), which is safe.
PERSISTENCE _fuelBudgetGet() :29872 and fuelSetBudget(val) :29873 do a raw JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(LS_KEY)) / localStorage.setItem(LS_KEY, JSON.stringify(d)) read-modify-write on the fleet_fuelbudget key. Per CLAUDE.md the loveandaman_v2 key is shimmed over an in-RAM _mem, so this does not hit disk — but it is a business record written through the generic blob path rather than a dedicated persist helper, and it will only survive if fleet_fuelbudget has a mapper/REST-index entry. If monthly fuel budgets vanish on refresh, this is why. It does at least read-modify-write, so it will not clobber sibling keys.

Fuel · the two aggregators :29874

_fuelAgg(month) :29874

Walks FL_DAILY[date][boatId] for the month and builds {fuel, cost, pax, tripDays, byBoat, byFam, missing, anomalies, weekly, priceMissing}.

  • Exclusions :29879: charter-owned and retired boats are skipped entirely.
  • §nil — "didn't sail" ≠ 0. A boat-day only counts if info.pax>0 || routes.length || fuel>0 :29883. This is the single most important rule on the page: a boat that stayed at the pier is never averaged in as a zero.
  • Price resolution :29886: per-boat price (flFuelPriceForBoat) first, then the pier default (flFuelPrice(ds, pier)). A missing price yields cost 0 and sets priceMissing, which surfaces as a "⚠ บางวันยังไม่ใส่ราคาน้ำมัน" warning :30129.
  • Engine hours per day :29888: for each engine on the boat, delta = todayMeter - flPrevMeter(engId, ds), and the day's runH is the max delta across engines (not the sum) — i.e. hours the boat ran, not engine-hours consumed.
  • Route apportioning :29894: fuel/cost split across the day's routes by pax share (info.routes[rid]/info.pax), or evenly by 1/nr when pax is 0. Fuel with no route at all is bucketed under _none = "ไม่ระบุเส้นทาง".
  • Anomalies :29900: needs ≥3 fuelled days for that boat; any day above avg*1.3 is flagged. Note the dead loop at :29899 that computes avg and discards it.
  • Revenue by route family :29902 is a separate pass over SB_BOOKINGS, excluding ['cancelled','rejected','cancelled_weather'] per the standard rule, using acctBookingTotal(bk) and splitting evenly across a booking's trips when t.subtotal is absent.

§fuelWk · _fuelWkAgg(month) :29910

The comment :29905–:29907 is explicit about the duplication: "per-day logic identical to _fuelAgg (price · engine hours · the 'actually sailed' condition) · deliberately does not reuse _fuelAgg because that one loops all of SB_BOOKINGS for revenue, which is not needed here, and the monthly table calls back 6 months."

INVARIANT These two functions must stay in lockstep. Change the price lookup, the engine-hour rule, or the "actually sailed" test in one and the weekly/monthly tables will disagree with the per-boat table on the same page. The duplication is a deliberate performance trade, not an oversight.
  • Week index :29929: Math.min(4, ceil(dayOfMonth/7)-1) → W1…W5, days 29–31 fold into W5.
  • _fuelWkMetric :29908: 0=L/hr · 1=L/day · 2=L/pax · 3=฿; fuelWkMetric(i) :29909 switches it.
  • _fuelWkVal(x,i) :29936 returns null, not 0, when the metric is uncomputable (no engine hours, no pax, no days). The comment says it: "null = can't be computed, which is different from 0." Every consumer must keep that distinction — fmtM renders null as —.

renderFuelIntel() :29943

Paints #fl-fuel-wrap. Thai-language UI throughout, Buddhist-era years (yy+543). Early-outs with a "no data this month" panel when a.tripDays===0 :29971.

Sections in order: month card + 4 KPIs (cost, ฿/pax with MoM delta, anomaly count, missing-fuel days + % logged) → per-boat table (expandable to per-route rows) → L-per-engine-hour efficiency bars → route-family cost-vs-revenue + weekly cost bars → §fuelWk weekly table → 6-month table → anomaly strip → missing-fuel list.

  • Projection :29959: proj = cost/elapsed*daysInMonth, where elapsed is today's day-of-month for the current month and the full month otherwise. Compared against the optional budget.
  • §คน/ลำ column :29979 — average pax per sailing day, coloured against boat.cap (≥80% green, ≥50% amber, else red). The comment explains it was added because the table had L/pax but never showed pax, so you couldn't tell a full boat from an empty one.
  • §self tone rule :30040: each boat is compared to its own previous-month baseline, not the fleet. ≤0.95× green, <1.15× amber, else red.
  • §baht: when the metric is ฿ the tone function returns transparent — money scales with days sailed, not efficiency, so colouring it would mislead :30040 :30091.
  • n=1 badge :30053 — a cell computed from a single day is badged; the footer states you need ≥3 days before reading a trend.
  • "ไม่ออก" (didn't sail) vs a faded — for weeks that haven't happened yet in the current month :30047.
  • The month-label card carries a long design-rationale comment :30115: the old small grey ‹ ก.ค. 2569 › stepper blended into the background and people read the big ฿ figure without noticing which month it was — hence the pink card with the full month name and "data through day N".
  • The page's CSS lives in an inline <style> inside the same innerHTML :30157 — all rules scoped under #fl-fuel-wrap, re-injected on every render.
  • THM_prev(ms) :30183 — tiny helper returning the short Thai month name from a YYYY-MM string. Defined after its uses inside renderFuelIntel; fine because of function hoisting, but easy to miss when grepping.
NOTE Dead code in this function: dot :30006, PAL/ci :30007, effMed's "worst" flag only firing for the last element :30030.

flRenderInsights() · the Fleet Insights page :30185

The largest single function in the slice (~1,270 lines, :30185–:31462). Paints #fl-insights-wrap. Everything is computed inline in one scope; there is no aggregate object like costAggregate, so this page's numbers are independently derived and can legitimately disagree with the Cost page.

Period + aggregation :30217

  • inPeriod(d) is a string comparison against a startDate built from toISOString() (see the TZ callout at the top).
  • Fleet = BOATS.filter(b => b.ownership!=='charter' && !b.retired) :30245 — same exclusion as Fuel.
  • Boat status uses getCurStatus(b, TODAY_STR).s ∈ available|fixing|unavailable :30246.
  • boatStats[id] accumulates {jobs, jobsActive, cost, incidents, curStatus} from periodJobs and periodIncs :30263.
HEURISTIC getHealth(s) :30285 is a hardcoded three-way rule with magic thresholds: jobs>=3 || cost>=100000 → Critical; jobs>=1 → Watch; else Healthy. This drives the Critical/Watch/Healthy pill row, the 3-card scorecard, and the "healthy fleet" wins card. Any complaint that "a boat is wrongly critical" traces to these two numbers. Note a ฿0-cost boat with 3 trivial jobs is Critical.
GLOBAL _insightsSort is stored on window and initialised with if(typeof _insightsSort === 'undefined') window._insightsSort = 'cost' :30300. The sort buttons write window._insightsSort directly in their onclick. It is also only used for the flat boatRows list — the 3-card scorecard that is actually rendered uses its own per-status sort (:30436: Critical→cost desc, Watch→jobs desc, Healthy→name asc). So the sort buttons defined at :30412 have no visible effect on the scorecard. boatRows[0] is still used for the "High alert" KPI and the cost-concentration insight, so changing the default sort does change those.

extractSupplier(mo) — 3-tier fallback :30673

  1. mo.supplier if set.
  2. Majority supplier across mo.items[].invId → FL_INVENTORY[].supplier.
  3. Regex on mo.refNote: split on ·, look for a segment matching a company-word list (Marine|Mall|Co. Ltd|Suzuki|Honda|Yamaha|Megazip|บจ.|บริษัท|Hardware|Trading|…), strip a leading อ้างอิง and any trailing document number; else the first segment that isn't a date/doc-number and is >3 chars. Final fallback 'Other'.
TRAP Tier 3 is legacy string archaeology and will mis-attribute freely — a new supplier whose name contains none of the listed words falls into whatever segment happens to be first. The "Cost by supplier" chart is indicative, not accounting. Its own inclusion rule is separate again: APPROVED_STATUSES = ['approved','ordered','received','paid'] :30736 filtered by mo.createdDate — note this is a different status set from the Cost page's CNT (which omits ordered) and a different date field. The two pages will not reconcile.

The insights array :30867–:31157

A list of plain objects {icon, color, bg, title, subtitle, detail, stat, statLabel, meta:{type,…}, category, metaLine, ctaLink, priority?}, pushed by nine independent detector blocks:

DetectorLineRule
Awaiting Invoice:30869done + awaitingInvoice + zero linked memos (stricter than the maintenance tab, which also keeps unpaid-memo jobs)
Engine service upcoming:30915hoursUntil = interval - (flEngHours(e.id) % interval), flagged within 20%
Boat docs expiring:30956b.docs[].exp: <0d expired, ≤14d critical, ≤60d warning
Avg time to close:31008needs ≥3 closed jobs; compares in-period vs out-of-period average duration

Recurring incidents:31061any boat with ≥2 incidents in period (one card per boat)
Long-running job:31079inprogress ≥14 days (one card per job)
Cost concentration:31100top boat >50% of period spend
Pending memos / Healthy fleet / All-clear:31118fallback positives
INCONSISTENCY Two different engine-service formulas coexist in this one function. The Upcoming card :30806 uses flEngServiceState(e).left and explicitly comments that it is "relative to the last logged service (oil change), not pure modulo" — it can go negative (overdue). The Upcoming insight :30919 uses raw flEngHours(e.id) % e.serviceInterval and requires hoursUntil > 0, so it can never report an overdue engine and will disagree with the card above it. Per CLAUDE.md the service cycle is a countdown against lastServiceHours — the modulo version is the wrong one.
STATUS ENUM The insights code checks m.status==='pending_approval' for memos :30286 :31036, while the projects memo tab and the cost model use 'pending' :28541 :29315. Verify which value real FL_MEMOS rows carry before touching either — one of these two sets is matching nothing.

Boundary · the maintenance progress timeline :31464

The slice ends on the comment line :31464:

// Progress-timeline inner HTML (shared by full render + surgical refresh on withdraw/remove)

_flMaintTimelineInner(m) itself begins at :31465, one line past this slice. What the comment tells you matters here, though: it is the extracted inner-render function that exists precisely so the withdraw/remove flows can update just the timeline sub-region instead of re-rendering the whole MJ detail — the surgical-update remedy for the scroll-jump problem described in CLAUDE.md, and the pattern that flRenderMaint/flSelMaint :29266 in this slice notably does not follow.

From the first lines visible past the boundary: it reverses m.progressLog, renders a dot-and-line timeline (newest dot pink #E03B7E, older dots green #1D9E75), and falls back to "No progress logged". DATA STORES TOUCHED Everything here is read/written on the in-RAM fleet arrays FL_MAINT, FL_INCIDENTS, FL_ENGINES, FL_GEARBOXES, FL_PROPELLERS, FL_INVENTORY, FL_MEMOS, FL_PROJECTS and BOATS, then persisted with flSave(). There is no local esc() anywhere in this slice — every renderer interpolates raw record fields straight into template literals, so a quote or < in a serial/name/note will break the markup (a real trap when adding new fields).
§TAGS IN THIS SLICE §mjLateParts (late parts edit on a closed job), §costDoubleCount (job cost was double-counted parts + memo), §dupNoId (history rows now carry an id), §memoTable (memo list rebuilt as a real table), §memoRecvWh (receive-warehouse defaulting). Each is surfaced in its own region below.

MJ detail renderer + late-parts edit mode :31465

_flMaintTimelineInner(m) :31465 and _flMaintPartsInner(m) :31496 are the two inner HTML builders that are deliberately extracted so they can be re-rendered surgically without redrawing the whole detail pane. Timeline renders m.progressLog newest-first; parts renders m.parts sorted by date with a date-divider row, plus a per-line "ย้อนหลัง" (backdated) chip when p.late.

§mjLateParts — editing parts on an already-closed job :31483

  • var _flMaintLateEdit :31484 — module-level string holding one job id that is currently in retro-edit mode. Switching jobs does not clear it automatically; only flMaintLateEditOff() does.
  • flMaintCanEditParts(m) :31485 — the single gate: m.status!=='done' || _flMaintLateEdit===m.id. Both the +Withdraw form and the per-row × remove button hang off it.
  • flMaintLateEditOn(jobId) :31486 — hard confirm() warning that withdrawing now will change the cost of a closed job and deduct real warehouse stock, and that every line will be flagged as retroactive. Then re-renders the detail.

flRenderMaintDetailPink() :31555 is the ~300-line detail pane, mounted into #fl-maint-detail-mount and driven by the module-level selection _selMaintId. Sections in order: header strip → 4 stat cards → linked-incident banner → description → parent project → boat status → affected assets → (timeline | parts) two-column grid → linked memos → delete.

Header buttonsPin shows only while not done; ▶ Start only when pending; ✓ Close only when inprogress. There is no UI path from done back to open.

Service resetOn a closed job the "+ Add asset" row is replaced by ↻ รีเซ็ตรอบเซอร์วิส (flMaintServiceResetManual, defined outside this slice) — but only if the title/detail matches an oil/service regex.
Memo buttoncanAddMemo = m.status!=='done' || m.awaitingInvoice. A closed job flagged awaitingInvoice keeps a distinct amber "+ Add Memo (awaiting invoice)" button.
§costDoubleCount UI At :31529 the parts subtotal appends an amber explainer whenever flMaintPartsCoveredByMemo(m) > 0: "this ฿X was bought through this job's memo, already paid, so it is not counted twice." Without it the job total legitimately does not equal Subtotal parts + memo and readers assume a bug.

Surgical refresh & scroll-jump defence :31539

flMaintRefreshParts(jobId) :31539 replaces only #maint-parts-list-<id>, #maint-timeline-body-<id> and the count badge, then resets the picker inputs. It blurs first if focus sits inside a region about to be replaced. This is the reason the +Withdraw button survives a withdraw and the page does not jump to top.

_flWithDetailScroll(fn) :31866 is the heavier fallback: walks up from the mount to find the nearest scrollable ancestor, blurs the active element, runs fn, then restores both scrollTop and window.scrollY three times — sync, in a requestAnimationFrame, and in a setTimeout(...,0).

TRAP If you add a new mutation that calls the full flRenderMaintDetail(), wrap it in _flWithDetailScroll or add a surgical path — otherwise you reintroduce the scroll-to-top bug these two helpers exist to fix.

flRenderMaintDetail(id) :31859 is a thin alias that just calls flRenderMaintDetailPink() and ignores its argument; the real selection comes from _selMaintId.

Auto-add engines & split one job per engine :31887

  • flMaintAutoAddEngines(jobId) :31887 — collects the boat's engines not yet on the job. ≥2 to add → opens the choice modal (flOpenEngChoiceModal); exactly 1 → adds silently.
  • flOpenEngChoiceModal(jobId,n) :31920 / flChooseEngMode(mode) :31935 — stashes the job id on window._flEngChoiceJobId, then dispatches to flEngAddAllToJob ('single') or flEngSplitIntoJobs ('split').
  • flEngAddAllToJob(jobId) :31902 — pushes one asset per engine with status:'fixing'.
  • flEngSplitIntoJobs(jobId) :31944 — first engine reuses the open job (only if it has no engine yet), the rest become cloned MJs, all keeping parentProjectId, incidentId, boatStatus and setFixing.
  • flSplitExistingJob(jobId) :31998 — splits a job that already holds ≥2 engines. Non-engine assets (gearbox/propeller/hull) all stay on the first job. Title base is recomputed by stripping any previous " — suffix".
MJ NUMBERING New job numbers come from Math.max(...) over parsed MJ-### — never length+1 — precisely so deleted jobs / gaps don't produce duplicates. Every insert path then calls flAssertUniqueNo(FL_MAINT, jobNo, 'MJ'). Copy both when you add a new creation path.

Stash a gearbox / propeller as stored Spare :32052

FL_STASH_LOCS :32052 is the 6-entry location enum used by both the stash modal and the start-job gear modal:

pier:tublamu · pier:panwa · pier:central
shop:honda-phuket · shop:suzuki-phuket · shop:prop-shop-phuket

_flStashPartRecord(rec,isGb,locKey,locLabel,srcNo,note) :32061 is the shared mutation: sets status='spare', spareLocation=locKey, and detaches one link level — a gearbox nulls engineId, a propeller nulls gearboxId — then appends a remove log entry. flMaintStashOpen :32070 / flMaintStashConfirm :32084 wrap it, remove the asset row from the job and log on the job.

NOTE "ถอดเก็บ" (stash) is explicitly not a repair — the part goes to a warehouse, not a shop, and it leaves the maintenance job entirely.

Add asset by prompt · incident→job choice modal :32109

  • flMaintAddAssetPrompt(jobId) :32109 — a chain of native prompt() dialogs (e/g/p/h, then a numbered pick list). Crude but the only generic add path. Propellers are found indirectly, via their gearbox's boatId.
  • flCreateMaintFromInc(incId) :32144 — navigates to the maintenance view then opens the create-job modal after a 100 ms timeout.
  • flOpenJobChoiceModal(inc) :32152 — asks single-job vs one-job-per-damaged-asset, previewing the incident's damagedAssets.
  • flChooseJobMode(mode) :32176 — three lines, but load-bearing. It writes window._flJobMode = 'single'|'split', closes the modal, and re-calls flSaveCreateJob(). The save function is written to be re-entrant: on the first pass it sees ≥2 damaged assets and no _flJobMode and bails out into the modal; on the second pass the flag is set, it consumes and immediately nulls it. Forgetting to reset _flJobMode would silently force the same mode forever.

Progress log fan-out & pin :32184

  • flMaintAddLog(jobId) :32184 — reads text/by/date inputs. If the chosen date ≠ TODAY_STR it stamps entry.createdAt = TODAY_STR, which is what renders the grey backdated chip in the incident timeline.
  • flPushLog(mjId,text,by,date) :32203 — mirrors a job log line onto the linked incident, found by either inc.maintId===mjId or inc.id===mj.incidentId. Called from ~a dozen places; it is silently a no-op when there is no incident.
  • flMaintTogglePin(id,event) :32216 — sets pinned/pinnedAt, re-renders list and (if selected) the detail.

Start a job · the gearbox/propeller & engine-swap decision :32235

_flMountedGearProp(m) :32235 collects the still-mounted (non-spare) gearboxes and propellers hanging off the job's engine assets, de-duplicated.

flMaintStart(id) :32250 is the guard: if the job has engine assets and those engines carry gearbox/propeller, it opens the three-way modal instead of starting directly. Otherwise it goes straight to _flMaintStartProceed.

flStartGearOpen :32257
Stage 1 of #fl-modal-start-gear. Context parked on window._flStartGear = {jobId, parts, boatId}.
Choice A/B — flStartGearChoice('keep'|'stash') :32270
'keep' leaves the parts in place; 'stash' runs _flStashPartRecord on each into the chosen FL_STASH_LOCS entry. Both then proceed to the normal start.
Choice C — flStartGearSwap :32293
SWAP path. Per engine: gearbox gets onBoatId/onBoatPos set and engineId=null ("คาเรือ รอเครื่อง" = stays on the boat, waiting for an engine); the outgoing engine gets boatId=null; status='fixing'. Builds ctx.swaps[].
flStartSwapRenderPicker :32315
Stage 2, one outstanding position at a time. Candidates = engines not in this swap, not already on this boat, not broken; spares sort before donors. Also offers a repair/store location for the pulled engine.
flStartSwapInstall :32348
Sheds the incoming engine's OWN gearbox (stays on its source boat, or goes spare), installs the engine at boatId+pos, then the waiting gearbox at that position adopts the new engine (onBoatId/onBoatPos cleared). Propeller follows the gearbox implicitly.
flStartSwapSkip :32385 / flStartSwapFinish :32393
Skip marks the remaining swaps filled='skipped' but still records the repair location. Finish either just refreshes (re-entry) or runs the real Start.
HOUR METER Explicit comment at :32368: the meter travels with the engine and is continuous, so a swap needs no hour freeze or baseline reset — flEngHours = baseHours + (latest − first meter reading) works across boats by itself.

flMaintSetRepairLoc(jobId) :32403 writes m.location and cascades it to e.spareLocation for every engine on the job that is off-boat or fixing. flMaintSwapEngine(jobId,idx) :32416 is the re-entry path — swap an engine from an already-started job — it sets reentry:true and onlyEngId and jumps straight into flStartGearSwap().

_flMaintStartProceed — the actual start transition :32443

Sets m.status='inprogress', then decides the boat's status from m.boatStatus (legacy fallback setFixing).

const _ranToday = _flBoatRanOn(b.id, TODAY_STR);
const _fixFrom  = _ranToday ? _flDayAfter(TODAY_STR) : TODAY_STR;
  • _flDayAfter(ds) :32434 — builds the next day via new Date(ds+'T12:00:00') and manual Y-M-D assembly, deliberately avoiding the toISOString() UTC shift.
  • _flBoatRanOn(boatId,ds) :32436 — true if Boat-Op TRIPS[ds][boatId].route exists or a non-cancelled SB_BOOKINGS booking has that boat via bkOpsRead(bk,ds) on that date. Excluded statuses: cancelled, rejected, cancelled_weather.
INVARIANT If the boat already operated today (ran in the morning, fault reported in the evening), the not-available period starts tomorrow so today still counts as an operating day. Any new code that pushes a boat status log from maintenance must reuse this rule or it will retroactively erase a day of operation.

Asset status only cascades to 'fixing' when the target boat status is fixing (cascadeAssetFixing); otherwise engines/gearboxes/propellers only get a service-start log line. Engine log entries capture hours: flEngHours(eng.id) at start.

Closing a maintenance job — the biggest cascade in the slice :32510

Three functions plus one module state object:

  • flMaintOpenCloseModal(id) :32510 — resets _maintCloseCtx={jobId,outcome:'success'}, clears the note, clears the awaiting-invoice checkbox, resets the outcome pills to the first.
  • let _maintCloseCtx :32524 / flMaintCloseSelectOutcome(val) :32525 — pill picker writing into that context.
  • flMaintCloseConfirm() :32530 — reads note + awaitInvoice, closes the modal, delegates to flMaintClose(jobId,outcome,note,awaitInvoice). No validation and no confirm — closing is irreversible from the UI.

Job statuses are a flat three-value set on m.status, with a separate m.outcome recorded only at close:

m.statusMeaningWhat the UI allows
pendingCreated, not started▶ Start · edit parts · add/remove assets · delete job
inprogressStarted; boat log entry open✓ Close · swap engine · stash parts · edit parts · delete job
doneClosedRead-only, except §mjLateParts retro-edit and the service-cycle reset button

Outcomes (OUTCOME map :32550) drive the status each asset type lands in:

outcomeenginegearboxpropellerboat
success ✓readyreadyactiveavailable
limited ⚠limitedlimitedlimitedavailable
rework ↻fixingfixingfixingfixing
decommission ✕brokenbrokenbrokenavailable
cancelled ⊘null (fixing→ready)nullnullavailable
1 · Stamp the job :32542
status='done', endDate=TODAY_STR, outcome, optional closeNote, optional awaitingInvoice. totalCost = flMaintCalcCost(id).
2 · Decide the boat's status :32561
Three branches. (a) m.setFixing===false → job ran in parallel with operation, so do not touch the boat; keep cur.s and skip the log entirely if unchanged. (b) Other MJs still inprogress on this boat → carry over the strictest remaining status (unavailable > fixing > available) and name the job it came from. (c) Otherwise use out.boatStatus. autoClosePrevLog(b,TODAY_STR) runs first to close any open log row.
3 · Append boat repair history :32617
b.repairHistory.push({date,jobNo,title,type,location,cost,assets[],startDate,endDate,outcome,closeNote}). Always written, even when the status log was skipped.
4 · Apply outcome to each asset :32627
Engine/gearbox/propeller each get a log line typed by outcome (service-cancelled / rework / repair|install) and their status from the table above. A gearbox closed success/limited also gets installHours re-baselined to the linked engine's current hours.
4b · decommission side-effect :32639
Engine gets retired/retiredDate/retiredReason, and every gearbox attached to it is detached (gb.engineId=null) with a log line; attached propellers get an informational note only. Rationale in the Thai comment: gearbox/propeller have their own jobs and must not be dragged down by a dead engine.
5 · Auto-close the linked incident :32689
Only when m.incidentId is set and outcome ≠ cancelled. If every MJ linked to that incident is done (this one counted as done), the incident flips to status='closed' + closedDate + a log line.
6 · Service-hour reset hook :32708
flMaintServiceReset(m, outcome) — defined outside this slice — is offered here for oil-change/service jobs so the countdown restarts against the engine's current hours.
7 · Persist & re-render :32710
flSave(); flRenderMaint(); renderBoats(); flRenderEngList?.().
EXPLICIT NON-CASCADE Trailing comment at :32712: decommission retires only the parts on this job. It never prompts to retire the whole boat — that is a separate manual action (flRetireBoat) from the boat detail page. Do not "helpfully" add that prompt back.
TRAP flUpdateMaint(id,'done') :33001 calls flMaintClose(id) with no outcome, note or awaitInvoice, so it silently defaults to success and bypasses the modal entirely.

flRetireBoat / flUnretireBoat :32716

flRetireBoat(boatId,reason) :32716 is deliberately minimal — it is a flag plus a log line, nothing else:

b.retired=true; b.retiredDate=TODAY_STR; b.retiredReason=reason||'';
b.log.push({id:'sl'+Date.now(), s:'retired', from:TODAY_STR, to:null, loc:'', note:'ปลดระวางเรือ…'});
DoesDoes NOT do
Sets retired/retiredDate/retiredReasonNo confirm() — it fires immediately on call (unlike flUnretireBoat, which does confirm)
Pushes a status-log row with s:'retired', an enum value outside the documented available|fixing|unavailable setDoes not call autoClosePrevLog — a previously open log row stays open alongside the retired row
flSave() + renderBoats() + boat detail re-render, then an alert()Does not touch engines, gearboxes, propellers, assignments or open maintenance jobs on that boat
Retired boats disappear from the pickers that filter !b.retired (create-job, incident, move-spare, add-spare)Does not cancel or close existing MJs/INCs, and does not clear b.pier or the boat's seat/booking role

flUnretireBoat(boatId) :32727 confirms, clears retired, stamps unretiredDate and pushes an available log row. It does not remove the earlier retired row, so boat history keeps both.

Add/remove job assets · §costDoubleCount cost formula :32737

flMaintAddAsset(jobId,type) :32737 is the modal-form (as opposed to prompt) add path. Beyond pushing the asset row it mutates the real asset: engine → status='fixing' + log with current hours; gearbox → status='fixing' + log carrying both engineHours and computed usedHours = removeH − (installHours||baseHours); propeller → status='fixing'. flMaintRemoveAsset :32806 logs and splices but does not restore the asset's previous status.

§costDoubleCount (2026-07-30) :32818 — a long Thai post-mortem comment. Parts normally flow memo → receive into stock → withdraw to job, and the old formula added partsCost + memoCost, i.e. the same money twice. MJ-070 showed ฿49,434 + ฿53,703.94 = ฿103,138 against ฿53,703.94 actually paid; a full scan found 18 jobs and ฿271,041 of phantom spend.

function flMaintCalcCost(id){          // :32829
  memos = FL_MEMOS where maintId===id and status ∈ {paid,received,approved}
  paidNames = normalized item names from those memos with memoType==='parts'
  partsCost = Σ over m.parts of qty×cost, SKIPPING any part whose
              normalized name is in paidNames
  total = partsCost + memoCost;  m.cost = total   // keeps m.cost synced
}
  • _flCostNorm(s) :32828 lowercases and strips space , . - _ ( ) /.
  • Matching is exact on the normalized name, never substring — the comment states short names like "Key" would otherwise collide and over-deduct. 196/197 real rows matched cleanly.
  • flMaintPartsCoveredByMemo(m) :32847 is the mirror sum used only for the on-screen explainer.
TRAP Only memos with memoType==='parts' contribute to paidNames. A mixed memo containing parts lines will have its full amount counted and its withdrawn parts counted again — the known residual edge of this fix.

Withdrawing / returning parts against warehouse stock :32860

  • flMaintFillLoc(jobId) :32860 — after picking an item from the datalist, repopulates the warehouse dropdown from inv.stocks[] filtered to qty>0. The datalist maps label→id through the global window._maintInvLabelMap, rebuilt on every detail render.
  • flMaintAddPart(jobId) :32874 — hard blocks if no item, no warehouse, or qty > invQtyAt(inv,location); deducts with invRemoveAt; appends an inventory withdraw history row (§dupNoId: rows now carry an id); merges into an existing m.parts entry only when invId + location + date + late-flag all match; recalculates cost; then surgical refresh.
  • flMaintRemovePart(jobId,idx) :32908 — returns stock to the same warehouse it came from (p.location, falling back to primary/legacy location), writes a receive history row, recalculates cost, surgical refresh.
§mjLateParts When m.status==='done', both paths tag the action: the part gets late:true + lateBy, the inventory note and both logs get "ลงย้อนหลัง / แก้ย้อนหลังหลังปิดงาน". The retro flag is part of the merge key, so a retro withdrawal never merges into a same-day original line.

Creating memos from a job or project :32937

  • _memoProjectId :32937 and flProjAddMemo(projId) :32938 — project-level overhead memo; clears _memoMaintId and pre-selects type labor, scope vessel.
  • flMaintCreateMemo(id, blank) :32965 — job memo. First line is _memoEditId=null so a fresh create never inherits a prior edit session; also nulls _memoProjectId. With blank falsy it pre-fills _memoItems from m.parts with fromInventory:true, category:'parts'.
  • flDeleteMaint(id) :32993 — one confirm(), then filters the job out of FL_MAINT. No unwind: withdrawn parts are not returned to stock, linked incidents keep a dangling maintId, linked memos keep a dangling maintId.
  • flUpdateMaint(id,status) :33001 — legacy dispatcher (see the trap under the close flow).

flSaveCreateJob() :33137 is the re-entrant save:

Validate :33141
Boat and title required → alert and abort.
Existing open job on this boat :33144
Lists them and asks OK/Cancel before creating another.
Incident with ≥2 damaged assets :33155
If _flJobMode is unset → open the job-choice modal and return (the modal re-enters here). Whole branch is wrapped in try/catch that falls through to the single-job path on error.
split path :33177
One MJ per damaged asset, sequential numbers from a pre-computed max, inc.maintId = first job, inc.relatedMaintIds = all of them.
unavailable needs a reason :33274
Single-job path only — blocks the save if _jobBoatStatus==='unavailable' with an empty reason.
Create :33278
Writes boatStatus, boatStatusReason, and the legacy mirror setFixing = (fixing||unavailable). Optionally links parentProjectId from _projCreateForId and returns to the project view instead of the MJ view.
TRAP The split path builds jobs with setFixing read from a checkbox #job-fixing and never sets boatStatus/boatStatusReason, while the single path sets all three. Split-created jobs therefore fall back to the legacy interpretation everywhere m.boatStatus || (m.setFixing===false ? …) is evaluated.

Incident list view :33325

State: _incStFilter, _selIncId, _incArchiveTab :33325. flRenderIncident() :33327 paints header pills, a 5-cell KPI strip, an Active/Archive tab bar, sub-status filters and the list, then calls the detail renderer.

The important piece is the derived status — an incident has no independent lifecycle field except a resolved override:

effStatus(inc):
  inc.status==='resolved'            → 'resolved'   // Quick Fix / manual
  no linked MJ                       → 'open'
  mj.status==='pending'              → 'pending'
  mj.status==='inprogress'           → 'inprogress'
  otherwise                          → 'resolved'
  • Severity is derived at save time from priority, not stored independently by the user (critical|major|minor).
  • avgFixDays is computed from mj.endDate − inc.date, floored at 1 day.
  • Archive tab = effStatus==='resolved'; Active = everything else.
  • incFilterSt :33529, incSetArchive :33530 (also resets the sub-filter), flShowInc/flSelIncident :33536 (identical two-liners), flDeleteIncident :33538 — deleting warns that the linked job survives.
TRAP effStatus only looks at inc.maintId, i.e. the first job. For a split incident with relatedMaintIds, the incident shows resolved as soon as job #1 is done, even if the siblings are still open. The detail pane's job list and the close-time auto-close (which does check all linked MJs) are the accurate views.

Incident detail pane :33552

flRenderIncDetailPink() :33552 mounts into #fl-inc-detail-mount. Notable parts:

  • Warning banner :33660 has four shapes: green Quick-Fix confirmation, red "no job linked yet" + Create-now, blue multi-job list (split by engine unit), blue single-job link.
  • Damaged assets :33709 — gearbox/propeller rows get a ⇄ Swap spare button; engine and hull rows only get a DAMAGED chip. Already-swapped rows show ⇄ SWAPPED → serial.

Quick Swap — swapping a damaged gearbox/propeller for a spare :33840

flSwapCtx :33841 holds {incId,assetType,assetId}. flOpenSwapModal(incId,assetType,assetId) :33842 builds the candidate list under three documented rules:

  • A spare is identified by a truthy spareLocation, not by status==='spare' (legacy).
  • shop:* locations are excluded — at a repair shop means not available.
  • Business rule "แบบ A" (strict): only spares at boat:<thisBoat> or pier:<boat's pier>. Cross-pier spares are deliberately invisible; otherSpares is hardcoded to [] :33868 with the branch left in place.
  • Compatibility: gearbox matched on brand only (a model mismatch shows an amber "⚠ model ต่าง" warning but is still selectable); propeller matched on size.

flConfirmSwap(spareId) :33921 — one confirm(), then the full exchange:

Spare (going on)Damaged (coming off)
boatId, engineId/gearboxId, note, propPos inherited from the damaged partboatId=null, link nulled
status = 'ready' (gb) / 'active' (prop); spareLocation=nullstatus = 'fixing' (gb) / 'damaged' (prop)
gb: baseHours = engHours; prop: installHours = engHours — a fresh wear cyclespareLocation='shop:honda-phuket' hardcoded default, note='ส่งซ่อมจาก <boat>'
install log line with engine hoursremove log with usedHours computed since the last install entry, plus a transfer "ส่งซ่อม" line

The incident's matching damagedAssets entry gets swapped/swappedTo/swappedDate, and a Quick-Swap progress line is appended. Finally, if a gearbox was swapped and propellers were attached to it, the cascade modal opens and flShowInc is deferred until that resolves.

Propeller cascade after a gearbox swap :34022

flPropCascadeCtx :34027 = {incId,damagedGbId,spareGbId,propIds[],engHours}. flOpenPropCascadeModal(...) :34028 renders one card per orphaned propeller with three radio options (default keep):

ChoiceEffect in flConfirmPropCascade :34081
keep 🔄 move to the spare gearbox (recommended, pre-selected)p.gearboxId = spareGbId + transfer log. Nothing else changes — the propeller stays on the boat.
stock 📦 shelve at a pierboatId=null; gearboxId=null; status='spare'; spareLocation=<pier>, note rewritten, remove log. Pier list here is 4 entries — pier:tublamu|panwa|ranong|central — one more than FL_STASH_LOCS and than the move-spare modal.
repair 🔧 follow the gearbox to the shopboatId=null; gearboxId=null; status='fixing'; spareLocation = damagedGb.spareLocation (i.e. the shop the gearbox was just sent to), transfer log.

flCancelPropCascade() :34073 is a genuine no-op on the data: skipping leaves the propellers still attached to the removed gearbox.

INVARIANT RISK Cancel/skip is the one path that leaves a propeller pointing at a gearbox that is off the boat and in a shop — violating the "1 gearbox = 1 propeller, at the boat's drive position" model. The lookup at :34013 also consults an undocumented p._origGearboxId back-reference which nothing in this slice writes.

Moving and adding spare gearboxes / propellers :34155

flMoveCtx :34156; flOpenMoveSpare(assetType,assetId) :34157 renders three radio groups — pier warehouses (tublamu / panwa / central, note: no ranong here), on a boat (boat:<id>, "carried during trips", filtered to non-charter non-retired), and repair shops (shop:*) — plus a free-text note pre-filled from item.note.

flConfirmMove() :34219 — the commit:

Guards :34223
Missing item → close silently. No radio selected → alert('กรุณาเลือก location'). Same location as current → close and do nothing (no log, no note update — editing only the note is impossible through this modal).
Classify the move :34235
Default type:'transfer', "ย้าย: A → B". Into a shop = type:'repair', "ส่งซ่อม". Out of a shop = also type:'repair', "รับกลับจากซ่อม". Labels come from flParseSpareLoc() (defined outside this slice).
Log :34245
item.log.push({date,type,desc,fromLoc,toLoc}) — the only place in this slice that records both endpoints on the record.
Apply :34248
spareLocation = newLoc; note overwritten only if non-empty; status forced to 'fixing' for shop:* and 'spare' for everything else. Then flSave() and re-render only if flAssetTab==='overview'.
TRAP flConfirmMove never touches engineId/gearboxId/boatId. Moving a still-installed part through this modal marks it spare at a new location while it remains linked to its engine/gearbox — the UI only offers this button for spares, so nothing guards against calling it otherwise.

flOpenAddSpare(assetType) :34262 / flSaveAddSpare() :34285 register a brand-new spare. Ids are generated as 'g'+max(numeric suffix)+1 / 'p'+… — not LA_UID, unlike everything else in this slice — and initial status is fixing for a shop location, spare otherwise. Propeller size "15.5×17" is parsed into diameter/pitch; defaults (Honda, BF250D Gearbox, 3 blades, Stainless, ฿8,500) are hardcoded.

Incident modal, Quick Fix and save :34316

  • _editingIncId :34316 distinguishes edit from create in one shared modal.
  • flIncToggleQuickFix(on) :34319 — toggles the badge, the root-cause row, and rewrites the save button to "บันทึก + ปิดทันที" in green.
  • flOpenAddIncidentModal(incId, opts) :34336 — on edit, re-checks the damaged-asset checkboxes inside a requestAnimationFrame (the lists are rendered first). Root cause is parsed back out of the remark string with /Root cause:\s*([a-z_]+)/i — a string round-trip, not a stored field. Default priority: 5 normally, 2 for Quick Fix.
  • incSelPrio :34410; qfOpen(boatId) :34417 just opens the same modal with {boatId, quickFix:true}.
  • flSaveIncident() :34423 — collects checked assets + comma-split hull text; severity derived from priority (≥4 critical, ≥3 major, else minor). Edit mode updates in place and logs "✎ แก้ไขรายละเอียด"; create mode assigns INC-### by max+1, runs flAssertUniqueNo, and for Quick Fix sets status:'resolved', quickFix:true, resolvedDate and appends a quickfix log entry to each damaged asset record.
TRAP Edit mode does not re-write quickFix, status or the "Root cause:" prefix in the remark; toggling Quick Fix while editing an existing incident changes the button label but not the saved record.

Inventory + Memo view state, grouping and density :34532

One long let line :34533 holds the whole view state: _invCat, _invLowOnly, _memoStFilter, _memoArchiveTab, _invLoc, _invSearch, _invTab, _selInvId, _memoSearch, _memoDensity, _memoView, plus _invSelected (a Set) :34535 and _invGroupCollapsed :34537.

Every one of these setters is a one-liner that mutates state and calls flRenderInventory() — a full re-render of the whole tab:

FnLineEffect
invToggleGroup(key):34538Collapse/expand one engine-model group
invGetGroup(item):34543Derives the group: explicit note match (DF325A/DF250W/BF250D/BF250A) wins, else supplier inference (honda→BF250D, suzuki→DF325A), else 'ทั่วไป'
invToggleSelect / invSelectAllVisible / invClearSelection:34559–:34571Bulk-memo multi-select. Select-all is a toggle over the currently visible ids
invSwitchTab / invLocFilter / invCatFilter / invToggleLow:34573–:34576Stock-tab filters
memoFilterSt / memoSetArchive:34577–:34578Memo status filter; switching tabs resets the sub-filter (Archive only has paid)

memoSetDensity(v):34592Sets _memoDensity to 'comfortable' ("ละเอียด") or 'compact' ("กระชับ")
memoSetView(v):34593'list' or 'board' (kanban); also resets the status filter
_memoLastDate(m):34594Max of created/approved/ordered/received/paid dates — the sort key and month-grouping key for the memo list

DENSITY MODEL _memoDensity is consumed in three places inside the memo table: the column spec _MCOL :34861 drops the "ผู้ขอ" (requester) column in compact mode; the row's item-name preview line is hidden; and the per-row progress bar inside the status cell is hidden. The density toggle is only rendered when _memoView==='list' :34851 — the board view has no density.

flRenderInventory — the 440-line Inventory/Memo page :34612

Mounts into #fl-inv-pink-wrap. Top-level structure: category pills header → KPI strip → Stock/Memorandum tab toggle → buildStockTab() or buildMemoTab(). Two nested helpers, buildItemDetail(item) and buildMemoTab(), are declared inside the function and therefore invisible to anything else.

Stock tab :34658

Filters by category, warehouse, low-stock and free text. Warehouse matching uses _invWhMatch :34663 which scans every stocks[] entry, so an item stocked in both warehouses appears under both tabs.

Grouped table :34699

Rows are grouped by invGetGroup in fixed groupOrder (Honda BF250D / BF250A / Suzuki DF250W / DF325A / ทั่วไป), each with a collapsible colour-coded header row, count badge and per-unit cost sum. Inside a group, rows sort by qty desc.

Dead code :34677

tableRows is a fully built flat row list that is never used — the grouped buildRow :34710 superseded it. Editing the wrong one is an easy mistake; the live one is buildRow.

Location cell

Three states: multi-warehouse breakdown "Tub Lamu (3) · Panwa (1)", "no stock" when all entries are zero, and "registered · no stock" when the item has neither location nor stocks.

Bulk float bar :34765

Sticky bottom bar appears when _invSelected.size>0, showing count and an estimate at qty=1, with a "+ สร้าง Memo จาก N รายการ" action.

Item detail :34779

Sticky right panel: qty / minQty / cost tiles plus a movement history timeline that renders edit entries as a field list and receive/withdraw entries as +n/−n.

Memo tab buildMemoTab() :34808. Active = created, pending_approval, approved, ordered, received; Archive = paid only. Sorted by _memoLastDate desc, with month divider rows carrying a per-month count and total.

§memoTable Three tagged decisions live here: (1) :34860 the column spec and the compact-mode column drop; (2) :34865 Thai Baht has no third decimal but raw toLocaleString() leaked qty×price fractions, so _mAmt pins 2 decimals for display only — stored values are untouched; (3) :34967 one memo = one <tr>, with the item breakdown as a hidden sibling <tr data-memo-items> toggled by an inline JS string (_openJs :34992) that walks nextElementSibling. Header cells are position:sticky :35041.

The approval stepper :34913 is where the memo workflow shape is declared:

parts / mixed : created → pending_approval → approved → ordered → received → paid
labor-only    : created → pending_approval → approved → paid

isLaborOnly = memoType==='labor', or a mixed memo with no item whose category is parts. The board view :35013 is only used when _memoView==='board' && _memoArchiveTab==='active', with four columns (Pending merges created+pending_approval).

Receiving stock and editing an inventory item :35053

  • flOpenReceiveStock() :35053 / flOpenReceiveOne(id) :35092 — the same modal #fl-modal-receive serves three modes (pick-an-item, fixed item, whole memo). Both wire an onchange warning when the chosen warehouse differs from the item's recorded location.
  • §memoRecvWh at :35105 — because the memo mode recolours the warning box to a neutral explainer tone, the per-item mode must restore the amber colours or its real warning renders invisible grey.
  • flOpenInvEdit(id) :35123 — pre-fills the edit modal and renders the last 10 audit entries. The engine model is split out of note by regex (^(BF250D|BF250A|DF250W|DF325A)) into a dropdown and recombined on save as model · rest; that combined note is what invGetGroup later reads.
  • flSaveInvEdit() :35167 — diffs old vs new as strings, aborts early with no write if nothing changed, confirms if partNo changed (may break memo history links), then applies and appends an {type:'edit', changes:[{field,from,to}]} history entry. Note it stamps new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10) here rather than TODAY_STR — a UTC-shift inconsistency with the rest of the file.
  • flSaveReceive() :35234 — two branches. Memo branch: skips labor items, matches inventory by invId then by exact/substring name, invAddAt at the chosen warehouse, and auto-creates a new inventory item (category 'อื่นๆ') for unmatched lines, back-writing it.invId; sets currentStep=4, status='received'. Single branch: validates qty>0 and appends a receive history row.

Warehouse transfer :35322

flOpenTransfer(id) :35324 populates "From" only with locations that hold stock, defaults "To" to a different WAREHOUSE_LOCATIONS entry; transferUpdateMax() :35357 keeps the qty max and the "คงเหลือ" hint in sync. flSaveTransfer() :35368 blocks on missing from/to, identical from/to, qty ≤ 0, or qty > invQtyAt, then delegates the actual stock move to invTransfer(...) (defined outside this slice).

Pier assignments (temporary / permanent boat relocation) :35395

  • flOpenNewAssignment(boatId) :35397 — defaults From = current pier, To = a different one from ['tublamu','panwa','ranong'], type temporary, 7-day window.
  • flSaveAssignment() :35420 — validates from≠to and end≥start; derives status planned/active/completed from the dates; pushes onto b.assignments[]; a permanent + active assignment rewrites b.pier.
  • flCancelAssignment(boatId,asnId) :35481 — sets status='cancelled'; flAutoUpdateAssignments() :35505 re-derives planned→active→completed for every non-cancelled assignment.
ARCHITECTURE VIOLATION All three functions bypass flSave() and instead do JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(LS_KEY)) → mutate → localStorage.setItem(LS_KEY, …) :35462, :35489, :35521. Per CLAUDE.md the state blob must never be written to real localStorage (it exceeds the quota and Safari throws QuotaExceededError); this works only because _mem shims that key. It is also the only writer of b.assignments outside the normal persist path, so those rows can miss the SQL mapper round-trip. Treat as legacy — do not copy this pattern.

EBS = Edit Boat Status during a job :35532

"EBS" is the prefix for the Edit-Boat-Status modal (#fl-modal-edit-boat-status), reached from the "Change" button on a job's Boat Status during Job card. It changes the boat's availability while a maintenance job is open, without starting or closing the job. State: _ebsJobId, _ebsNewStatus :35533.

  • flOpenEditBoatStatus(jobId) :35535 — shows the current status (same boatStatus || setFixing fallback), clears every .ebs-st-opt, resets _ebsNewStatus=null, hides the reason row, dates to today.
  • ebsSelectStatus(st, el) :35558 — the picker. Structurally identical to selectJobStatus :33081 (same three colour pairs, same "reason row only for unavailable" rule) but writes _ebsNewStatus and targets .ebs-st-opt. Two near-duplicate pickers — change one and you must change the other.
  • flSaveEditBoatStatus() :35572 — blocks on: no job, no status chosen, status identical to current, missing effective date, or unavailable with no reason. Then (1) updates m.boatStatus/boatStatusReason/setFixing; (2) closes every open boat log row whose note contains this job's number by stamping l.to = effDate, then pushes a new row — including an explicit available row so the change is visible in history; (3) appends a progress-log line; then saves and re-renders detail, list and boats.
TRAP The log-closing match is a substring test on the free-text note (l.note.includes(m.no)). MJ-01 would match MJ-010; note text is written by several different code paths, so keep the "Maintenance Job <no>" convention when you add new boat-log writers.

Legacy item-history panel, add stock, memo-from-inventory :35645

  • flShowItemHistory(id) :35645 — an older side-panel implementation (#inv-hist-panel, #inv-grid-wrap, #fl-inv-list) that duplicates buildItemDetail. It uses Thai month abbreviations and Buddhist-era years, and highlights the source row by innerHTML.includes(...) string matching. Probably orphaned by the newer panel (inferred — no caller is visible in this slice).
  • flOpenAddStockModal() :35693 / flSaveAddStock() :35708 — new item; supplier autocomplete is built from the distinct existing suppliers to keep spelling consistent; note is again engineModel · extraNote.
  • flOpenMemoFromInventory() :35730, flOpenMemoFromSelected() :35752 (bulk — builds items at qty 1, auto-picks the majority supplier among the selection), flOrderItem(id) :35824 (single low-stock reorder).
  • flShowToast(msg,type) :35817 — the toast used by ~20 functions in this slice; creates a fixed-position div, fades after 3 s. type==='error' switches to red.

Memo workflow engine :35841

flAdvanceMemo(id) :35841 drives m.currentStep / m.status along the sequence, choosing the labor-only sequence when applicable. Two steps intercept instead of advancing:

→ approved :35853
Opens flOpenApproveModal :35869; flSaveApprove :35876 requires an approver name, then writes currentStep=2, status='approved', approvedBy, approvedDate, approveNote.
→ received :35858
Only for memos that actually contain parts items → flOpenReceiveMemo. A labor-only or parts-free memo skips straight to paid :35917.
otherwise :35862
Advance step + status, save, re-render, toast.
FIELD-NAME TRAP flSaveApprove writes m.approveNote :35883 while the renderers read m.approvedNote (with approveNote only as a fallback in the tooltip at :34981). The approval note therefore does not show in the memo detail row's green approval banner :34958.

flMemoWarehouse(mo) :35889 (§memoRecvWh) guesses the receiving warehouse from the memo's boat's current pier via getBoatCurrentPier, returning {wh, why}; the fallback is คลัง Tub Lamu with an explanation. flOpenReceiveMemo(moId) :35898 then must clear the onchange handler that flOpenReceiveOne left on the location select :35905 and re-set the value every time, because the shared modal retains state between openings. Quantities are rendered read-only ("จำนวนตาม Memo ที่อนุมัติแล้ว · แก้ไขไม่ได้") behind hidden #recv-qty-i inputs.

Engine / Gearbox / Propeller CRUD modals :35955

Status enums, declared as plain arrays whose index is the picker position:

const flStValues     = ['ready','fixing','broken','spare'];   // :35955  engine, gearbox
const flPropStValues = ['active','fixing','broken','spare'];  // :35956  propeller
let flSelSts = {eng:0, gb:0, prop:0};                          // :35957  selected index

flPickSt(type,idx) :35960 repaints the 4 tiles from a fixed colour array and toggles two rows: the location row shows for Spare (idx 3) and, for engines only, also for Fixing (idx 1) — because a repairing engine needs "where it was sent".

ModalOpenSaveLink invariant enforced on save
Engine:35976 (flOpenEditEngModal :35990 is an alias):35994spareLocation kept only when status is spare or fixing. Legacy pos/location values not in the dropdown get an option injected so they aren't silently lost.
Gearbox:36020:36068Engine picker offers only engines that are mounted on a boat and have no other gearbox (1 engine = 1 gearbox). status==='spare' ⇒ engineId=null. spareLocation: from dropdown if spare, null if attached, otherwise preserve the existing value (orphan case).
Propeller:36110:36149Gearbox picker offers only gearboxes installed on an engine and not already carrying a propeller (1 gearbox = 1 propeller). status==='spare' ⇒ gearboxId=null (asserted twice, :36166 and :36182). size is recomposed as diameter×pitch.

flOpenAddPropModal(id) :36110 specifically: doubles as add and edit (flEditPropId), pre-fills 12 fields, re-injects a "(legacy)" option when the stored spareLocation isn't in the dropdown, filters the gearbox select as above, and sets the status tile from flPropStValues.indexOf(p.status).

TRAP flPickSt('prop', flPropStValues.indexOf(...)) returns -1 for an unknown/absent status (e.g. the 'damaged' value that flConfirmSwap writes to a swapped-out propeller :33996). flPickSt then highlights nothing and a subsequent save reads flPropStValues[-1] === undefined, wiping the status. 'damaged' and 'limited' (written by the limited close outcome) are both outside these enums.

Save handlers differ in what they refresh: engine save preserves the list scroll explicitly (:36012); gearbox and propeller save prefer flRenderAsset() so the current tab (Overview / Gearboxes / …) refreshes, falling back to the per-list renderer.

The end of the block — what actually runs last :36191

The final statements of this <script> are a four-line boot section, not a function definition:

// ── init Fleet ──
flLoad();                                              // :36192
// Layer 4: data-integrity check after load · console warnings
if(typeof flValidateDataIntegrity === 'function')
    flValidateDataIntegrity();                         // :36194
flDrWeekStart = addDays(TODAY, -TODAY.getDay()+1);     // :36195
</script>                                              // :36196
RESOLVING THE DOC CONFLICT Both claims are partly right. flLoad is defined far earlier, around :13815; what sits at the end of the block is the top-level call flLoad(); :36192. So "the block ends with flLoad()" is accurate as a description of the last executed statements — but the very last line before </script> is actually the flDrWeekStart assignment (Monday of the current week, via addDays), preceded by the guarded flValidateDataIntegrity() call. The last function definition in the block is flSavePropeller :36149.

Immediately after </script> the file continues with modal markup — the first is #fl-modal-assign-eng :36198 — so every modal id referenced above (fl-modal-eng-choice, fl-modal-start-gear, fl-modal-stash, fl-modal-maint-close, fl-modal-create-job, fl-modal-incident, fl-modal-swap, fl-modal-prop-cascade, fl-modal-move, fl-modal-add-spare, fl-modal-receive, fl-modal-transfer, fl-modal-assignment, fl-modal-edit-boat-status, fl-modal-memo, fl-modal-approve, fl-modal-engine, fl-modal-gearbox, fl-modal-propeller, fl-modal-inv-edit, fl-modal-add-inv) lives in the HTML that follows this slice, not in it.

CROSS-CUTTING RULES FOR ANY EDIT HERE
  • Numbering: MJ-### / INC-### always max(parsed)+1, always followed by flAssertUniqueNo. Never length+1.
  • Dates: use TODAY_STR and _flDayAfter; the two toISOString().slice(0,10) uses in this slice (:35222, :35413) are UTC-shift bugs waiting to bite at +07:00.
  • Any new mutation on a job/asset should append to both the record's log/history and m.progressLog, and call flPushLog if an incident may be linked — that mirroring is what makes the merged incident timeline complete.
  • Re-render surgically (flMaintRefreshParts) or wrap in _flWithDetailScroll; a bare full re-render scroll-jumps.
  • Asset link invariants: 1 engine = 1 gearbox = 1 propeller; a spare part must be detached. Both save handlers and both stash/cascade paths enforce this — new paths must too.
